# CCS reservation system: project guide for Claude Code

This file is the standing brief for the **College Club Seattle (CCS) reservation and member system**. It replaces the Oct 20 CLAUDE.md, which was never committed to the repo. This one was rebuilt on Oct 22, 2026 from the Phase 1 kickoff prompt. The system is a **standalone product**: it is separate from the member usage dashboard that shares this repo (see "The dashboard next door").

Read `AGENTS.md` too: this repo runs Next.js 16, which differs from older versions. Read the relevant guide in `node_modules/next/dist/docs/` before writing Next.js code.

## Who you're working for and how to talk to them

Grant Carlson is a board member and House Committee chair at College Club Seattle, a member-owned rowing and social club on Lake Union. Grant is not a software engineer.

- Explain in plain English, with comparisons to running an apartment building (units, leases, tenants, a front desk, a rent ledger).
- Ask questions as multiple choice, recommended option first, with room for Grant's own answer.
- Skip comprehension checks. Explain, then move on.
- Never say something is finished until lint, type check, build and every test pass. Show the results.

Other people: Caitlin Sogn and Julia (staff) will edit this system with Claude Code themselves. Write code and settings so they can.

## Decisions made on Oct 22, 2026

- **Build path: full replacement.** This app, on Vercel, will become both the CCS public website and its reservation and events system, replacing Wix and iCrew. (Open question 2: answered.)
- **Build on what exists.** Repo `grantaltom-ms/college-club`, Vercel project `college-club` (college-club-gamma.vercel.app). No new repo, Vercel project or Supabase project.
- **Standalone.** The reservation system shares nothing with the dashboard except the repo, the Vercel project and the brand stylesheet. No shared code, tables or data.
- **Built in isolation.** Phase 1 uses fake data only. Nothing goes live to members. Wix, iCrew, Mailchimp, QuickBooks and Brivo are not touched.
- **The household is the unit of membership and billing.** One QuickBooks record is one household. Each adult gets their own login, all linked to the household. Ending a household removes every login in it at once. Juniors and staff are not members in this system. (Open question 8, juniors: answered, out of scope.)
- **QuickBooks:** CSV files only, no live connection. Changed Oct 24 (Grant, step 9): one **invoice** per household per month with a **separate line for each kind of charge** (dues, event tickets, visitor fees, bar, snack bar), instead of one charge line. Nothing goes to QuickBooks by itself: staff download the files and import them there. See "Step 9".
- **Accounts and domain** are owned by a CCS-owned email shared by a few people, not by one person. Never hard-code keys or passwords. Use environment variables and update `.env.example`.
- **Rollout:** a small pilot group first, then a single cutover date for everyone. Boat reservations move all at once, so never design for iCrew and this system both taking boat bookings.
- **Staff edit with Claude Code.** Keep booking rules and billing logic separate from page content. Every change goes through a preview plus automated checks. Rule settings are editable as data, not code.
- **Git:** branch, pull request, merge, every time. Never commit straight to `main`. Never merge a pull request without Grant's approval.

Answers Grant gave to the Phase 1 plan (Oct 22, 2026):

- Dashboard page files move into a `(dashboard)` route-group folder. Every dashboard URL stays exactly the same. Grant approved this move.
- `/club` is visible locally, in CI and on Vercel preview deployments. It returns "page not found" on the production site.
- Crew on a multi-seat boat do **not** count toward their own limits, but a crew member can't be in two boats at the same time.
- Taking a boat out of service keeps existing bookings. Staff get a list of affected members. Nothing is cancelled automatically.
- Members see only "Booked" on a taken slot, and "Yours" on their own bookings. Staff see names.
- "7 days ahead" means today plus the next 7 calendar days, counted on the Pacific clock.
- Grant turns on GitHub branch protection (pull request plus passing checks required before merge). Steps are in the first pull request.

## Build order (10 steps)

1. Household and member structure, with fake test households
2. Bookable equipment and spaces, with reservation rules (boats first)
3. Fitness classes with capacity and waitlists
4. Events and RSVPs, including links from Mailchimp emails straight to an RSVP
5. Staff admin screen
6. Real logins (one per adult, tied to the household)
7. Importing real members from CCS records
8. Public website pages in the CCS look
9. QuickBooks CSV export
10. iCrew history import and cutover tools

## The dashboard next door (do not touch)

The repo also holds the CCS member usage dashboard. The board uses it. Its pages are open to anyone with the link (no login, marked noindex), and member names are stripped on the server before anything reaches the browser. It reads the **live** Supabase project `hgjcnuwxhemynnvsnmou`, which holds real member data (`members`, `qb_members`, `club_totals`, `shell_outings`, `reservation_counts`, `roadmap`).

- Don't change any dashboard page or URL: `/`, `/visits`, `/timing`, `/shells`, `/reservations`, `/email`, `/goals`, and `/financials` and `/membership` (added to the dashboard by Grant's own pull request 31 on Oct 27; they live in `src/app/(dashboard)/` like the rest, and the navigation bar in `src/components/Nav.tsx` is the dashboard's). Board members have these links.
- **Financials has a password** (Grant, Oct 27): one shared board password in `DASHBOARD_FINANCIALS_PASSWORD` (private settings only, at least 8 characters). The right password gives that browser a 30-day pass (a cookie scripts cannot read, signed with the password itself, so changing the password locks everyone out at once); "Lock Financials on this device" throws it away. `/api/financials` refuses a browser without a pass, so the figures never leave the server without the password. With no password set the page is locked for everyone and says how to set it up. Logic: `src/lib/financials-lock.ts`; handlers `/api/financials/unlock` and `/lock`. Every other dashboard page still opens without a password.
- Don't change or reuse its tables. `reservation_counts` is dashboard data, not bookings.
- The dashboard tests must keep passing untouched.
- Never connect reservation-system work to the live Supabase project.
- Its page files live in `src/app/(dashboard)/`. The folder name is invisible in URLs.

Inside `src/club/`: `auth/` holds signing in (email links, sessions, the staff list, the test outbox), `engine/` holds the rules (the lease terms; every change needs tests), `store/` holds where data is kept (`memory.ts` for the in-memory sample, `postgres.ts` for Postgres; both fit one `ClubStore` interface, so the rules never know which), `sample/` holds the invented households, `identity.ts` is the one "who is the current member" function, `enabled.ts` decides whether `/club` exists at all.

The new tables are an apartment building: household = the lease, member = a person on the lease, household history = the tenant file, qualification = "certified to use the rooftop grill", bookable item = a unit, rule set = the house rules.

## Safety rules for Phase 1

- **`/club` only exists when sample data is allowed** (`src/club/enabled.ts`): in development, or with `CCS_ALLOW_SAMPLE_DATA=1`, and never when `VERCEL_ENV=production`. Otherwise it answers "page not found".
- **The public website under `/site` is hidden the same way** (`siteEnabled` in `src/site/enabled.ts`, which follows `sampleDataAllowed`): visible while developing, in CI and on previews, "page not found" on production, including the page titles, so nothing about it leaks. Switching it on for the public at cutover (question D) changes `siteEnabled` only.
- **The "act as this test member" picker** is a test shortcut that exists only where invented data is allowed (`pickerEnabled` in `enabled.ts`, which follows `sampleDataAllowed`, not `clubEnabled`). Real people sign in by email link (step 6). When the member area is one day switched on for real members, the picker and the test outbox stay off because they hang off the sample-data switch, so that change is made in `clubEnabled` only. It is tested absent in a production setting.
- **Every permission check goes through `getCurrentActor()`** in `src/club/identity.ts`. Step 6 replaced what is inside that function (a real sign-in first, the test picker second) and nothing else: no page or handler changed how it asks.
- **Sign-in rules.** A sign-in link is made only for an address that can sign in, and the page never says whether one is; it works once, for a short time, and looking at it records nothing. The browser holds a long random secret that the database knows only as a fingerprint. Whether someone is still signed in is decided on every visit, so ending a household or removing an adult signs them out at once. The staff list (`CLUB_STAFF_LOGINS`) holds real addresses, so it lives only in private settings. `CLUB_SITE_URL` must be set wherever real email is sent, so a link can never be pointed at someone else's server.
- **Secrets stay in environment variables.** `CLUB_LINK_SECRET` (personal RSVP links) and `CLUB_IPAD_SECRET` (the front-desk iPad's private address, and with a different label the honor snack bar iPad's) are both server-only, at least 16 random characters, never in code or in an address. Where the member area is switched off neither exists. Changing one switches off every address made from it. The real email sender's key (`RESEND_API_KEY`) is the same kind of secret: private settings only, never in code, a message, a log line or an error.
- **Never use `SUPABASE_URL` or the service key for club work.** Club code uses its own `CLUB_DATABASE_URL` (local or test databases only). The Postgres store (`createClubPool`) and the tests both refuse any database address that looks like Supabase. With `CLUB_DATABASE_URL` unset the member area runs on the in-memory sample.
- **The public Request a tour form is the one place a stranger can write to the club's records** (Grant, Oct 29: a real form). It saves a name, an email, an optional phone number and an optional note, and nothing else, in `club.tour_requests` (never removed, changed only by marking one "followed up" once; the database refuses anything else). It lives under `/site`, so it is "page not found" on the live site until cutover, like every `/site` page; its server action checks `siteEnabled()` before it reads the form. A hidden box catches robots (they are thanked and nothing is saved), one address is saved at most 3 times in 24 hours and the whole site 30 in an hour (placeholders in `tour-rules.json`), every field has a length cap, and a failed notice email never loses a request. Tests use invented names only.
- **What a public page may never show** (Grant, Oct 29): the bar, Happy Hour or drinks; access codes; staff phone numbers; iCrew links. The only phone number on a public page is the main line, (206) 622-0624. Pages under `/site` and the new homepage follow this. The current `/site` words do not yet: see "Public site refresh".
- Fake data only. Invented names only. Never real member data. If Grant uploads a file that has real names in it (the iCrew report), read only its layout and counts: print no name, copy nothing into the project, and build and test with invented files.
- Stop and ask before: spending money (including Supabase branches and paid Vercel features); deleting any file, table or data; connecting to the live Supabase project or applying a migration to it; touching Wix, iCrew, Mailchimp, QuickBooks, Brivo or real member data; changing any dashboard page, table or URL; merging a pull request or changing Vercel production; changing any decision in this file.
- Vercel runs Node 24. CI runs Node 24 as of this change, so tests match what goes live.

## What to keep from iCrew, and where to do better

Keep: members see what's free and book a boat or space themselves; booking windows and limits that stop one person hogging equipment; boats tied to skill levels; staff can take a boat out of service; multi-seat boats are booked as one outing.

Do better:
- One CCS-branded login and site, not a separate iCrew-branded app.
- Built for the whole club (rowing, fitness, social, spaces), not just a boathouse.
- Track whether a booking was actually used. iCrew only counts reservations.
- Keep a real record of when a household joins and leaves. iCrew has none.
- Rules are settings staff can change, not things a vendor configures.
- Every member and household gets a permanent internal ID from day one, with empty slots for QuickBooks, Brivo and Mailchimp IDs. (iCrew and Brivo share no ID today and match by name only.)

## Step 1: households and members (fake data only)

- **Household:** permanent ID, display name ("The Lindqvist household"), membership type (Resident = 33 and over, Intermediate = under 33, Non-Resident = lives farther away and visits rarely), status (active, or ended with an end date), join date, empty slots for QuickBooks ID and notes.
- **Member (one adult):** permanent ID, household, first and last name, email, phone, role (primary or additional adult), status, empty slots for Brivo credential ID, Mailchimp contact ID and future login ID.
- **Qualifications:** a member can hold rowing qualifications (sculling levels). Each records a level, who granted it, and when. Staff grant them; members can't give themselves one.
- **History:** a log per household of every change that matters (joined, member added or removed, type changed, membership ended), with date, what changed, and who changed it. The log can only be added to, never edited.
- **Ending a membership:** every member in the household is blocked from new bookings at once, and all future bookings are cancelled and logged. Past bookings stay as history.
- **Fake data:** about 14 invented households covering a single adult, couples, a three-adult household, each membership type, one ended household, one that joined this month, members with and without rowing qualifications, and one member whose email is shared with a partner. Invented names only; boat names come from `src/lib/shells.ts` when step 2 needs them.
- **Test member picker** and **one "current member" function**, as described above.

## Step 2: bookable equipment and spaces, with rules (built Oct 22; in review)

One engine for everything bookable: Shell, Kayak, Paddle Board, Dock Space, Gym Space, Room Space, Work Space. Fully set up in the fake data: shells, kayaks, paddle boards, plus Dock Space to prove the engine is general.

Each item: name, type, seat count for boats (1 single, 2 double, 4 quad or four), qualification needed (if any), in-service or out-of-service status with reason and expected return date, and the rule set that applies. Rules attach to a type of item (for example "all singles") and individual items can override them.

Every number below is a **placeholder** until staff confirm real values (open question 5). Mark them as placeholders in the settings and on screen during testing. **Superseded Oct 30 (question CK):** the booking numbers in this table were replaced by iCrew's real settings; see "Booking numbers from iCrew".

| Rule | Placeholder value |
|---|---|
| How far ahead a member can book | 7 days |
| When new days open for booking | Midnight Pacific, a rolling window |
| Bookable hours | 5 AM to 8 PM (the club's member hours) |
| Time slots | 30-minute steps |
| Shortest and longest booking | 30 minutes and 2 hours |
| Future bookings one member can hold at once, per type | 2 |
| Bookings one member can have per day, per type | 1 |
| Latest time to cancel without it counting as a no-show | 2 hours before start |
| Gap between bookings of the same boat | 0 minutes (setting exists, off) |
| Qualification needed | Per boat class |

Behavior:
- **No double booking, ever.** Two requests for the same boat at overlapping times at the same moment produce exactly one booking. Enforced in the database (an overlap constraint), not only in the page.
- **Multi-seat boats:** the person booking owns the booking and adds other active members as crew. Each crew member must also be qualified. Crew don't count toward their own limits, but can't be in two boats at once.
- **Clear refusals** in plain language ("You can book up to 7 days ahead. Saturday opens Thursday at midnight."), never a bare error.
- **Cancelling:** members cancel their own bookings. Late cancellations are recorded as late.
- **Out of service:** blocks new bookings. Existing overlapping bookings stay; staff get a list of affected members.
- **Attendance:** every booking ends as upcoming, completed, cancelled, late-cancelled or no-show. Built in step 5: staff check people in or mark no-shows, and a booking nobody checked in for becomes a no-show by itself when it ends (see Step 5).
- **Staff override:** staff can book past the rules (for a lesson). Each override is logged with a reason. A test staff identity in the picker is enough for now.
- **Time zone:** store exact moments, show Pacific time. Clocks fall back on Nov 1, 2026; no lost or doubled slots that day.
- **Screens:** a day view by item (filter by type; members see "Booked" or "Yours"), a booking form that shows the rules and why anything is refused, and "My bookings" with cancel. Plain and functional, CCS fonts and colors, works on a phone.

### Step 2 as built

- **Rules are data.** `src/club/settings/booking-rules.json` holds every number (all marked placeholder) and the item classes (single, double, quad, kayak, paddle board, dock space). Rules layer: defaults, then the class ("all singles"), then the single item. `parseRuleBook` refuses typos and contradictions in words ("maxMinutes (100) must be a whole number of 30-minute slots"), so staff edits are caught by the tests. The database has matching tables (`booking_rule_defaults`, `item_classes`, `items`) so the staff screen in step 5 can edit the same data.
- **The engine** is `src/club/engine/bookings.ts`. `checkBooking` returns every reason a booking is refused, in a fixed order, in plain language, and marks which ones staff may set aside. `createBooking`, `cancelBooking`, `markAttendance`, `takeOutOfService`, `returnToService` do the work, each as one all-or-nothing change.
- **Facts versus rules.** Staff can book past the *rules* (window, hours, step, length, limits, qualification, gap) with a recorded reason, and the record lists exactly which rules were set aside. Staff can never set aside a *fact*: the slot is taken, the item is out of service, the time has passed, the member has left, nobody in two boats at once.
- **Time.** Every time is a real moment; days, hours and "midnight" are Pacific. Slots are stepped in real elapsed time, so Nov 1, 2026 (25 hours) has no lost or doubled slot and Mar 14, 2027 (23 hours) is handled. Late-cancel cutoffs count real hours.
- **No double booking, two layers.** The engine checks first and explains. The database (`club.bookings`, migration `20261023000000`) also refuses an overlap by itself with an exclusion constraint, and lines up competing requests for one item with a lock (without it, colliding requests stalled for about a second on a deadlock instead of getting a clean "taken").
- **Household link.** Ending a membership or removing an adult cancels their future bookings (not late; staff-cancelled), takes them off other people's boats as crew (the booking stays), leaves bookings under way and past ones alone, and writes it to the household's file. All in the same all-or-nothing change.
- **Screens.** `/club/book` (day view, filter by type, days in the window), `/club/book/new` (form that runs the real rule check and shows the rules), `/club/my-bookings` (staff see "All bookings"), `/club/equipment` (staff only: take out of service, return, see affected members).
- **The test clock.** `CCS_CLUB_NOW` (for example `2026-10-28T20:00:00Z`) freezes "now" for the member area, in development and tests only. It does nothing where the member area is switched off.

### The Postgres store (built Oct 22, after step 2)

- **What it is.** `PgClubStore` (`src/club/store/postgres.ts`) keeps the same `ClubStore` interface as the in-memory copy, so the rules engine did not change. With `CLUB_DATABASE_URL` set, `/club` reads and writes Postgres (a local or throwaway one only; hosted Supabase addresses are refused). With `CCS_ALLOW_SAMPLE_DATA=1` too, the invented club is loaded the first time, only if the database has no households.
- **How a change works.** Take one database-wide "writers" lock (shared by every app server), load the current records, let the engine change a working copy, write back exactly what changed, all in one transaction. Writers therefore take turns and always start from the truth. That is what makes a member's limits and "nobody in two boats" race-free (open question G, now answered), with the database's overlap rule still underneath as a second line of defence. Readers take a consistent snapshot and never wait for writers.
- **Rules and the database.** `booking-rules.json` is still the source of the rules; `syncRules` mirrors it into the rule tables on start-up so items can point at their classes. In step 5 the staff screen edits those tables instead.
- **What it loads.** All households, members, history, items and out-of-service entries, and bookings that ended in the last 90 days (`historyDays`). Older bookings stay in the database; the screens' "Past" list shows the last 90 days until a full-history view exists.
- **Never deletes.** The store refuses to delete anything and refuses to rewrite a history line. Bookings are cancelled by status, as in real life.
- **Proof.** The household, booking, household-plus-booking and daylight-saving tests run on both stores (the Postgres run needs `CLUB_TEST_DATABASE_URL`; CI always sets it). Extra tests check that Postgres holds exactly the same sample club as memory, that two app servers sharing one database cannot beat a limit, a slot or a "two boats" rule by racing, that the database overlap rule is translated into a plain refusal, and that the booking browser tests pass on a Postgres-backed server.

Not built yet, on purpose when this was written: the attendance-marking screen for boat outings and the staff admin screen (both now built in step 5), emails or texts.

## Step 3: fitness classes with chairs and a waiting list (built Oct 22; in review)

Grant's answers (Oct 22): the waiting list moves up **automatically and instantly**, first in line first; nobody can be **in two places at once** (two classes, or a class and their own boat, as booker or crew); a class **holds a gym or room space** (members cannot book that space while it runs); **no money** in step 3 (classes are included in membership; fees wait for the QuickBooks step). Assumed unless Grant objects: only staff create and change classes; members see just "full" and their own waiting-list place, never other names; classes have their own section next to Book rather than being folded into My bookings.

The building comparison: a class session is a scheduled use of the clubhouse room. The chairs are its unit count; the waiting list is the applicant queue; a sign-up is a lease on one chair for one evening.

- **Pieces.** A *class* ("Morning Yoga": usual chairs, length, room, instructor, optional own rules). A *session* is one dated occurrence with its own number of chairs, and it holds its room. A *sign-up* is one name on one session's sheet: `enrolled` (has a chair), `waitlisted`, or how it ended: `cancelled`, `late_cancelled`, `attended`, `no_show`.
- **Rules are data.** `src/club/settings/class-rules.json` (placeholders, like the boat rules): sign-up window (14 days, opens at midnight Pacific), closes 30 minutes before, leave without penalty until 2 hours before, the automatic move-up runs until 2 hours before, waiting list up to 10, 5 upcoming places at once (a waiting spot counts), 1 class a day. A class can override any of them under its own `rules`. A typo is refused in words by `parseClassRules`.
- **The engine** is `src/club/engine/classes.ts`: `checkSignUp` (every reason in plain words, read only), `signUp`, `leaveClass`, `markClassAttendance`, `createClass`, `planSessions` / `scheduleSessions` (single or weekly; weekly keeps the same Pacific clock time across the Nov 1 and Mar 14 clock changes), `cancelSession`, `setCapacity`, `sessionView`, and `classLedger`. `engine/ledger.ts` combines it with the boat ledger as `clubLedger`: ending a household or removing an adult now also takes them off future classes (not as a late cancellation), moves the waiting lists up behind them, and writes `signups_cancelled` to the household's file.
- **The waiting list.** A chair freed by someone leaving, by more chairs, or by a member leaving the club goes to the first person waiting, in the same all-or-nothing change. After the move-up cutoff (2 hours before) a free chair goes to whoever asks first, and someone already waiting can take it. Order is the order names were written (database `seq`), so someone who leaves and comes back goes to the end.
- **Facts, not rules.** Unlike boats, staff **cannot** set aside class rules for one member: they raise the chairs instead. Reason: a sign-up has no room in its record for an override reason, and "add a chair" is the honest tool. Grant confirmed this Oct 22 (open question L).
- **One place at a time.** A chair *or* a waiting spot holds the person's time. Booking a boat is refused if you (or your crew) are in a class then (`in_class`); signing up is refused if a boat is booked then (`boat_busy`) or another class overlaps (`person_busy`). A class holding a room makes `checkBooking` refuse that room (`room_in_use`), for staff too.
- **The database enforces** (migration `20261025000000_club_classes.sql`, tested against a real Postgres): chairs never over-filled, a room never used by two classes or by a class and a booking at once (one lock per room shared by both checks), one live line per person per session, sign-ups only move forward, a session cannot be moved or revived, chairs cannot drop below the people in them. Each rule was broken on purpose to prove a test notices.
- **Screens.** `/club/classes` (pick a day, see chairs left, your place), `/club/classes/[sessionId]` (the rules in words, why a sign-up is refused, sign up / join the waiting list / take a free chair / leave; staff also see the sheet and names, mark attendance, change chairs, cancel the class and see who to contact), `/club/my-classes`, and for staff `/club/classes/manage` (add a class, schedule one date or a weekly run, with every clash explained before anything is saved). The `/club/book` day view shows a room held by a class as "Class".
- **Sample data.** Invented Boathouse gym and Clubhouse room; Morning Yoga, Erg Conditioning, Stretch & Mobility for a fortnight; tomorrow's Erg Conditioning is full (4 chairs) with two people waiting.
- **Not built, on purpose:** emails or texts (a member whose waiting spot became a chair only sees it in My classes; Grant confirmed that is fine for now, open question N), an attendance screen beyond the buttons on the class page, fees, class series editing (cancel one session and schedule another).

## Step 4: events and RSVPs (built Oct 23, reworked the same day; in review)

Grant's answers (Oct 23): each adult RSVPs for themselves **plus a guest count**; an event can have an **optional limit with a waitlist** that works like the classes; an emailed link identifies who is replying with **a personal secret link per adult, one click**. Grant said there should be **no "not going"**: events are ticketed, so you only RSVP if you want to go, and changing your mind is just cancelling. **"Ticketed" means a price per spot is recorded and the household is billed later with its dues; nothing is collected at RSVP** (Grant, Oct 23: open question R answered). Assumed unless Grant objects: only staff create events; events do **not** clash with boats or classes; members see counts and their own RSVP, never other names.

**The RSVP screen (Grant's design, Oct 23).** The person picks **how many people are coming, counting themselves**, with a minus button on one side and a plus on the other (`PartyStepper`, the only client-side piece). Before they press anything the page says how it will go: room for the party, or "There are only 2 spots available. There is a waitlist. To RSVP now, lower the number to 2. Or join the waitlist for 4." The words come from the pure functions in `src/club/party-words.ts` (tested without a browser), and the server runs the same rules again on submit. If they join the waitlist with more people than there are spots, they are told no, told there is a waitlist and that they are on it, told which smaller number would work, and told **when the place will be removed**.

The building comparison: an event is a building social. Places are the seats, an RSVP is a reply card for one leaseholder plus guests, and the waiting list is the queue for cancellations.

- **Pieces.** An *event* (title, place, start and end, optional limit on places, optional price per place, optional reply-by moment). An *RSVP* is one member's reply: how many guests they bring, and its state: `going`, `waitlisted`, or how it ended: `cancelled`, `late_cancelled`, `attended`, `no_show`. The RSVP remembers the price per place at the time, and whether it came from the event page or an emailed link.
- **Spots.** The member and each guest take one spot. The limit counts people, not RSVPs. A *party* is the member plus guests (the screen says "party of 3"; the database still stores `guests`, the party minus the member). If the whole party does not fit, the **whole party waits together**. Guests are only a number (no names, not members).
- **Rules are data.** `src/club/settings/event-rules.json` (placeholders): RSVP closes 60 minutes before the start (or at the reply-by moment staff set), cancel without penalty until 48 hours before, the automatic move-up runs until 2 hours before, waitlist up to 30 parties, up to 4 guests per RSVP (a party of 5), and **a waitlist place is held for 60 minutes** (`waitlistHoldMinutes`; 0 means it never runs out). `parseEventRules` refuses typos in words.
- **The engine** is `src/club/engine/events.ts`: `checkRsvp` (every reason in plain words, read only), `rsvp`, `changeGuests`, `cancelRsvp`, `markRsvpAttendance`, `createEvent`, `updateEvent`, `cancelEvent`, `eventView`, `promoteWaitlist` and `eventLedger`. `clubLedger` now also takes leaving adults off future events (not as late), moves the lists up behind them, and writes `rsvps_cancelled` to the household's file.
- **The waitlist.** When spots free up (a cancellation, a smaller party, a bigger or removed limit, a household leaving), the **first party in line that fits** gets them at once, in the same all-or-nothing change. A party that does not fit yet **keeps its place in line** and a later, smaller party that fits goes ahead (Grant confirmed Oct 23, open question S). A newcomer who fits goes straight in. After the move-up cutoff (2 hours before) free spots go to whoever asks first, and someone already waiting can take them if their whole party fits. Order is the order names were written (database `seq`), so someone who cancels and comes back goes to the end.
- **The one-hour hold (Grant, Oct 23).** A waitlist place lasts **one hour from the member's last action on the site** (joining, changing the party, or pressing **Keep my place**); then it is removed ("Your place on the waitlist ran out, so it was removed. Join the waitlist again."). Opening the page does not count (nothing is recorded by looking). Joining again goes to the end of the line. It is stored as `holdUntil` on the RSVP and goes away the moment the person gets spots. Expiry is **lazy**: every event change first removes expired places (`sweepExpiredHolds`, cancelled by the system with reason `HOLD_RAN_OUT`, not as a late cancellation), and every read hides them, so nothing needs a timer. The consequence: a freed spot never waits on an expired place, and nobody sees a stale line. The sample club's waitlists never run out (sample rules use 0) so the demo stays put.
- **Facts, not rules.** As with classes, staff cannot RSVP someone past the rules; they raise the limit instead.
- **Personal email links.** `engine/rsvp-links.ts`: each adult's link carries a code that is a keyed fingerprint (HMAC-SHA256) of "this event, this member", made with a secret that lives only on the server (`CLUB_LINK_SECRET`, see `.env.example`). Codes are computed, never stored, and checked in constant time. A link only fits that adult and that event, so it can only RSVP, change guests or cancel *that* RSVP. **Opening a link records nothing** (email scanners open links); a button press does. Every wrong link looks like a page that does not exist. The link signs nobody in and sets no cookie. `identity.ts` holds `resolveRsvpLink` next to `getCurrentActor` so step 6 can replace both together.
- **The secret.** With no `CLUB_LINK_SECRET`: a random one per run while developing on a computer; in a deployed build, **no secret and no links** (rather than a different secret on every server). The event page then says so. There is no way to revoke one person's link without rotating the secret for everyone (open question Q).
- **The CSV.** Staff download `/api/club/events/[eventId]/links`: one personal link per active adult of an active household (email, name, household, link), with spreadsheet formula protection, for a Mailchimp merge field later. Nothing is sent. The Mailchimp contact ID slot stays empty.
- **The database enforces** (migration `20261026000000_club_events.sql`, tested against a real Postgres): an event with a limit never has more people going than the limit (member plus guests), a name is on a list once, an RSVP only moves forward, a cancelled event stays cancelled, the limit cannot drop below the people going. `club.end_household()` is replaced again to cancel future RSVPs too. Each rule was broken on purpose to prove a test notices.
- **Screens.** `/club/events` (what is coming, places left, your RSVP), `/club/events/[eventId]` (the rules in words, the people picker with the price total and a live message, why an RSVP is refused, RSVP / join the waitlist / RSVP for the smaller number instead / take free spots / change the party / keep my place / cancel; staff also see the list with names, guests and how each RSVP was made, mark attendance, edit, cancel the event and see who to contact, download the links), `/club/events/manage` (staff add an event; mistakes are explained before anything is saved), and the link page `/club/rsvp/[eventId]/[memberId]/[token]`.
- **Sample data.** Invented Members' Dinner (12 spots, $45, full with two parties waiting), Fall Regatta Social (40 spots, $25), Boathouse Work Party (free, no limit).
- **Not built, on purpose:** emails or texts of any kind (nothing is told about a change, a cancellation or a waiting-list move-up; open question T), payment, per-person link revoke, event series, an attendance screen beyond the buttons on the event page, editing an event's time after it has RSVPs without telling anyone.

## Step 5: the staff office (built Oct 23; in review)

Grant's answers (Oct 23): the office covers the **household desk** and a **staff home page**, plus a light **attendance** tool: members will check themselves in on an **iPad** (its own step, right after this one), so a full attendance desk is not needed, but **staff must be able to check someone in** when the member cannot do it themselves. House rules are changed **through Claude Code** (a preview plus the automated checks), so there is **no rules-editing page** this round. On the iPad, members will **pick their name** (like signing the clipboard at a front desk). A reservation nobody checked in for becomes a **no-show automatically** the moment its time ends, and staff can correct it afterwards.

Grant's answers after reviewing the pull request (Oct 23): events are marked by staff, not automatically; staff can correct an outcome any time; a multi-seat boat has one outcome per outing; **certain reservations, like the club room and work-from-club, should not require check-in at all** (built, see below); and **members should be able to check in themselves from their own phone**, as well as on the iPad (not built yet: it belongs to the next step).

The building comparison: the manager's office. The front desk can sign up a new tenant, add or remove a name on a lease, change the lease type, certify someone for the rooftop grill, or end a lease, and always sees a list of what that will cancel before it happens. The clipboard by the door (the check-in record) says who was ticked off, when, by whom, and what it said before; nothing on it is ever erased.

- **The check-in record.** `club.attendance_log` / `ClubState.attendance`: one line every time someone is recorded as having come (`attended`) or not (`no_show`), for boat and space bookings (`booking`), class chairs (`class`) and event RSVPs (`event`). A line holds who it is about, when, the outcome, what it said before (`previous`), who wrote it (`by`), how (`via`: `staff`, `automatic`, or `ipad` for the next step), and an optional note. Lines are only added; a correction is a new line. On a booking "attended" is stored as `completed`.
- **Rules are data.** `src/club/settings/attendance-rules.json` (placeholders): check-in opens **30 minutes before the start**; an unchecked booking or chair becomes a no-show **0 minutes after it ends**; and `checkInNotNeededFor` lists the kinds of booking that **need no check-in at all** (today: Room Space and Work Space). `parseAttendanceRules` refuses typos and unknown booking types in words.
- **Automatic no-show.** `settleAttendance` in `engine/attendance.ts` marks unchecked boat and space bookings (every kind except those in `checkInNotNeededFor`) and enrolled class chairs once their time has run out. A booking of an exempt kind is simply over: it stays `upcoming` in the records (nothing claims anyone came), screens say "Finished", staff cannot check it in or mark it, and it is on none of the staff lists. It records the moment it came due (end plus grace), not when it happened to run, so the result is the same whenever it is first noticed. **Events are not marked automatically** (there may be no iPad at the door; staff mark them; they still show "Awaiting attendance record"). The stores apply it (`Settler`, given to `MemoryClubStore` and `PgClubStore`, wired in `store/instance.ts` on `clubNow`): readers see the settled club without anything being saved by looking, and every change settles first so it is saved with the change. In Postgres the settle is saved as its own step before the change, because a booking that ran out of time and was then corrected by staff in the same moment is two steps with a line each.
- **Staff check-in.** `recordAttendance(store, ctx, rules, input)` replaces the three older marking functions. Staff only. Check-in opens per the rule; a no-show can only be recorded once the start has passed; a corrected outcome (attended and no-show swap either way) needs no reason, writes a new line, and may carry a note; **confirming** an automatic no-show (staff press "Confirm no-show") writes a line with nothing changed, which takes it off the "needs a look" list; repeating a human decision is refused. On a multi-seat boat staff can name which of the people on it they are checking in; the outing has one outcome.
- **The database enforces** (migration `20261027000000_club_attendance.sql`, tested against a real Postgres): a line is about exactly one record of the kind it says; who and how agree (system for automatic, staff for the front desk, a member for the iPad); only staff write a confirmation; the record is append-only (no update, delete or truncate); a booking, chair or RSVP may move between attended and no-show but nowhere else; and **no outcome can change without a matching line written at the same moment that says what it was before** (a deferred check at the end of the change). Each of these was broken on purpose to prove a test notices.
- **Household desk.** Staff only. `/club/admin/households/new` (add a household with a primary adult and an optional second; mistakes come back with what was typed and are explained in the engine's own words), and on each household's page (`/club/household/[id]`, staff see a "Staff tools" section): change the membership type, add an adult, grant a qualification, remove an adult, end the membership. **Removing an adult and ending a membership go through a page that first shows what will be cancelled** (`/club/admin/households/[id]/remove/[memberId]` and `/end`): `departurePreview` in `engine/departure.ts` names every boat and space booking, seat on someone else's boat, class place and event RSVP, worked out without changing anything, and a test holds it to the real thing (what the preview names is exactly what the real step cancels, on both stores). Removing a primary adult makes staff choose the new primary; the last adult cannot be removed (end the membership instead). A household that has ended cannot be changed; a returning household is a new record (open question A).
- **Staff home page.** `/club/admin`: **needs a look** (automatic no-shows from the last 3 days that nobody has looked at, with "They were there" and "Confirm no-show"), today's counts, what is out of service, classes and events in the next 7 days with people waiting, the staff tools, and the check-in rules in words with the placeholder warning (`officeSummary` in `engine/office.ts`).
- **Check-in page.** `/club/admin/check-in`: with no name typed it lists everything within two hours of now; typed, it finds active members by name or email (up to 6) and shows what each is down for from a day back to a day ahead, with buttons that only offer what will be accepted. The same buttons (`AttendanceButtons`, one handler: `/api/club/attendance`) are on All bookings, each class sheet and each event sheet. The `back` address a button returns to is only believed if it is inside `/club`.
- **Sample data.** Today's invented bookings, made "yesterday": Cormorant 7 am (Alex Brooks) and Grebe 8 am (Kenji Tanaka) over with nobody checked in, so they are automatic no-shows to look at; Mink noon to 2 pm (Priya Rao) under way; Beaver 1:30 pm (Dev Rao) with check-in open; Drift 3 pm (Julien Moreau) with check-in opening at 2:30 pm.
- **Not built, on purpose:** a house-rules page (rules change through Claude Code), adding or retiring boats and spaces, emails or texts, any limit on how long ago a correction can be made, per-member counts of no-shows (what a no-show leads to is open question I), and editing a person's details after they are added. (Member check-in on the iPad and on their own phone was built next: see below.)

## Member check-in (built Oct 23; in review)

Grant's answers (Oct 23): members check themselves in on the front-desk **iPad** and on **their own phone**. The iPad lists **only people with something they can check in for right now** (not the whole membership), with a box to type the first letters of a name. It lives at a **private address** that staff open once on the iPad and bookmark (a secret in an environment variable; changing it switches the old address off). The phone button **trusts the member and records "from their phone"**: no location or code check. Assumed unless Grant objects: a member can only say "I came", from when check-in opens until the time runs out; they cannot undo a tap (staff correct, as before); events stay staff-marked; the club room and work-from-club never appear; one outcome per outing, so anyone on a multi-seat boat can tap and the record says who did; the iPad goes back to its start after 20 untouched seconds.

The building comparison: signing the clipboard yourself instead of asking the front desk. The iPad is the lobby kiosk, kept on a key at the front desk; the phone is the tenant's own copy of the sign-in sheet.

- **The engine** is in `engine/attendance.ts`. `memberCheckIn(store, ctx, rules, { kind, id, via })` is one all-or-nothing change: the actor must be a member who is still active in an active household and who is really on the reservation (the booker, crew still aboard, or the one holding the chair); events and anything cancelled, exempt (`checkInNotNeededFor`), waiting without a chair or already recorded are refused in words; it opens `checkInOpensMinutesBefore` before the start and closes at the end plus `noShowAfterEndMinutes` (the moment the automatic no-show would land). It writes `status = completed/attended`, and one line: `by` the member, `via` `ipad` or `phone`, `previous` null. Refusals carry `not_open`, `already_recorded` or `too_late` (their notices are in `web.ts`). `selfCheckInPhase` (one reservation: not yet / open / done), `checkInChoices` (what one member can do now or soon) and `checkInPeople` (the iPad's names, by last name, two with the same name told apart by household) read the same rules; `howCheckedIn` gives staff the words ("on the iPad", "from their phone", "by Ben Quill on the iPad").
- **The record.** `via` is now `staff | automatic | ipad | phone`. Migration `20261028000000_club_member_check_in.sql` (tested against a real Postgres): who and how agree (a member for the iPad and phone); a member's own tap is only ever "attended", the first line for that record, about themselves (`by_id = member_id`), for a booking or class chair (never an event); and a trigger refuses a tap by someone not on the outing (booker or crew not removed) or not holding the chair. Each of these was broken on purpose to prove a test notices.
- **The iPad** (`src/app/club/ipad/`, outside `(member)` so it shows no menu, picker or "acting as" line). The address is `/club/ipad/<code>` where the code is a keyed fingerprint (HMAC-SHA256) of the word "ipad" made with `CLUB_IPAD_SECRET` (`engine/ipad-address.ts`, `ipad-secret.ts`): computed, never stored, checked in constant time; the secret itself is never in an address. Opening it signs nobody in and sets no cookie; every wrong address is "page not found". The start screen lists only people with something open now, with a type-to-narrow box (`name-filter.ts`: matches the start of any word, so "pri", "rao" and "priya r" all find Priya Rao). A person's screen (`/club/ipad/<code>/<memberId>`) shows only what is open now or just done, each with one big "I'm here" button; someone with nothing open is sent back to the start, so the screen never says anything about a person who is not there. It returns to the start after 20 untouched seconds (`AutoReturn`; a touch restarts the wait). The button posts to `/api/club/ipad/check-in`, which starts with `startIpadPost` (the address must be genuine; the person is whoever was picked). Staff see the address at `/club/admin/ipad` (linked from the Staff office), with set-up steps and what to do if it leaks. In a deployed build with no `CLUB_IPAD_SECRET` there is no iPad; while developing a random secret is made for the run.
- **The phone.** An "I'm here" button (`SelfCheckIn`) on My bookings and My classes for a reservation that is open, or a line saying when it opens. One handler, `/api/club/check-in`, uses `getCurrentActor()` like every other member action, so step 6 replaces nothing here. While under way a checked-in outing shows "Checked in" and stays under "Upcoming".
- **Staff screens** say how each check-in was made, and staff can correct any of them as before.
- **Layout.** `src/app/club/layout.tsx` is now only the shell (fonts, the switch); the menu bar, banner and "acting as" line moved to `(member)/layout.tsx`, and every earlier page moved into `(member)` (URLs unchanged).
- **Sample data** (today, 1 pm Pacific on the frozen test clock): Amara Okonkwo has the Otter kayak (the iPad's single-boat example), Chidi Okonkwo the Skimmer paddle board (the phone's), Camille and Julien Moreau (partners sharing an email address) hold chairs in a new Lunchtime Mobility class at 1:15 pm, and Walter Pemberton has the club room, which needs no check-in so he never appears on the iPad.
- **Not built, on purpose:** location, code or device proof for the phone button; a staff PIN on the iPad; emails or texts; undoing a tap as a member; a member checking in their crew separately (one tap covers the outing); events (staff mark them).

## Step 6: real logins (built Oct 23; in review)

Grant's answers (Oct 23): people sign in with an **email link** (no password); partners sharing one address are **asked "Which of you?" and the device remembers the answer**; for now **no real email is sent** (the test build shows the "email" on a test outbox page and the real sender is chosen later); and **staff sign in from an email list kept in the site's private settings**, so the record shows who did what. (Open question C, shared emails: answered.)

The building comparison: the lobby door. A sign-in link is a one-time key left in the mailbox; the mailbox may hold mail for two people, so after the key turns the person says which of them they are. The key only works once, only for a few minutes, and the doorman asks again at every visit whether you are still a tenant.

- **The flow.** `/club/sign-in` (type your address) sends `/club/sign-in/<code>` by email. Opening that page **records nothing** (email programs open links to check them). It shows who the address can sign in as: one button for one person, or "Which of you?" for partners, with the person this device chose last time offered first. Pressing a button signs in: the link is used up, a session is made, and two cookies are set (`ccs_session`, the key, and `ccs_signin_who`, the device's memory of who it is). Signing out (`/api/club/sign-out`) forgets the key on the server and clears both the key and any test-picker cookie.
- **No one learns who is a member.** Asking for a link gives the same answer for every well-formed address; nothing is made or sent for an address nobody can sign in with (including someone whose household ended or who left it). A mail that fails to send looks the same too. The address typed is never put in a web address. A known limit: a request for a known address takes a little longer than for an unknown one.
- **Rules are data.** `src/club/settings/login-rules.json` (placeholders): a link works **15 minutes** and once; a browser stays signed in **30 days**; one address can ask for **5 links an hour** (more are quietly ignored). `parseLoginRules` refuses typos and silly values in words (links 5 to 60 minutes, sessions 1 to 90 days, 1 to 20 links an hour).
- **The engine** is `src/club/auth/login.ts`: `requestSignInLink`, `previewSignIn`, `confirmSignIn`, `resolveSession`, `signOut`, `rememberedIdentity`. `identities.ts` decides who an address opens (`identitiesForEmail`: active adults of active households, plus staff entries) and whether a session still holds (`identityOf`). `confirmSignIn` accepts only someone the link's address opens; a wrong choice changes nothing and leaves the link usable; of ten simultaneous presses exactly one signs in. `resolveSession` is asked on every visit, so ending a household, removing an adult or taking a staff address off the list ends their browsers at once, and a browser that stopped working is signed out for good.
- **Staff.** `CLUB_STAFF_LOGINS` is entries `email=Name` separated by semicolons or new lines (`auth/staff-list.ts`; a mistake is refused in words, and the list is ignored where the member area is off). A staff session's label is the name on the list, so `by` on every check-in, household change and booking override now carries a real name instead of "Test staff". The list is private settings (Vercel > Environment variables), not code: whoever owns the site's settings changes it (question E). A person who is both staff and a member of the club gets both choices on their link.
- **Where things are kept.** `AuthStore` (`auth/store.ts` in memory, `auth/postgres.ts` in Postgres, chosen by `auth/instance.ts` like the club store) holds only **fingerprints** (SHA-256) of link codes and session keys; nothing is deleted (a used link and a signed-out browser are marked). Migration `20261029000000_club_logins.sql` (tested against a real Postgres, each rule broken on purpose): fingerprints of the right shape, lower-case addresses, links live at most 24 hours and are used once while alive, sessions name exactly one member or one staff address and last at most 90 days, rows change only to be used or signed out and are never removed, and a session cannot be handed to someone else. Times come from the caller (`clubNow()`), never from the database clock, so the test clock works.
- **First sign-in.** The first time a member signs in, their empty `loginId` slot is filled (best effort: a failure never stops a sign-in), and the household page shows staff "has signed in" or "has not signed in yet".
- **Email.** `auth/mail.ts`: a `Mailer` interface and the test outbox (the last 50 "emails" kept in memory, shown at `/club/outbox`, which has a warning on it and exists only where invented data is allowed). `getMailer()` returns the outbox there and null everywhere else, in which case the sign-in page says email sign-in is not switched on and no link is made. The real sender, a `Mailer` that sends through Resend, was built later (see "The real email sender" below). Sign-in emails link to `CLUB_SITE_URL` when it is set; only a test build may fall back to the request's own address (a forged Host header could otherwise send a victim's link to someone else's server).
- **Safety.** Form posts to the three sign-in handlers must come from this site's own pages (`sameOrigin`, using `Origin` and `Sec-Fetch-Site`); the session cookie is `httpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Secure` on https, and its life is counted by the browser's clock; every page and handler is "not found" on the live site, including with the right staff list set and a signed-in cookie sent.
- **Screens.** `/club/sign-in`, `/club/sign-in/[token]`, `/club/outbox` (test only), and "Sign in" / "Sign out" in the menu. The "acting as" line reads "Signed in as ..." for a real sign-in and "Acting as ..." for the test picker. `getCurrentActor()` answers once per request (`React.cache`).
- **Not built, on purpose:** a real email sender (built later, see "The real email sender" below); "sign out everywhere"; seeing or ending someone else's sessions; editing a person's email (built next: see "Editing a person's details" below and question AK); passwords, codes by text, or two-step checks; anything on the iPad, which keeps its own private address; real members.

## Editing a person's details (built Oct 23; in review)

Grant's answers (Oct 23): **staff only** (members ask staff; a member cannot change their own name, email or phone); when the **email changes the person is signed out of every device** and signs in again with the new address; the household's file records **what each detail was and what it is now** (old and new values), and who changed it.

The building comparison: the front desk correcting a name or phone number on a lease. Changing the email is changing the lock on the unit: old keys stop working, and the tenant asks for a new one at the new address. The tenant file always gets a line saying what it said before and what it says now.

- **What can be changed.** First name, last name, email, phone (`DETAIL_FIELDS` in `engine/households.ts`). Role, status and the outside-ID slots have their own tools. Someone who has left, or whose household's membership ended, cannot be edited (their file is closed); a save that changes nothing is refused in words ("Nothing was changed: ...", error code `unchanged`) and writes no line.
- **The engine.** `planMemberDetails` says what saving would do, or why not, and changes nothing; `updateMemberDetails` runs the same check inside one all-or-nothing change and writes one `member_updated` line to the household's file. The line's sentence reads "Details changed for Ada Quill: name from ... to ...; email from ... to ...; phone from ... to ..." (each value shortened to 50 characters so the sentence always fits the file's 500-character limit; the full values are kept in the line's details as `<field>From` and `<field>To`). `signInChanged` is true when the address as the sign-in compares it (trimmed, lower case) is different, so a change of capital letters alone signs nobody out.
- **Signing out.** `editMemberDetails` (`auth/edit-details.ts`) is what the page uses. The club's records and the keys are separate stores, so it cannot be one all-or-nothing change; the order is chosen so the failure that can happen is the safe one: check the change, **sign the person out everywhere first**, make the change, then **sign them out again** (in case they signed in with the old address in between). If the first sign-out fails, nothing changed and staff just try again. A sign-in link sent to the old address before the change dies by itself (an address opens whoever has it on file when the link is used), and partners sharing an address are unaffected unless it is their own email that changes. Staff sessions are keyed by address and are never touched.
- **An address sign-in can use.** `cleanMember` now refuses any address `normalizeEmail` (the sign-in's own test) would refuse, such as `ada..quill@example.com` or `ada quill@example.com`, for adding an adult as well as editing: the earlier looser check could put an address on file that nobody could ever sign in with.
- **The database enforces** (migration `20261030000000_club_member_details.sql`, tested against a real Postgres): the new line kind exists; a name, email or phone **cannot change without a matching `member_updated` line in the same change** (about this member, in this household's file, saying what each changed detail was and what it is now; checked at the end of the change, so the line may come after); and an adult who has left or whose membership ended **cannot be edited at all** (`guard_member` replaced with the same rules plus this one). A save that names the details without changing them (a first sign-in filling the login slot, a role change) needs no line. Each rule was broken on purpose to prove a test notices.
- **Screens.** On each household's page (staff only) every adult has "Change name, email or phone..." which opens `/club/admin/households/[id]/members/[memberId]/edit`: a form with what is on file, a note that a change of email signs the person out of every device, and the engine's own words when something is wrong (the form comes back with what was typed). The handler is `/api/club/households/update-member` (`startPost`, so staff only and "not found" on the live site). After saving, the household page says what happened ("Details saved...", and, for an email change, that the person was signed out of every device). The staff-only tools and the file are the only places any of this shows.
- **Not built, on purpose:** members editing their own details (question AO); asking the old address to confirm a change; telling the person by email that their address changed (there is no real sender yet); editing the household name, membership type or join date (the type already has its own tool); "sign out everywhere" without an email change (question AM).

## Step 8: public website pages (built Oct 23; merged as PR 21)

Grant's answers (Oct 23): the public pages live **under `/site`**, hidden on the live site exactly like `/club` (visible on previews, in development and in CI; "page not found" on production), so every dashboard link stays exactly as it is and nothing public goes live before cutover. At cutover (open question D) Grant and the board decide where the dashboard moves and the public site takes the home address. The **words are carried over from the current Wix site** (collegeclubseattle.com, the address Grant gave), read as a visitor reads it: public pages only, no Wix account access, no changes to Wix, and no attempt to get past the members' login. Pages: **Home, About, Membership, Rowing and fitness, Contact**, and a **Sign in** link in the header that goes to the existing `/club/sign-in`. **No forms** at first: contact details only (no real email sender existed, and a public form would collect strangers' details with nowhere safe to send them). **Superseded Oct 29: the Request a tour form was added, saved for staff (see "Public site refresh").** Not chosen, so not built: a public events list (it would need a "show to the public" choice on each event).

Grant's answers to the step 7 plan, given in the same round, were parked and then used when step 7 was built (see "Step 7" below).

The building comparison: the lobby sign and the leasing office window. Before, only tenants with a key saw the inside; this is the sign on the street, with the hours, the rent for each kind of unit, and the phone number.

- **What was readable.** Public on the current site: Home, Membership (what every membership includes and the two plans with their prices), Contact (address, phone, hours, the membership email, the two directors' names and roles; **their own email addresses are deliberately left out**, Grant, Oct 23: the one public email is the membership address, and a web form comes with the real email sender). **Behind the members' login, so not readable and not carried over:** Club information, Programs, Rowing, Social events (and the class calendar, event list, weekly email archive and Sockeye Racing Camp pages are the same or are app-driven). The About and Rowing and fitness pages are therefore **placeholders built only from sentences on the public pages** (Grant, Oct 23: use placeholder information for now), each with a visible "Preview note, not part of the page" saying so. Also not carried: the live water feed and weather, and the Request a tour and Apply today forms (the Membership page points to the membership email and phone, with a preview note saying why).
- **The words are data.** `src/site/content/site.json` holds the club details (name, address, phone, the one public email, hours, directors' names and roles) and every page (heading, intro, an optional sign-in button, sections of paragraphs, bullets and cards, and an optional preview note). `parseSiteContent` (`src/site/content.ts`) refuses a typo, a missing page, an empty section, a phone number or email that cannot be right, a too-long line, and a button that goes anywhere but a page of this site or `/club/sign-in`, in plain words, so a mistaken edit is caught by the tests and the build. A test also checks the phone and email are written the same way everywhere, the membership email is the only email address anywhere in the content (and on every page in the browser), no text links to another website, and every menu path is a page that exists. Staff change the words with Claude Code like the house rules; there is no editing page.
- **The pages** (`src/app/site/`): a shared layout (menu, a "Preview of the new club website. It is not live yet." banner, footer with address, phone, email and hours), one component that draws any page from its content (`SitePage`), and the five pages; the Contact page adds the details block. Same brand fonts and colors as the rest of the app, roomier than the member area; the cards stack and nothing scrolls sideways at 375 pixels wide; every menu link is at least 36 pixels tall. Every page is marked noindex until cutover.
- **Hidden like `/club`.** Every layout and page checks `requireSite()` itself (a layout's "not found" does not stop the page beside it, and a page's "not found" does not hide the layout around it), and the page titles come from `pageMetadata`, which gives nothing when the site is off. `site-hidden.spec.ts` proves, on the production-style servers (switch off, and switch on at the live address), that every page answers 404 with none of its words, its title or the club's contact details anywhere in the response, and that `/` is still the dashboard.
- **Not built, on purpose:** forms (the Request a tour form came later, Oct 29), a public events list, search-engine settings (everything stays noindex until cutover), the live water feed, the class calendar, the weekly email archive, and moving the dashboard off the home address (that is the cutover decision).

## Step 7: importing households from a roster file (built Oct 23; merged as PR 22)

Grant's answers (Oct 23): the real roster comes from the **QuickBooks customer list**; the import is **upload, preview, then confirm** (nothing is saved until staff press the confirm button, and every problem is explained in words); the file is **one row per adult**, with the household's QuickBooks ID, name, membership type and join date repeated on each of that household's rows; a second upload **adds new households and skips ones already here** (matched by QuickBooks ID) and **never changes, ends or removes anything**; **membership type and join date are both required**, a blank is refused in words; and it is **built and tested with invented files only**. The real import is done later, at switch-on, by staff, after the email sender, the staff list and `CLUB_SITE_URL` are settled (AI, AN).

The building comparison: move-in day. The leasing office gets the tenant list from the old filing cabinet (QuickBooks) as a spreadsheet. The clerk first gets a read-back ("14 new leases, 23 people; 2 already on file and left alone; row 7 has a unit type that does not exist"), and only when the list is clean does the clerk sign them all in at once. Nobody already on file is touched, and nobody is ever moved out.

- **The file.** A CSV (what a spreadsheet saves as "CSV (Comma delimited)"). Columns, found by their names in any order, spaces and capitals ignored: QuickBooks ID, Household name, Membership type, Joined, First name, Last name, Email, and optionally Phone and Role (Primary or Additional; with no one marked, the first adult listed is the primary). Extra columns, such as the ones in a QuickBooks export, are ignored and mentioned. Dates are `2020-01-15` or `1/15/2020`. The blank template (`/api/club/import/template`, staff only) holds three invented example rows, and the import refuses them (`EXAMPLE-1`, `EXAMPLE-2`) so nobody adds them by accident. Limits: 500 KB and 3,000 rows. An Excel file, a file with a stray binary character, an empty file and a quotation mark that is never closed are each refused in words that say what to do.
- **The reader** is `engine/roster.ts` (`readRoster`) over `engine/csv.ts` (`parseCsv`: quoted cells with commas, line breaks and doubled quotation marks, any line endings, the invisible marker some spreadsheets add). It changes nothing and reports **every** problem at once, with row numbers as a spreadsheet shows them (the header is row 1): an empty or over-long field, a membership type that is not Resident, Intermediate or Non-Resident (written any way, "non resident" is fine), a join date that is not a date, not real, before 1900 or in the future (Pacific "today"), an email or name the sign-in could not use (the same test as adding an adult), two rows with the same QuickBooks ID that disagree about the name, type or date, an adult listed twice, and two rows both marked Primary. Anything wrong means nothing can be added.
- **The plan and the run** are `engine/import.ts`. `planImport` says exactly what a file would do and changes nothing: the households it would add, the households already here (skipped, and told apart if one of them ended), and **notices** that do not stop the import: columns ignored, a household with the same name but a different QuickBooks ID, an address used in more than one household in the file, an address already used by someone in the club. `runImport` is staff only and one all-or-nothing change on the store's draft copy under the writers lock: it **reads the file again inside the change**, refuses a file with problems, refuses when nothing is new, and refuses when the file or the club's records no longer match what was previewed (a fingerprint of the exact file plus the counts the staff member saw), then adds each household with its adults. Two staff pressing confirm at once add the households once (the second sees them already here and is told so).
- **What gets written.** Each new household goes through `addHouseholdRecords` (shared with the household desk's "Add a household", so there is one way to make a household): the household with its QuickBooks ID, the adults, and a `joined` line in the household's file that says "Added from a roster file; join date ..." and records who did it. The QuickBooks ID slot has existed since step 1 and is already unique in the database, so there is **no migration** in step 7. A household whose QuickBooks ID is already taken is refused by the engine as well.
- **The screen.** `/club/admin/import` (a link in the Staff office's tools), staff only (a member gets "page not found", someone signed out is asked to sign in). A file box, **Check the file** (shows the problems, or the preview), and **Add these households** which only appears for a clean preview. The preview lists up to 50 households with their adults and says "...and N more" past that. Changing the file clears the preview, so the Add button never belongs to a different file. The two buttons are one server action (`importRoster`), which checks the member area is on and the person is staff before reading the file, and refuses an Add that does not carry the fingerprint and the counts of a check (plain digits only). After adding, the page says how many households and adults were added.
- **Hidden like everything in `/club`.** The page, the template and the action are "page not found" on the live site; `club-hidden.spec.ts` covers all three on the production-style servers. The import screen is the first place that uses a Next.js server action instead of a form handler, so that spec also calls the real action with the ID the build gave it (read from `.next/server/server-reference-manifest.json`), as the browser would, with and without a test-member cookie, and checks the answer is "page not found" with none of the club's words. Broken on purpose (the member-area check removed and the staff check replaced by one that throws), it fails with a 500 on both servers. A plain form post to the page gets Next.js's own "Invalid Server Action request" (400), the same as the dashboard's home page does; that is the framework, not the import.
- **Not built, on purpose:** updating households that are already here (a second upload never changes them, so corrections go through the household desk); ending households that are missing from a file (question AX: resignations need their own plan); importing juniors (out of scope) or staff; reading an Excel file directly; fixing a file on the screen; undoing an import (each household can be ended like any other, but ending is a membership decision, not an undo); importing history from before today (the join date is kept, and the file records when it was added); a QuickBooks connection (CSV only, as decided).

## Step 9: the QuickBooks files (built Oct 24; in review)

Grant's answers (Oct 24): the files are **QuickBooks Online invoice files** (plus a plain charges file for reading, checking and entering by hand). One **invoice per household per month**, with a separate line for each kind of charge: **monthly dues, event tickets, visitor fees, bar and snack bar**. Grant approved this replacing the Oct 22 decision ("one charge line per household per month"). **Dues:** every household that is a member on the **1st** pays a full month, at the rate of the membership type it had on the 1st. **Event tickets:** **everyone who held spots** (came, did not come, or cancelled too late), the whole party times the price recorded at RSVP; an on-time cancellation and a waiting-list place are not billed. **Visitors:** visitors to the clubhouse (not event guests), signed in on the **front-desk iPad**, a count only (no names); up to 4 a day are free and **once a member has more than 4 in a day, every visitor that day costs $10**. **Bar and snack bar:** staff enter each charge on a screen. (**Superseded Oct 31 for the snack bar:** members put their own snacks on their account on an iPad, see "The honor snack bar iPad"; bar charges are still typed in by staff.) **Corrections:** the month is **locked when it is closed; a mistake is fixed next month** with an adjustment. **Sales tax:** Grant first chose "QuickBooks adds the tax" (Oct 24), then changed it on Oct 25: **no tax is charged on any line**, bar and snack bar included, because his monthly statements show none; the rules file's `taxable` list is empty (see question AY). No live QuickBooks connection. Real data is never used; every number and name is a placeholder until staff and the bookkeeper confirm them.

The building comparison: the rent ledger and the monthly statement run. Staff write bar and snack-bar charges and adjustments into the ledger; the lobby kiosk (the iPad) logs guests; on the 1st the office reads back each tenant's statement ("14 statements, $X in all; two tenants have no account number yet"), and only when that is clean does it close the books for the month. A closed month is never reopened: a mistake found later is a credit or an extra line on a later statement, so nothing is ever billed twice.

- **Rules are data.** `src/club/settings/billing-rules.json` (all marked placeholder): monthly dues per membership type (Resident $154.50 and Intermediate $103.00 are the amounts on the public Membership page; Non-Resident $77.25 is invented because the public page only says to ask), `visitorFreePerDay` 4, `visitorFeeCents` 1000 (charged for **every** visitor that day once over the free number), `visitorsPerEntryMax` 12 and `visitorsPerDayMax` 30 (typo guards), `invoiceDueDays` 15, the **QuickBooks product or service name** for each kind of line (Membership Dues, Event Tickets, Guest Fees, Bar Sales, Snack Bar Sales; QuickBooks matches these exactly), and which kinds carry sales tax (`taxable`: none, the list is `[]`; naming a kind such as `["bar", "snack_bar"]` marks its lines taxable in the preview and the plain file again). `parseBillingRules` refuses a typo, a missing key, a negative or silly number, and a name with a comma, quotation mark or line break, in words.
- **Charges.** `recordCharge` (staff only): a *sale* is a bar or snack-bar amount (never negative); an *adjustment* may be a credit and may go under any line, and needs a note. Dated today or an earlier day of an **open** month (never the future, never a closed month). `voidCharge` needs a reason, keeps the line, and works only while its month is open. Nothing is deleted.
- **Visitors.** `recordVisitors`: on the iPad the member signs in their own visitors (always dated today, 1 to 12 at a time, 30 a day at most); staff can enter or correct a count for today or an earlier open day. Only a **count** is kept (per member per day). A member who has left cannot bring visitors. `voidVisitors` as for charges. The fee is worked out per member per day when the month is planned: 4 or fewer is free, 5 or more costs every visitor that day.
- **The month.** `planBilling(state, rules, month, now)` says exactly what closing a month would do and changes nothing: each household's invoice (lines in a fixed order: dues, event tickets, visitor fees, bar, snack bar; adjustments after the plain line of their kind; invoice numbers `YYMM-NNNN`), the total, and **problems** that stop it (a month not finished yet, already closed, out of order, before the records this screen has loaded, a household with something to pay but **no QuickBooks ID**) and **notices** that do not (credit lines, ended households billed a full month, event RSVPs billed although attendance was never marked, the placeholder warning). Bar and snack-bar sales add up to **one line per household per kind** (the individual charges and their notes stay on the Charges screen, which marks them "Billed in September 2026"). Event tickets are billed in the month the event **ends**. `runBilling` (staff only) re-plans inside one all-or-nothing change, refuses with any problem, refuses if the month no longer matches what staff previewed (a fingerprint of the exact invoices), and then freezes every line. Months are closed **in order** (the first can be any month, then each next month). `suggestedMonth` offers the month after the last closed one, or the most recent finished month.
- **The files** (`engine/billing-files.ts`), worked out from the frozen lines, so downloading a month again always gives the same bytes. **QuickBooks Online invoice file:** columns `InvoiceNo, Customer, InvoiceDate, DueDate, Memo, Item(Product/Service), ItemDescription, ItemQuantity, ItemRate, ItemAmount`, dates written `MM/DD/YYYY` (the month's last day, and 15 days later), customer and item names exactly as in the rules and household names. Split into **parts** of whole invoices: QuickBooks takes at most 100 invoices and 1,000 rows (header included) per import. **Credits are left out** (QuickBooks does not import negative amounts), counted, and listed in the plain file; an invoice whose every line is a credit is left out too. Sales tax cannot be imported at all, and the whole invoice import is unavailable if automated sales tax is on in the QuickBooks account (question AY); with the club's own rules no line is taxed, and the closed-month steps say so. **Plain charges file:** every line (credits, the Taxable and Adjustment flags, the household's QuickBooks ID; Taxable is "No" on every line while the `taxable` list is empty). Text cells are protected against spreadsheet formulas (`csvCell`).
- **Staff screens.** `/club/admin/charges` (add a sale or adjustment, recent charges, void with a reason), `/club/admin/visitors` (enter a count for a member and day, recent entries with each day's total and fee, void), `/club/admin/billing` (pick a month; the **preview** with problems, notices, the invoice list (up to 50, then "...and N more") and the **Close** button, which only appears when there are no problems and carries the preview's fingerprint; for a closed month, the frozen invoices, the download links by part, the credits note and the steps for importing into QuickBooks Online; the list of closed months). The buttons are Next.js server actions (`charges/actions.ts`, `visitors/actions.ts`, `billing/actions.ts`, `households/actions.ts`), each starting with `startStaffAction()` (`web-actions.ts`: the member area must be on and the person must be staff, otherwise "page not found", before the form is read). Downloads: `/api/club/billing/<month>/qbo/<part>` and `/api/club/billing/<month>/charges` (staff only, `startStaffGet`, "not found" for anything else, `no-store`). If the Postgres store did not load all of an old month's lines, the screens say so and refuse the file rather than make a short one (`linesComplete`).
- **Visitors on the iPad** (outside `(member)`, no menu): a big "I'm bringing visitors" link on the start screen, then `/club/ipad/<code>/visitors` (type at least 3 letters of your name; **at most 5 names are ever shown and only active members**, so the shared screen cannot be used to browse the membership), then `/club/ipad/<code>/visitors/<memberId>` (the rule in words, the member's count so far today, one big number from 1 to 12, "Sign in my visitors"; it says what the day now costs, for example "That makes 5 visitors today.
