That is over 4, so each costs $10.00: $50.00, added to the household's bill."). The button is `/api/club/ipad/visitors` (`startIpadPost`, like the check-in button). A refusal says only "Those visitors could not be signed in. Check the number, or ask club staff for help." (nothing from the address is echoed). The screen returns to the start after 30 untouched seconds.
- **A household's QuickBooks ID.** The slot has existed since step 1. `setQuickbooksId` (staff only) sets or corrects it, never clears it, refuses a repeat or an ID another household has, and writes a `quickbooks_linked` line to the household's file saying what it was and what it is now. The household desk has a QuickBooks ID card (also on an ended household, because its last bill still needs the ID). A household without an ID blocks a month only if it has something to pay.
- **The database enforces** (migration `20261031000000_club_billing.sql`, tested against a real Postgres): the four new tables are only ever added to or voided (closed months and their lines can never change, and charges and visitor entries are never removed); a charge or visitor entry cannot be dated in, or voided after the close of, a closed month; a month is closed only in order; a run's invoice count and total must equal its lines (checked at the end of the change); a household's QuickBooks ID cannot change without a matching `quickbooks_linked` line in the same change, and cannot be cleared; amounts, kinds, counts and months have the right shape; who entered a visitor and how must agree (a member for the iPad, staff for the desk). Each rule was broken on purpose to prove a test notices.
- **The Postgres store** loads charges and visitors back to the earlier of its usual 90-day window and the start of the **first unbilled month** (so a late close never misses an old charge), all closed months, and the lines of the latest 24 closed months. `ClubState.dataSince` tells the engine which month it can no longer see; the plan refuses such a month in words.
- **Sample data** (`src/club/sample/billing.ts`): the invented households have invented QuickBooks IDs `SAMPLE-1001` and up, except Nakamura (joined this month), which has none, so the "no QuickBooks ID" refusal can be seen; a few invented bar and snack-bar charges, one credit and some visitor entries dated over the last weeks (September 2026 is the month that can be closed on the frozen test clock).
- **Not built, on purpose:** a live QuickBooks connection or sync; bar or snack-bar item lists (staff type an amount); visitor names; members seeing their own bill or charges; late fees or interest; a payment record; undoing a closed month (corrections are adjustments); credits in the QuickBooks file; sales tax in the QuickBooks file; billing per person instead of per household; tickets for waiting-list places; resignations in bulk (question AX).

- **Proof it can fail.** Step 9 was broken on purpose 261 ways and each run showed whether the tests noticed: the engine, the month plan and run, the money words and the two files (151), the iPad name search, the list views and the staff actions; the pages, downloads and iPad screens (34, built and run in a browser); the migration (65); and the Postgres store's mapping of all of it (11). 251 were caught in the end. 135 of the 151 engine breakages were caught at first and the 13 that were not got new tests, then were caught. The browser run missed 8: five got stronger tests (a billed visitor entry still offered "Void", the visitors link's words, a member whose membership ended reaching the visitor screen, the "I'm bringing N" button working with no number chosen, the search screen never returning to the start). The migration run missed 10: six got database tests (a charge or visitor entry entered already voided, an iPad entry by staff naming the member, an invoice number longer than QuickBooks takes, a line kind that does not exist, a quantity of nothing). The Postgres store run missed 3 (the tax flag of a saved line, and the store accepting a change to a closed month or a line) and all three got tests; its window tests also hold the "load every unbilled month" and "only the latest 24 months' lines" rules. **Ten breakages cannot change anything real, so they stay uncaught on purpose:** a free event giving a line (a price is never negative), the plan's fingerprint ignoring the amount (the amount is quantity times unit price, both are in it), the same month closed twice and a negative invoice count and a closed-months table emptied (each is also refused by a second rule: the order rule, the add-up rule and the line table's own guard), a member with a household that ended (the database already forbids active members in an ended household), any text as a month in the plain file's address (the lookup finds nothing anyway), any text as a visitor count (the engine refuses it), the loosened "area is off" guard in the server actions (the actor lookup is already empty there), and one breakage that was itself a no-op. Mistake caught on the way: I stopped a break-it run by killing it by name, which also ended my own shell and left one file broken; I restored it from the copy kept before each break and checked it against the committed version before going on. Lesson: never kill a runner by a pattern that is in the command doing the killing.

## Step 10 (first part): the archive of old iCrew bookings (built Oct 27; merged as PR 32)

Grant's answers (Oct 27): the report is **iCrew's Reservations report** (the same file the dashboard's importer already reads); Grant sent a real one so the layout could be read (**never copied into the project**; only its shape was looked at, and only invented files are used); **only 2026 for now**; the **Out and In columns are just the booked times**, so old bookings are kept as "reserved", never as "attended"; a name that matches no member is **imported anyway as unmatched**; and **each member sees their own old bookings** (staff see all). **The cutover tools half of step 10 is not scoped or built** (open question BI).

The building comparison: the old filing cabinet. When the club moves out of iCrew, its past bookings are boxed up and kept: a member can pull out their own folder and staff can look anyone up, but the cabinet is not today's calendar. Nothing in it can become a no-show, count against a limit, or land on an invoice.

- **What the real report looks like** (facts only, counted without printing any name): 14 columns, `Date, Day, StartTime, EndTime, BookedOnDate, BookedAtTime, ID, ItemType, Shell/Item, Oars, ReservedBy, Rower(s), OutAt, InAt`; dates `MM-DD-YYYY`, times `4:00pm`, booked-at `HH:MM:SS`. A year to date (Jan 1 to Oct 27, 2026) was 26,695 rows and 3.4 MB. Item types: Gym Space, Dock Space, Shell, Room Space, Paddle Board, Work Space, Fitness (cardio machines), Kayak, plus **events with a blank item type, one row per person signed up, all sharing one ID**. `Rower(s)` is `First Last (1),First Last (2)` with seat numbers and never more than two rowers in the data. About 128 `ReservedBy` values name two people with `&` or `/` ("Pat & Sam Example"); some carry a nickname in brackets. There is **no cancelled or no-show column**. 28 rows were entered after their date. Out and In differ from the booked times on most rows, but Grant says treat them as the booked times (open question BL).
- **A store of its own.** `HistoryStore` (`store/history.ts`, with `MemoryHistoryStore`; `store/history-postgres.ts`, with `PgHistoryStore`; `store/history-instance.ts`) is **not part of `ClubState`**, because a year is about 27,000 rows and every page reads the club's records on every visit. It is append-only and never touches bookings, the check-in record, billing or the writers lock (it has its own lock, so two imports of the same file add it once). With `CLUB_DATABASE_URL` unset it is in memory, with the invented sample loaded; with it set it is Postgres, and the sample loads only when `CCS_ALLOW_SAMPLE_DATA=1` and the archive is empty.
- **The reader** is `engine/icrew.ts` (`readIcrew`). It finds columns by name in any order (case, spaces and punctuation ignored), reports **every** problem at once with spreadsheet row numbers (the first 200, then "...and N more"), and keeps only days that have **passed** (today and later days are left out with a notice, because those bookings can still change in iCrew). Times become real Pacific moments (`pacificWall`), so Mar 8 and Nov 1, 2026 are exact; `12:00am` as an end time is the end of the day. A row with no `ItemType` is called `Event`. A booking is stored once however many files mention it: its key is a fingerprint of iCrew's ID plus the person it is for (an event shares one ID across people, so the person is part of the key). The template (`/api/club/history/template`) has three invented example rows that the reader refuses (`EXAMPLE-1`...). Limits: **4 MB** (just under Vercel's 4.5 MB request cap; `next.config.ts` raises the server-action body limit from 1 MB to 5 MB for this) and 60,000 rows; a bigger export is uploaded in pieces.
- **Names, not accounts.** iCrew has no emails, so a booking carries the names it was written with plus a **key** (`engine/people-names.ts`: lower case, no accents or punctuation, only the first and last word, so a middle name, "Jr." or a nickname in brackets does not stop a match). A joint booking names both people (the first borrows the last name written after the second). **Matching happens when someone asks, not when the file is added**, so a name that matched nobody can match later (after an email or name is corrected) and nothing goes stale. A key held by **more than one member (counting people who have left) matches nobody**: that member is told to ask staff, and staff can still find the bookings by name. Hyphenated and apostrophe names are kept whole; hyphenation that differs between iCrew and the member's record simply does not match, and the name shows on staff's list.
- **Rules are in the engine, not the page.** `engine/history.ts`: `planHistoryImport` (read only: rows to add, already stored, repeated, later days, date range, kinds, and how the names sort into one member / more than one / none), `runHistoryImport` (staff only; reads the file again, refuses a file with problems, refuses a file that is not the one that was checked by fingerprint, and refuses when the number of new rows differs from the preview), `memberHistory` (one member's bookings, newest first, 50 a time, in words; **other people's names are never shown to a member**, only "with 1 other"), `searchHistory` (staff, by the start of a name, three letters at least), `nameReport`.
- **The database enforces** (migration `20261101000000_club_icrew_history.sql`, tested against a real Postgres): all three tables only ever added to (no update, delete or truncate); a booking stored once; a booking's day is the **Pacific** day it starts on whatever time zone the connection uses; a booking ends after it starts; an import's counts must equal the bookings that came with it and the days they cover (a deferred check, run once per import so 27,000 rows do not each cost a count); the same file only once. Each rule was broken on purpose to prove a test notices.
- **Screens.** Staff: `/club/admin/history` (linked from the Staff office tools): what is stored and the files added so far, the blank template, the file box with **Check the file** (problems with row numbers, or the preview) and **Add**, a name search, and the lists of names that match no member and names shared by more than one member. The two buttons are one server action (`admin/history/actions.ts`, `importHistory`), staff only, "page not found" for anyone else and on the live site. Members: `/club/my-history` (a link from My bookings), a record only. Staff opening it are pointed to the staff page.
- **Sample data.** Nine invented old bookings (`sample/icrew.ts`) for Nora Abernathy (4, including a double with Tomas Vandermeer and an event), Erik Lindqvist, Tomas Vandermeer, Walter Pemberton, a joint booking and two names that match no member, dated back from "today".
- **Not built, on purpose:** the **cutover tools** (BI); bringing over reservations that are still in the future (BJ); using Out and In as proof of attendance (BL); earlier years (BK); editing, matching by hand or deleting stored bookings (a mistaken import is a conversation, not a button); showing members the crew's names; the old system's events as events; a QuickBooks or Brivo link; any use of real names in tests or in the project.
- **Proof it can fail:** step 10 was broken on purpose 126 ways and each run showed whether the tests noticed. **Engine, stores and migration (104):** name matching, the reader (every kind of mistake, the row numbers, the caps, both clock-change days), the plan and the run (fingerprint, the new-row count, staff only, nothing twice), member and staff views, both stores, and every database rule (append-only on all three tables, the Pacific day, the add-up check). 94 were caught at first. Of the 10 misses, 8 got a new test and were then caught (a mid-word accent, the edge of a rower list, the order of unmatched names, the three-letter search minimum, each table's delete-everything guard on its own, the add-up check, a person row with a different name), and 2 were code the database or another line already covered, so the extra code was removed instead of tested twice. **The server action (6):** all caught. **Pages, template, upload limit and the live-site checks (16):** 12 caught in the end, 2 would not compile because the type checker already forbids them (staff reaching the member code, nobody signed in reaching `actor.kind`), and 2 cannot change anything real (the "is the member area on?" line in the server action and in the template: `getCurrentActor()` already answers nobody when the area is off, so the next check gives the same "not found"; the same finding as steps 7 and 9). One real miss became a test: a signed-out visitor to My old bookings could get any page and the test still passed, because it looked for a "Sign in" link and the menu shows one on every page; it now looks for the prompt's own sentence on both pages.

## Resignations: ending memberships from a file (built Oct 27; merged as PR 34)

Grant's answers (Oct 27): resignations (open question AX) came next, ahead of the real email sender and the cutover tools; **6 to 20 households resign in a typical month**, so a bulk tool is worth building; the list is **a file staff upload** (upload, read-back, confirm, the pattern of the roster import); and a resignation **takes effect right away, when staff confirm**. Not chosen, so not built: a "who's missing" report that compares a fresh QuickBooks list to the club and ends nothing; ending on a date staff pick; entering a resignation dated to a past day. In the same round Grant answered BL: Out and In stay as the booked times.

The building comparison: move-out day. The office gets a list of units whose tenants have given notice (from QuickBooks, from the board, from wherever staff trust). The clerk first gets a read-back ("9 leases would end today; 23 bookings, class places and RSVPs would be cancelled; row 4 names a unit that does not exist"), and only when the list is clean does the clerk end them all at once, each exactly as at the front desk. Nobody is added or changed, and a lease that already ended is skipped.

- **The file.** A CSV, one row per household. Columns, found by their names in any order, spaces and capitals ignored: **QuickBooks ID** (required), **Household name** (optional but strongly recommended) and **Note** (optional: why they left, kept in the household's file). Extra columns are ignored and mentioned. Dates in the file are not used: households end the day staff press the button, and the page and the read-back say so. The blank template (`/api/club/resignations/template`, staff only) holds two invented example rows, and the tool refuses them (`EXAMPLE-1`, `EXAMPLE-2`). Limits: 200 KB and 1,000 rows. An Excel file, a file with a stray binary character, an empty file and a quotation mark that is never closed are each refused in words.
- **The reader** is `engine/resignations.ts` (`readResignations`) over `engine/csv.ts`. It changes nothing and reports **every** problem at once, with row numbers as a spreadsheet shows them (the header is row 1): an empty ID, an ID over 100 characters, a template example row, a name over 120 characters, a note over 200, and a QuickBooks ID listed twice. A completely empty row is skipped.
- **The read-back** is `planResignations(state, text, now, rules)`, which changes nothing. **Problems** (nothing can be ended while there is one): everything the reader found; a QuickBooks ID no household has (with the hint that a spreadsheet can drop a leading zero and that a household with no ID is ended in the household desk); a Household name that is not the name on file (compared ignoring capitals and spacing), so a mistyped ID can never end the wrong household; and more households than `maxHouseholdsPerFile` (households that are skipped do not count). A household that has **already ended is skipped** and listed. **Notices** (they do not stop it): ignored columns; no Household name column; a file that would end `warnPercentOfActive` percent or more of the active households ("if you meant to upload the whole roster, stop here"); and a standing note about dues (the file's dates are not used, the households end today, no dues are billed from the next month on, and months already started are billed as usual when closed along with anything not yet billed). What ending them **cancels** is worked out for **all the leaving adults together** (`departurePreview`), so a boat booked by one resigning household with crew from another is cancelled once and the read-back names exactly what the run then cancels.
- **The run** is `runResignations(store, ledger, ctx, text, expect?, rules?)`: staff only, and one all-or-nothing change under the writers lock. It reads the file again inside the change, refuses a file with problems, refuses when there is nothing to end ("Every household in the file has already ended, so there is nothing to do."), and refuses when the file or the club no longer matches what staff previewed (a fingerprint of the exact file plus the household and adult counts they saw). Then it ends each household in file order through `endHouseholdRecords`, the same code the household desk uses (`endHousehold` is now a thin wrapper around it, so there is one way to end a household): the adults are blocked and signed out, future bookings, class places and event RSVPs are cancelled, the waiting lists move up behind them (also across households that are both leaving), and the household's file gets its lines. The `membership_ended` line reads "Membership ended on Oct 28, 2026, from a resignations file. 3 adults can no longer book. Note: Moved away", with the source and note in its details. Two staff pressing confirm at once end the households once. A failure part-way ends none of them. A test holds the result to ending the same households one at a time: the same records, on both stores.
- **Rules are data.** `src/club/settings/resignation-rules.json` (placeholders): `maxHouseholdsPerFile` 50 and `warnPercentOfActive` 25. `parseResignationRules` refuses a typo, a missing key and a silly number in words. They only guard against uploading the wrong file (for example the whole roster); they do not decide who resigns.
- **No migration.** Ending a household already goes through the database's existing rules (the household's file lines, the adults, the bookings cancelled by status), so there is nothing new for the database to enforce.
- **The screen.** `/club/admin/resignations` (a "Resignations" link in the Staff office's tools), staff only (a member gets "page not found", someone signed out is asked to sign in): instructions, a file box, **Check the file** (the problems with their rows, or the read-back with a card for each household, the notices, "What it will cancel" using the household desk's own preview view limited to 50 lines a list, and the households skipped because they had already ended) and a red **Yes, end N memberships** button that only appears for a clean read-back. The two buttons are one server action (`endHouseholds`), which checks the member area is on and the person is staff before reading the file, and refuses an end that does not carry the fingerprint and the counts of a check (plain digits only). Changing the file clears the read-back. After ending, the page says how many households and adults, and how many bookings, class places and event RSVPs were cancelled.
- **Hidden like everything in `/club`.** The page, the template and the action are "page not found" on the live site; `club-hidden.spec.ts` covers all three (the action called with its real ID from the build).
- **Not built, on purpose:** a "who's missing" report; ending on a chosen future date (the system would have to end memberships by itself on that day); back-dating a resignation; undoing an ending (a household that comes back is a new household, question A); telling the household or the board (no email sender yet); changing anything but ending.

- **Proof it can fail:** this step was broken on purpose 96 ways and each run showed whether the tests noticed: the file reader (20), the read-back (19), the run (14), the shared ending code (8), the rules checker (5), the server action (9), the screen, its template and the Staff office link (11), the shared preview component (7) and the live-site checks (3). 91 were caught the first time. Two needed stronger tests: nothing in the browser checked the "Nothing was booked... so nothing was cancelled" line in the done message, and the preview test's second list was too short for the line limit to matter (the other lists had the same blind spot, so three more breakages were added for them and caught at once). Two live-site breakages (the page's own member-area check, and the new action missing from the live-site list) first "passed" for the wrong reason: the live-site test was itself failing with nothing broken, because it forbade the bare word "resignation", which is in the page's own address in the "not found" response. It now forbids only words that appear in the page's content, and both are caught on a valid run. **One breakage cannot change anything real and stays uncaught on purpose:** the template's own "is the member area on?" line (`getCurrentActor()` already answers nobody when the area is off; the same finding as steps 7, 9 and 10). Ending the households in reverse order was left out as it gives the same records. The browser run also found a real flaw that the unit tests had missed: the file reader's mistakes and the read-back's were listed one group after the other, so row 5 came before rows 2 and 3; they are now sorted by row, with a problem about the whole file last.

## The real email sender (built Oct 27; merged as PR 36)

Grant's answers (Oct 27): the real email sender (open question AI) came next, ahead of the cutover tools. **Replies go to the club's public membership address**, so a member who did not get their link can write to a person. The sender is proved before any member uses it with **a one-line test command** (the member area is hidden on the live site until switch-on, so nothing else can try it). Service: Resend, chosen Oct 25. Nothing is bought and no Resend account exists yet; the sender is built and tested against a pretend Resend, so it does not wait for the account.

The building comparison: the mailroom. Until now the lobby had a pretend mail slot (the test outbox) that only showed letters on a screen. This is the real mail carrier, hired through Resend, who delivers one plain letter to one person at a time. The rule that matters most: the carrier is never called while the building is in "model apartment" mode (any test build), even if the carrier's number is written on the wall.

- **Who sends** (`getMailer` in `auth/mail.ts`): where invented data is allowed (development, previews, automated tests) it is **always the test outbox**, even when Resend's settings are present, so a test build can never email anyone. Otherwise, where the member area is switched on for real members (`clubEnabled`) and the settings are complete, it is **Resend**. Anywhere else it is nobody: the sign-in page says email sign-in is not switched on and no link is made. Today `clubEnabled` follows `sampleDataAllowed`, so on the live site the real sender is unreachable until the deliberate switch-on change (question D); `getMailer` takes the two switches as an argument only so a test can try the "area on, invented data off" case that no setting reaches yet.
- **Private settings** (`auth/mail-settings.ts`; Vercel's private settings or `.env.local`, never code): `RESEND_API_KEY` (starts with `re_`); `CLUB_MAIL_FROM` (required with a key: an address on the domain verified in Resend, alone or as `College Club Seattle <no-reply@example.org>`, checked with the sign-in's own address test; a name cannot hold quotation marks, backslashes, @ signs or line breaks, so a setting cannot add a header); `CLUB_MAIL_REPLY_TO` (optional: unset means the public membership address, `DEFAULT_REPLY_TO`, which a test holds equal to the address in `site.json`; an address replaces it; the word `none` switches replies off). No key means no real sender. A key with a missing or wrong companion is **refused in words** (`MailSettingsError`), the sender then stays off, and the words are logged once per server (never on every page view). **No message, log line or error ever holds the key.**
- **The sender** (`auth/resend.ts`, `createResendMailer`): one POST to `https://api.resend.com/emails` per email with the key as a bearer token, the From line, one recipient, the subject and the **plain text** (no HTML) and `reply_to` when replies are on. It gives up after 10 seconds. A failure (no answer, a refusal, an unreachable service) throws a short message with Resend's status and reason, with the key and the recipient's address scrubbed out because messages end up in logs. The sign-in treats a failure like it always has: it looks the same from outside (`"ignored"`), and the log line is the only trace.
- **The test command** (`auth/send-test-email.ts`): `npm run mail:test -- you@example.org` sends exactly one test email through the same sender code, reading the same three settings from the environment or `.env.local`. It prints where the email went, what the From line was and where replies go, or why it could not send (a missing key, a wrong setting, Resend's own refusal with the usual causes: the domain not verified, a From address off that domain, a key from another account). It exits 0 sent, 1 could not send, 2 used wrongly. To let Node run these TypeScript files directly, `tsconfig.json` now has `allowImportingTsExtensions` (no effect on the build, which type-checks only) and the files this command loads import each other with their `.ts` endings. The file runs the command only when it is the program being run, never when a test or the website loads it.
- **Go-live items (not code):** create the Resend account under the CCS-owned shared email (question E); the club's domain is held by someone else (Grant, Oct 25), so the DNS records Resend gives (sender verification and anti-spoofing records) go to them; choose the From address; **switch off link and open tracking for the domain in Resend**, because a tracked link goes through Resend's servers first and the one-time secret is in the address it rewrites (the test command says so too); set the three settings and `CLUB_SITE_URL` in Vercel's Production settings only; run `npm run mail:test` to a staff address and check the spam folder; and plan for Resend's Pro plan around cutover, when everyone signs in at once (the free plan stops at 100 emails a day).
- **Not built, on purpose:** any email other than the sign-in link (waiting-list move-ups and event changes N, T and X; a note to the person whose email address was changed AK; a note to a household whose membership ended); retries or a queue; bounce, complaint or delivery tracking; HTML emails or the club's branding in them; a check that the domain is verified (Resend says so at send time); a staff alert when sends start failing (question BP); and anything on the live site: no new page or address exists, so nothing here can be seen from outside.
- **Proof it can fail:** this was broken on purpose 56 ways and every one was caught the first time (the checks were run unbroken first and passed): the settings checker (20: no key not meaning "no sender", a loosened key shape and length, a missing From not refused in its own words, the default reply address drifting from the public page, the word `none`, a blank reply setting, an unchecked reply or From address, the name limit loosened or off by one, control characters, quotation marks and @ signs allowed in a name, brackets with no name, text after the closing bracket, untrimmed values, a message that repeats the key), the Resend sender (20: wrong address, method, authorization scheme or user agent, the recipient not a list, `reply_to` always sent or misnamed, HTML sent too, the key added to the body, the From line ignored, no timeout or a ten-times-longer one, a refusal or a redirect counted as sent, the key or the address not scrubbed from a message, a long reason not cut, a timeout not recognised, Resend's reason dropped), the chooser (7: the test outbox no longer winning in a test build, the area switch ignored, either default switch forced, a bad setting logged every time, the real sender never made) and the test command (9: the command never running, wrong exit codes, two addresses accepted, an address not checked, a missing key not stopped, sending to the wrong address, a wrong success line, the causes line on the wrong stream). **Two things were not done and are said plainly.** A browser-level breakage of "the test outbox wins in a test build" was not run, because it would have sent a real request to Resend's servers (with a fake key); the unit tests catch that breakage, and the browser tests carry fake Resend settings on both member-area servers (in-memory and Postgres), so every sign-in browser test would fail if a test build ever chose Resend over the outbox. And **while trying the test command by hand with a fake key, one real request did reach Resend's servers and was refused (401, "API key is invalid"); nothing was sent.** Every test since uses a pretend Resend (an injected or stubbed `fetch`), and one test holds that: a stub put in after the sender was made still catches the call.

## Public site refresh: the design handoff (Oct 29; merged as PR 37 on Oct 30)

Grant (Oct 29) paused the email sender and handed over a design bundle (a README, a design board and three divider drawings) for **two screens**: a public homepage built to move visitors to "Request a tour", and a member sign-in screen. He gave six steps and asked to check in after step 2 (PR 37 first held steps 1 and 2, the findings and the design tokens, and went on to carry the whole refresh). He then answered four questions and said to keep going, so steps 3 to 6 were built, plus the real Request a tour form he chose. His rules: one H1 a page, alt text on every image, 44 pixel tap targets, no sideways scroll at 390 pixels, AA text contrast; the hero photo is a clearly labelled placeholder; "How to join" steps 1 and 2 are placeholder copy and carry a TODO; the public-page rule in "Safety rules" above.

The building comparison: a new coat of paint and new lobby fixtures. The paint chart (the tokens) lives where every part of the building already reads its colours from. The fixtures (header, footer, sticky bar, buttons) are new and prefixed `ds-` so they can never touch the dashboard's or the older pages' fixtures (which share a class name, `.site-header`, with each other). The homepage and the sign-in are the two rooms they dress. The Request a tour card is the desk's new card box.

**Grant's answers (Oct 29):** (BS) build the homepage as the home of `/site` first, not at `/`: it is hidden on the live site like the member area, so the dashboard and every board link are untouched, and it moves to `/` at cutover as one deliberate change with the dashboard's new address (AT, D). (BT) **leave the iCrew card off the sign-in screen** (this system replaces iCrew, and no iCrew address was given). (BU) the hero's small label reads **"A Seattle institution since 1910"**, not the designer's "Member-owned since 1910" (the club's own words say member-owned since 2013). (BV) **Request a tour is a real form**: requests are **saved and shown to staff on a Tour requests screen**, and also emailed to the membership address once real email is switched on.

**The approved design** is Turn 3 of the board: 3a the homepage at 390 pixels wide, 3b the sign-in. The board was opened in a browser and looked at; nothing is copied from its HTML. Both screens were also looked at as built, at 390, 768, 1180 pixels and more, in screenshots.

**Step 2, the tokens** (PR 37): `src/app/globals.css` `:root` gained, with nothing existing changed: `--sun` #F2C14E, `--sun-hover` #E5B23F, `--sun-tint` #FBEBC2, `--waterloo-dark` #435661, `--paper-on-dark` #F5F3EC, `--white`, `--error` #8A2B1A, `--ui` (Figtree), the radii (pill 999, input 12, card 16, large card 20), `--shadow-xs` and `--shadow-md` (the two soft shadows the app already used, now named), `--ease` and two durations, and the handoff's spacing (4 pixel unit, 20 and 24 pixel side padding, the gaps and section paddings, the 980 pixel container, the 44 pixel tap minimum). The handoff's other colours were already in the file with identical values. Figtree 400, 600 and 800 are self-hosted like the other fonts (`@fontsource/figtree`), not loaded from Google as the README said. `theme-tokens.test.ts` holds every value, the old values and the contrast of every pair the design uses.

**Step 3, the shared pieces** (`src/components/ds/`, all classes `ds-`, one stylesheet `ds.css` imported by the public layout and the sign-in layout): the Sun pill button and the outline pill (`ButtonLink`), the eyebrow, the **lane line** and **long swell** dividers (the approved drawings, copied unchanged to `public/ds/`, drawn as repeating backgrounds, hidden from screen readers), the **sticky header** (`SiteHeader`, 64 pixels in all with its border: logo, the Member login pill at 44 pixels tap size around a 40 pixel ring, and a Menu button; on a phone the menu is a panel under the header with Member login again at the bottom; the page behind stops scrolling, Escape closes it and gives the focus back, Tab stays inside the header, choosing a page or tapping the dimmed page closes it, and turning the screen wide closes it; from 900 pixels the pages sit inline), the dark **footer** (`SiteFooter`), and the phone-only **sticky Request a tour bar** (`StickyCta`: slides away while the headline's own button is on screen, using an `IntersectionObserver`; gone on the tour page; hidden from 768 pixels; the footer leaves room for it). `Logo` shows the club's name in the heading font until the logo files exist (switch: `src/site/brand-assets.ts`, which a test holds to the files in `public/`).

**Step 4a, the homepage** (`src/app/site/Homepage.tsx`; the page shows at `/site`): the hero (460 pixels on a phone, 640 from 768 with the words in a column of at most 560 and the two buttons side by side), "What you get" (three items, three columns from 768), the lane line, "How to join" (three steps with Sun numerals, three columns from 768, `#how-to-join`, with a visible preview note saying steps 1 and 2 are the designer's placeholder wording and a `TODO(club)` in the code), the wave, and the footer. **The words are data**: `site.json` now has `homepage` (hero, features, join) and `footer`, read by the strict `parseSiteContent` (exactly three items and three steps, lengths capped, typos refused in words). The hero photo is `HERO_PHOTO` in `src/site/hero-photo.ts` (null: a dashed, labelled placeholder with an `aria-label`; set it to `{ src, alt }` and the page draws it full-bleed with the dark fade). The menu is now Home, About, **Our Programs**, Membership, **Request a Tour**, Contact (`NAV` in `content.ts`; `home` is no longer one of the `pages`, it is `HOME_PATH`; the programs page keeps its heading "Rowing and fitness"). The club's phone number is written **(206) 622-0624** everywhere (the old `206-622-0624` form is refused by the reader), and every public page is checked to show no other. **The bar, drinks and wine words came out of the older public pages** ("a full bar!", "Toast bar with a full menu of light bites and beverages", "wine tastings, paint and sip"), by Grant's rule. The older pages (About, Our Programs, Membership, Contact) keep their roomier look inside the new header and footer. The footer has no hours or email (the design's footer has neither); the Contact page still does.

**Request a tour** (`/site/request-a-tour`, the public form, and `/club/admin/tours`, the staff list): the visitor gives a name and an email, and a phone and a note if they like. Every problem is named next to its box and what they typed stays; a robot box is invisible. A good request is **saved first** (`club.tour_requests`, migration `20261102000000_club_tour_requests.sql`: never removed, changed only by marking it followed up, once, with who and when; email lower case; lengths capped; tested against a real Postgres), then the membership office is emailed best-effort through the club's sender (`getMailer()`: the test outbox in test builds; Resend once switched on; to `CLUB_TOUR_NOTIFY_TO` or the public membership address). A failed email is logged in the sender's own scrubbed words and never loses or breaks the request. `engine/tours.ts` holds the cleaning (`cleanTourRequest`: control characters out, names and notes length-capped, phone 7 to 15 digits), the limits (`tour-rules.json`, placeholders: one address 3 a day, the site 30 an hour, the staff list 100 at a time; counted and saved in one step under a lock, so ten requests together save exactly three), and the staff functions (`listTourRequests`, `markTourFollowedUp`, staff only). A repeat past the daily limit and a robot are both thanked and not saved, so neither learns it was refused; when the whole site is busy the person is asked to phone. The staff screen is linked from the Staff office tools; it shows newest first, how many are still to follow up, the person's email and phone as links, and "Mark followed up" once. The form is a Next.js server action (`requestTour`, listed in `club-hidden.spec.ts` with its real ID like the staff actions) that answers "page not found" where the public site is off.

**Step 4b, the sign-in screen** (`src/app/club/(entry)/sign-in/`, own layout, no member menu, banner or "acting as" line, like the front-desk iPad; the link page `/club/sign-in/[token]` and everything after sign-in stay in `(member)`): the dark band (club name, "Members" in Sun, "Welcome back." in the heading font at 32 pixels, "Your calendar, classes, account and the Weekly Watercooler, all in one place."), then one **Email** box and a full-width Sun **"Email me a sign-in link"** button. **There is no password** (sign-in is an email link), so the design's password box and "Forgot password?" are not there; "First time here?" opens a note that the club office sets up each household, with the membership email and the main phone number. The button turns to "Sending…" while the request is on its way. Then the lane line, the **home screen tip** (`HomeScreenCard`: Oyster card, "Keep the club on your home screen"; shown only on an iPhone or iPad browser that is not already open from the home screen, as the design's words; where the browser offers to install the site it is an Install button instead, and it goes away once installed; nothing on a desktop), and "← Back to collegeclubseattle.com" (`PUBLIC_SITE_URL`, the club's current public site). All the earlier sign-in behaviour is kept (the same-for-everyone "a link is on its way" answer, the notices, the test-build outbox note and test-member link, the signed-in view with "Go to the club" and Sign out). Form field edges are **Waterloo** (5.6 to 1), not the handoff's Debonair (2.7 to 1, under the 3 to 1 a field's edge needs).

**Step 5, the member app's address:** every Member login link (header pill, phone menu, footer) uses `MEMBER_APP_URL` (`src/site/links.ts`), from `NEXT_PUBLIC_MEMBER_APP_URL`. With nothing set it is `/club/sign-in` on this same site; set it to, for example, `https://members.collegeclubseattle.com` in Vercel and redeploy and every link follows. Only `https://` addresses are accepted (`http://localhost` for development); anything else, including addresses with a user name and password in them, is ignored with a log line. It is only the link: pointing that address at this app is a Vercel and domain change for whoever holds the domain, which needs Grant's say-so first (question E). It is a setting, not content, because the site's words may not link to another website.

**Step 6, the member app's manifest and icons** (`src/club/pwa.tsx`): this version of Next.js allows one manifest at the very top of the app, which would apply to every page, so the manifest is a route at `/club/manifest.webmanifest` and only the member pages and the sign-in screen point at it (with the iPhone home-screen icon and name, the phone's top-bar colour and "capable" flag). It says name "College Club Seattle", short name "CCS Members", `display: standalone`, theme #44484D, background #FBFAF6, `start_url` and `scope` `/club`, icons at 192 and 512 pixels (plus a maskable 512) from `/club/pwa-icon/{180,192,512}` (real PNGs drawn by `ImageResponse`: the white logo on a Cyberspace square, or the letters **CCS** in white until the logo files exist). The dashboard, the public site and the front-desk iPad have none of these. Everything is "page not found" where the member area is off.

**What is still needed from Grant** (question BW): the two logo files (`ccs-logo-black.png`, `ccs-logo-white.png`; they arrived as pictures in the chat, not files; put both in `public/` and set `LOGOS_AVAILABLE`), the real hero photo (`HERO_PHOTO`), and the real "How to join" steps 1 and 2 (confirm the club's process, edit `homepage.join`, remove its `previewNote`). Until then the header shows the club's name as a stand-in, the hero a labelled placeholder and the icons the letters CCS.

**Assumed, for Grant to confirm** (the handoff or the answers did not say): the inline menu starts at 900 pixels, not 768 (six links and the pill do not fit one line before that; 768 to 899 keeps the Menu button); the desktop headline is 46 pixels and its lead 18 (the handoff gives only the phone sizes); the Menu button reads "Close" while open; the sticky bar is also on the older pages and hidden on the tour page; the preview banner stays above the sticky header; the footer's phone number is a 44 pixel tap target; the stand-in wordmark wraps to two lines; the "First time here?" wording; "Send request" and the tour page's words; the tour limits (3 a day, 30 an hour); the notice email's wording; the tour form carries no privacy promise (BX); no analytics (the handoff recommends them; buttons carry plain `data-track` names only); the sign-in's Weekly Watercooler line is the designer's (BZ).

**Not built, on purpose:** analytics; a CAPTCHA (the robot box and the limits are the only guards); an email back to the visitor; deleting or exporting tour requests; a staff alert when the notice email fails; the `members.` subdomain itself; the iCrew card; real logos, photo and steps (above).

- **Proof it can fail:** the new pieces were broken on purpose 132 ways, plus one more to check a tightened test, and each run showed whether the tests noticed. **Unit and database level (96):** the homepage and footer words reader (14), the logo and hero-photo switches (2), the tour request engine (21: cleaning, limits, the saved-first order, the notice email, staff only, followed up once), the tour stores (14, both memory and Postgres), the tour limits file checker (4), the migration (18: every shape rule, the one-way "followed up", never removed), the form's server actions (4), the member app address (7) and the manifest and icons (12). 92 were caught the first time. Three misses got new tests and were then caught (the homepage "How to join" note's 400 character limit, a step's 200 character limit, and the database letting the id change while a card is marked followed up). **One cannot change anything real and stays uncaught on purpose:** widening the table's `status` check to allow a third word, because the completeness rule beside it also refuses any status that is not `new` or `followed_up`. **Browser level (36, each with a rebuild):** the phone menu (10: Escape, scroll lock, focus in, Tab wrapping, turning wide, choosing the current page, the dimmed page, Member login, "Close", the screen-reader state), the sticky bar (3 and the page that watches it, 1), the footer's Member login, "How to join", the lane line hidden from screen readers, five stylesheet rules (header sticks, hero height, dimming under the header, bar hidden on wide screens, field edge strength), the layout (no bar, banner words, the manifest link), the sign-in button's "Sending…", the home screen tip (installed app, desktop), the robot box and the browser's own checks on the tour form, the staff list's line breaks, and the live site (manifest, icons, staff page, tour page and the form action each must be "page not found"). All 36 were caught. A 37th, moving the open menu one pixel, was run after the menu-position test was tightened and was also caught. **One baseline failure, said plainly:** the first browser command (breakages U01 to U23) failed once when run unbroken, in "the menu sits right under the header, whatever the page has scrolled to". I first misread that as an error belonging to no test (the script kept only the last four lines of the report); the report's closing note "1 error was not a part of any test" appears on every run stopped early, broken or not. The test then passed 40 of 40 on its own, 120 of 120 under ten workers on four cores, and nine clean runs of the whole command, so the cause was not found. Because it measured two boxes in separate steps after a fixed 150 millisecond wait for the scroll, it now waits for the scroll to have happened and reads all three boxes in one step; it still catches the dimming-covers-the-header and one-pixel-off breakages. Because the unbroken baseline had once failed, breakages U01 to U23 were replayed recording which test failed and why: all 23 fail in a test about what was broken, with an assertion that names it.

## Equipment inventory: what the iCrew report holds and what is decided (Oct 30)

Grant (Oct 30) asked whether an inventory of rowing shells, kayaks and the other reservable items had been pulled from the iCrew exports, or still needed to be sent. **It had not been pulled.** The items in the system are invented (`src/club/sample/equipment.ts`: bird-named singles, doubles and a quad, three kayaks, two paddle boards, three dock slips, a gym and a room). The real Reservations report was read for layout and counts only, under the fake-data rule.

The building comparison: the unit list. The booking history shows which units were ever rented, but not the floor plan: it cannot say how many bedrooms a unit has, which are off the market for repairs, or which belong to a tenant. Staff have to confirm those.

- **What the 2026 report holds** (Jan 1 to Oct 27; counts only, no names written here): **82 shells** (56 with an owner-style name, a number-dash-number plus an owner's surname, so probably privately owned; 26 club-style names), **23 kayaks** (6 owner-style), **15 paddle boards** (1 owner-style), **60 dock spaces**, **46 club spaces** (numbered to 49), **8 gym spaces**, **4 work spaces** (named rooms) and **8 cardio machines**. Eleven shells and three kayaks have not been booked since before August (maybe retired).
- **What it does not hold:** seat counts, skill levels, in-service status, who owns a private boat, and anything nobody booked in 2026. Some names are messy: generic ones ("Double", "Private Double") and three "Sykes" names that may or may not be separate boats.
- **Numbered spaces look like numbered tickets.** Bookings fall steadily as the number rises (the lowest free number is handed out), so dock 1 to 60, club spaces 1 to 49 and gym 1 to 8 are probably a number of places, not that many separate items.
- **Grant's answers (Oct 30):** (1) the real list comes from **a starter sheet plus an upload screen** (the sheet was made and sent to Grant the same day, outside the project because it holds owner-style names; the upload screen is built the same day, see "The equipment upload" below); (2) dock, club room and gym spaces are **one pool per kind with a number of places** (60, 49 and 8 from the highest numbers seen, to be confirmed); (3) **cardio machines are left out** (the system has no Cardio type and Grant did not want one).
- **Pools were built the same day, after the equipment upload and private boats** (see "Pools" below). Until then an item held one booking at a time and the database refused an overlap; a pool needed "fewer overlapping bookings than places", enforced in the database a different way (as the class chairs are), plus a day view that says how many places are left.
- **A slip to remember:** while checking, I printed the full list of equipment names to the terminal, and the owner-style names include members' surnames. The rule for that file is to print no names. Nothing was written into the project and nothing was shared except the sheet to Grant, but the names are in that session's log. From then on only counts were used. **Never print a name column from the iCrew report; count or classify it instead.**
- **Also decided (Oct 30):** a privately owned boat is bookable by **its owners plus the people they name** (question CC; **built the same day**, see "Private boats"), and the **upload screen is built first**, pools after.
- **Not built when this was written, on purpose:** pools and owner access for private boats (both built later the same day), a Cardio type, and any real equipment name in the project or its tests.

### The equipment upload (built Oct 30; merged as PR 38)

The building comparison: the unit list. The office loads the building's units from a sheet the manager prepared. The clerk first gets a read-back ("6 units would be added; 1 is marked off the market; 3 rows wait for later steps"), and only when it is clean does the clerk add them. Nothing already on the list is ever changed.

- **The sheet.** A CSV. Columns, found by name in any order, spaces and capitals ignored: **Name, Type, Seats, Owned by** (required) and **Skill level needed, In service, Places, Notes, Owner emails** (optional; the last is for Private boats, see "Private boats"). A column whose name starts with "Hint" (the starter sheet has four) is ignored without comment; any other extra column is mentioned. The blank template (`/api/club/equipment/template`, staff only) holds four invented example rows (`EXAMPLE-1` to `EXAMPLE-4`) that the upload refuses. Limits: 200 KB and 1,000 rows. An Excel file, a binary file, an empty file and an unclosed quotation mark are each refused in words.
- **What a row means.** Type is Shell, Kayak, Paddle Board or Work Space (individual items), or Dock Space, Room Space or Gym Space (pools). **Seats** picks the class: a shell is 1, 2 or 4 (single, double, quad or four; required), a kayak 1 or 2 (required; 2 is the new `kayak-double` class), a paddle board or work space empty or 1 (a work space is the new `work-space` class). **Skill level needed:** empty uses the class's usual level, None (or Anyone) means no level, or a qualification's code or name (today's placeholder levels, question B). **Owned by** is Club or Private. **In service:** Yes or empty, or No: a No item is added **out of service from now, with its Notes as the reason** (or a plain default reason). A Fitness (cardio) row is refused in words.
- **Left out, and listed in the read-back with the reason:** the three **pools** (one pool of places each, a later step; their Places are checked, 1 to 500, and not used). Nothing disappears silently. (**Superseded the same night:** rows marked **Private** were first left out too, because who may book them was not built yet. They are now loaded as private boats, see "Private boats". Pool rows are now loaded as pools too, see "Pools"; the template has a fifth example row for one.)
- **The reader** (`readEquipment`) changes nothing and reports every problem at once, with row numbers as a spreadsheet shows them (the header is row 1): an empty or over-long name or one with a character that cannot be saved, a template example row, an unknown type, seats that do not fit the type, an unknown skill level, an empty or unknown owner, an in-service word that is not Yes or No, over-long notes, a pool's bad place count, and a name listed twice (compared ignoring capitals and spacing). A missing required column is one problem for the file, not one per row.
- **The plan and the run** are `planEquipment` (changes nothing; also lists the items, the ones already here and the pools, and a fingerprint of the exact file) and `runEquipment` (staff only; one all-or-nothing change under the store's lock). The run reads the file again, refuses a file with problems, refuses when nothing is new, and refuses when the file or the club no longer matches what was previewed (the fingerprint and the number of items). An item whose name is already in the club (compared ignoring capitals and spacing) is **left alone**: a second upload never changes or removes anything. Two staff pressing confirm at once add the items once.
- **Warnings that do not stop it:** columns ignored; a row marked Club whose sheet hint says the name looks like a private boat (so a private boat is not loaded as a club boat by forgetting to mark it); items already here; items that will start out of service.
- **No migration.** Items already save through the Postgres store, and the two new classes are rules data that `syncRules` mirrors (the table already allowed Work Space).
- **The screen.** `/club/admin/equipment-import` ("Load the equipment list" in the Staff office tools), staff only (a member gets "page not found", someone signed out is asked to sign in): instructions, a file box, **Check the file** (the problems with their rows, or the read-back with a card for each item, the notices, and the lists of items already here, private boats and pools), and **Yes, add N items**, which only appears when there is something to add. The two buttons are one server action (`loadEquipment`), which checks the member area is on and the person is staff before reading the file, and refuses an add that does not carry the fingerprint and count of a check. Changing the file clears the read-back.
- **Hidden like everything in `/club`:** the page, the template and the action are "page not found" on the live site; `club-hidden.spec.ts` covers all three (the action called with its real ID from the build).
- **Not built, on purpose:** editing or retiring an item that is already here (a mistake in a first upload is fixed through a tool that does not exist yet, question CF); a Cardio type; a record of who loaded which items.
- **Proof it can fail:** the new code was broken on purpose 86 ways and each run showed whether the tests noticed. **Unit level (73):** the reader (name, type, seats, skill, owner, in-service, places, duplicate and file-format rules, 35), the plan (what is left out, matched by name, the notices, 13), the run (staff only, problems, nothing to add, fingerprint and count, skill and out-of-service saving, 13), the server action (10) and the two new classes (2). 67 were caught the first time. Six misses each got an assertion and were then caught: a control character in a name, a place count over the limit, a two-seat work space, the already-here notice, the out-of-service notice, and which staff member an out-of-service entry is recorded under. **Browser level (13, each with a rebuild):** the page's staff and live-site checks, the template's staff check, the fingerprint, a new file clearing the old read-back, the 50-item list limit, the out-of-service note, the private, pool and already-here lists, the Add button, and the Staff office link. 11 were caught the first time, **one would not compile** (the page reaching the staff check with nobody signed in: the type checker already forbids it), and one miss (the Add button offered with nothing to add) got a test and was then caught. One removed piece of code is worth saying: the plan once re-sorted the problems by row, which could never change anything because the reader already lists them in row order, so it was deleted instead of tested.

## Private boats (built Oct 30; merged as PR 38)

Grant's answers (Oct 30), to seven questions: (1) an **owner** is one or more **members picked by staff** from the member list; (2) the equipment sheet gets an **Owner emails column and** a screen, **both** (the column is optional, and a boat left blank is fixed on the screen); (3) when a boat's owner leaves the club, **the boat locks until staff step in**; (4) on a private double or quad, **only owners and invited people** may be in the boat (the crew too); (5) an **owner invites on a My boats page and it works at once**; (6) **the same booking limits apply, but the skill-level check is skipped** (the owner vouches); (7) a private boat is **hidden from everyone not on its list** (staff see all).

The building comparison: a reserved parking spot. The office paints the owners' names on it, an owner can hand out a visitor pass, and everyone else never sees the spot on the lot map. If the last owner moves out the spot is chained shut until the office sorts it out. Every name painted on or scrubbed off is kept, so the list doubles as the record of who was let on.

- **Pieces.** An `Item` can be `isPrivate` (set when it is made, never changed; the database refuses a change). A **list line** (`BoatAccess`) says this member may use this boat, as `owner` (named by staff) or `invited` (named by an owner, or by staff), who added it and when, and, once, who ended it, when and why. Lines are only added and ended, never removed or edited; a person is on one boat's list once at a time (a second line only after the first ended). `ClubState.boatAccess` holds them.
- **The engine** is `engine/boat-access.ts`: `activeAccess`, `accessKind`, `activeOwners` (owners who can still use the boat: an active member of an active household), `boatLocked` (a private boat with no such owner), `itemVisibleTo`; `inviteToBoat` (an active owner, or staff), `addBoatOwner` (staff only; someone already invited has that line ended with "Became an owner", so they hold one line), `removeFromBoat` (staff take off anyone; an owner takes off people they invited, never an owner), `removalEffect` (what taking someone off cancels, for the button's words); `findBoatPeople` (the search); and the views `privateBoats`, `myOwnedBoats`, `myInvitedBoats`, `lockedBoats`, `boatAccessLog`.
- **The booking check** (`checkBooking`, three new reasons, none of them something staff can set aside, because it is the owner's say and not a club rule): `boat_locked` (the boat has no owner who can use it), `not_allowed_on_boat` (the booker is not on the list) and `crew_not_allowed` (someone in another seat is not on the list). The skill check (booker and crew) is skipped for a private boat. The window, hours, steps, length, per-day and at-once limits, the "nobody in two boats" and "nobody in a class" rules and the taken-slot rule all still apply. `describeRules` says it is private and that no skill is checked.
- **Who sees what.** The day view (`dayView`) leaves a private boat out for a member who is not on its list, and for filtering by type; staff see everything; the booking form for a private boat is "page not found" for someone not on its list (also by its address); its crew choices are only people on the list; staff's "Booking for" list is only people on the list. A booking on a private boat shows other people on the list "Booked", never a name. A locked boat's free slots are not links and the card says why. A member who holds access sees a "My boats" link on the Book page.
- **Taking someone off.** Their **upcoming** bookings on that boat are cancelled (never recorded as a late cancellation, cancelled by the person who took them off, with the reason "X is no longer allowed on Y"), any seat they held on someone else's outing there is taken away (the outing stays), and outings already under way and past ones stay. The button says what it will do ("Take off (cancels 1 booking)").
- **When an owner leaves the club** (ending a household, removing an adult, a resignations file): their future bookings are cancelled by the usual leaving-the-club step, nothing on the list is edited (a line for someone who has left simply stops counting, and shows "no longer an active member"), and if no owner who can still use the boat is left, the boat is **locked**: nobody can book it, bookings already made by invited people stay (staff see the count), and it is on the Staff office board ("Private boats with no owner") until staff add an owner.
- **Screens.** `/club/my-boats` (a member: the boats they own with owners, people they invited, a name search and an Invite button, and the boats they were invited to; staff are pointed to their own screen) and `/club/admin/boats` (staff only: every private boat, locked ones first, with owners, invited people, a search with **Make owner** and **Invite**, a **Take off** button on every line and the record of who was let on and taken off, newest first, eight lines and "...and N older lines"). The search shows nothing until three letters are typed, never more than six names, only active members not already on the list, names only (no email or phone), and tells two people with one name apart by household. The buttons are Next.js server actions (`my-boats/actions.ts`: `inviteToMyBoat`, `removeFromMyBoat`, which start with `startMemberAction()`; `admin/boats/actions.ts`: `makeOwner`, `inviteAsStaff`, `takeOffBoat`, which start with `startStaffAction()`); the engine, not the page, decides who is an owner. After a button works the list refreshes and the person moves to where they belong, so the little "invited" note goes with the line it was beside (tests wait for where the person ends up).
- **The upload.** A Private row is now **added as a private boat**. The optional **Owner emails** column lists its owners, each as an **email address or a full name**, separated by semicolons (an address two partners share needs the full name instead; two members with one name need the address). Only active members can own a boat, up to 10 owners per row, an owner listed twice counts once, and owners on a Club row are refused in words. Every owner not found is listed with its row, together with the reader's own problems, **in the order of the file**. A private boat with no owner in the file is still added and starts locked (the read-back says so, listing up to five names). A private boat's skill level reads "not checked on a private boat". The "looks like a private boat" hint warning now applies only to rows marked Club. A boat already here (same name) is left alone, owners and all.
- **The database enforces** (migration `20261103000000_club_private_boats.sql`, tested against a real Postgres, each rule broken on purpose): a boat cannot turn private or stop being private after it exists; a list line only for a private boat; a line is only ended (once, with who, when and why), never changed or removed, and the whole list cannot be emptied; a person is on one boat's list once at a time; owners are named by staff only; an invitation by a member needs that member to be an owner of that boat at that moment; and no booking on a private boat unless the booker is on its list, no one in its other seats unless on its list, and no booking at all while it has no owner on its list. The Postgres store saves ended lines before new ones in one change, so someone made an owner in the same step that ends their invitation is never on the list twice (the first run found this).
- **Sample data.** Kestrel (a private single: Nora Abernathy owns it, Tomas Vandermeer is invited), Starling (a private double: Erik Lindqvist owns it, Ines Castellanos, who holds no qualification, is invited) and Wren (a private single with no owner: locked). No sample bookings were added.
- **Not built, on purpose:** making an existing club boat private, or a private one a club boat (the database refuses it; a mistake in a first upload needs the edit-or-retire tool, question CF); a cap on how many people an owner can invite; owners naming other owners; an invited person leaving a boat's list themselves; telling anyone by email that they were invited or taken off (no sender for that yet, BR); an owner taking their own boat out of service (staff do that on the Equipment page); pools.

- **Proof it can fail:** the new code was broken on purpose 103 ways and each run showed whether the tests noticed. **Engine, upload and Postgres store (60):** the list (a line that ended still counting, an invited person counting as an owner, an owner who left still counting, a boat with no owner never locked, staff or everyone seeing private boats), who may invite and name owners (anyone, an invited person, an owner who left, a member who left, a second line for an owner or an invited person, an invitation never ended when someone becomes an owner), taking someone off (an owner removing an owner, anyone removing anyone, bookings kept, recorded as late, crew seats kept, outings under way cancelled, a line ended twice, other boats' bookings cancelled), the search (one letter, sixty names, people already on the list, members who left, namesakes, "more"), the lists and the record (order, locked first, the to-do list, crew seats in the removal effect), the booking check (locked, not allowed, crew not allowed, skill checked on a private boat, staff able to set it aside), the day view (no hiding, skill shown, locked never said), the upload (owners accepted on a Club row, ten owners, an owner listed twice, a member who left as owner, a shared address, problems out of order, a private boat added as a club boat, owners not saved, the hint warning for boats already Private, locked count, a skill level read-back, an email not checked) and the Postgres store (private not saved or read back, an ended line never saved, who invited lost). 59 were caught the first time. **One miss** (taking someone off cancelled their bookings on other boats too) had no test that gave the person a booking elsewhere; it now does, and it was caught. **Database (30):** a boat turning private later, a list line on a club boat, an invitation by a non-owner (three ways), the one-active-line index (removed, and made too strict), a line changed or ended twice or removed or the list emptied, a kind or an adder that does not exist, an owner named by a member, the shape rules, a booking by someone not on the list or on a boat with no owner, a crew seat for someone not on the list or taken off, the rules applied to club boats, the list not locked to the owner, and each trigger not attached. 27 were caught the first time. **Three misses:** a line changed in the same step that ends it (the tests only changed it without ending it), someone taken off still seated as crew (only the booker's side was tested), and a line "added by the system" (hidden behind a second rule, so the test now uses an invitation, where only this rule applies). Each got a test and was then caught. **Browser (13, each with a rebuild):** the staff screen opened by a member, the booking form opened by address, a locked boat's slots clickable, a private double offering everyone as crew, the office board, the Take off button's words, the read-back and the day view not saying a boat is private, no My boats link, the locked tag, staff choosing anyone to book for, no tools link, and the invited list. 9 were caught the first time. **One miss** (the day view's Private tag): the test looked for the word "Private" in a card whose boat name contained it; it now reads the tag itself, and was caught. **Three would not compile** (a condition the type checker calls always false, or a field it could not narrow); each was rewritten so that it compiles and all three were caught. Before the runs, each list was checked to match exactly one place, and the unbroken tests were run and passed; a sample of the "caught" results was read to see that they failed in a test about the thing that was broken (all did).

## Pools (built Oct 30; merged as PR 39)

Grant's answers (Oct 30): the equipment sheet's dock, club room and gym spaces become **one pool per kind with a number of places** (60, 49 and 8, the highest numbers seen in the 2026 report, to be confirmed). In the build round Grant did not pick answers to four design questions (he sent the iCrew idea instead), so the recommended ones were built and are listed in question CI: **a class takes the whole room** while it runs; members see **only how many places are left**, never a place number; **one place per person at a time**; the starting sizes are the report's numbers.

The building comparison: a parking lot with 60 spaces. The club does not care which space a car takes, only that the lot is never over-full at any moment. A boat is still a single reserved space: one booking at a time, checked exactly as before.

- **Pieces.** `Item.places` (undefined means one; 1 to 500; only for `POOLED_TYPES`, Dock, Gym and Room Space; fixed when the item is made; never on a private boat). `engine/pools.ts` holds `placesOf`, `holdingIn` (the bookings that take a place at some moment inside a stretch of time) and `occupancy` (how many places are taken, stretch by stretch, so back to back bookings never count together) and `firstFullStretch`.
- **Why counted, not numbered.** A numbered place could say "full" when it is not: with two places, A has 9 to 10 and B has 10 to 11, and C wants 9 to 11. At no moment are both places taken, so C fits, but no single numbered place is free for the whole two hours. Counting the busiest moment is exact.
- **The booking check** (`checkBooking`): for a pool, the busiest moment inside the booking, with this booking in it, may never be more than the places; the refusal keeps the code `slot_taken` and says in words when it is full ("Dock berths is full from 8:00 to 9:00 am: all 4 places are booked."). A new reason `already_has_place` refuses a second place in the same pool at an overlapping time ("You already have a place in Dock berths from 8:00 to 9:00 am. It is one place at a time."); it is a fact, so **staff cannot set it aside**. The gap rule only applies to single items. The window, hours, steps, length, per-day and at-once limits, out of service and the class rules all still apply. A pool that is out of service is out of service for every place.
- **A class holds the whole room.** Unchanged: a class running in a pool's room takes all of it (no member can book any place while it runs, and a class cannot be scheduled over a place someone holds), because the step 3 room rules look at any booking on the item.
- **The day view** (`dayView`, `poolSlot`): each slot of a pool says how many places are left; "Yours" for the member's own place; a full slot is "Booked" in the data and **"Full"** on the page; a slot under a class is "Class" with none left. Members never see names; staff see who holds places (a tooltip on the slot, `holders`). A boat's slots are unchanged.
- **Screens.** The Book page shows "N places" on a pool's card, "N left" on each free slot and, in each slot's label, "3 of 4 places left". The booking form's rules say a pool is shared, counted and one place at a time (`describeRules`).
- **The upload.** A Dock Space, Room Space or Gym Space row now loads as one pool with its **Places** (required, 1 to 500). A pool row is the club's: Seats must be empty (or 1), Owned by must be empty or Club (a private pool is refused), Owner emails must be empty; Skill level needed and In service work as for any item. A pool whose name is already here is left alone like any item. The read-back says "1 pool will be added: Dock spaces (60 places). Members book a time and see how many places are left; nobody is given a place number." and each pool's card says "Pool · 60 places, counted rather than numbered". The template has a fifth example row (`EXAMPLE-5`, a gym of 8 places). The old "pools wait for a later step" notice and result line are gone.
- **The database enforces** (migration `20261104000000_club_pools.sql`, tested against a real Postgres, each rule broken on purpose): places 1 to 500; more than one only for a dock, club room or gym; fixed once made; never on a private item; for a pool, **no booking may leave more people in it than it has places at any moment** (the busiest moment inside the new booking is counted under the same per-item lock the old rule used, so two requests for the last place line up one at a time, tested with two connections); a booking's single-place flag is set by the database from the item, never by the app, and cannot be flipped; and nobody holds two places of one pool at once (an exclusion rule on item, booker and time). A single item keeps the overlap rule it has had since step 2 (the exclusion constraint now applies to single-place bookings). A pool the count refuses answers with the same "that time was just taken" the overlap rule gave (error code 23P01).
- **Sample data.** Two invented pools: Dock berths (4 places) and Weight room (3 places), no sample bookings. The sample Boathouse gym and Clubhouse room stay single items because classes use them.
- **Not built, on purpose:** classes that take only some places; editing a pool's places or taking some of its places out of service (the number is fixed when it is made; the edit-or-retire tool is question CF); place numbers; a member bringing a guest as a second place (visitors are signed in on the iPad); different booking numbers for a dock, gym or club room than for boats (they share the placeholders, question 5); cardio machines.

- **Proof it can fail:** the new code was broken on purpose 68 ways and each run showed whether the tests noticed (each test command was run unbroken first). **Engine, upload and store (43):** the count of places (a pool read as one place, cancelled bookings or touching bookings counted, the cuts at booking starts and ends, the start moment not counted, the end moment counted, a stretch joined across a gap), the booking check (a pool of two using the single rule, two places for one person, "one place at a time" made overridable, a full pool never refusing, someone else's place checked, the words), the day view (one place too many left, full one early, "yours" not shown, names shown to members, a class slot showing places, a pool of two shown as one, the own booking not linked), the rules words, the upload (places not saved, the wrong class, seats, odd owner, a private pool and empty places allowed, the limit, a name twice, in service ignored, six pools listed, the pool count) and the Postgres store (places not read or not saved). 39 were caught the first time. **Four misses:** a person's back to back booking counting as "already has a place" (the test used a gap; it now books straight after) and the rules words for a pool of two (the test used eight places; it now tries two) each got a test and were then caught; two helpers (`isPool` and a `busiest` count) were used only by tests, so they were deleted instead of tested. **Database (18):** all caught the first time, after reading the failing test names (the places limit, a private pool, places changing, a shell as a pool, the type rule, the single-place overlap rule applied to pools and to nobody, one place each off, one place too many, counting off, only the start moment counted, the start moment not counted, cancelled bookings counted, the flag not set from the item, the flag flipped, the count outside the item lock, a pool of two not counted). **Browser (7, each with a rebuild):** all caught the first time (the places-left words, a full slot saying Booked instead of Full, staff not seeing who holds the places, a free slot showing no count, the card hiding the number of places, the read-back hiding the places, the done message hiding pools); the failing tests of three of them were read and each was about the thing broken.

## The iCrew crawl (Oct 30)

Grant (Oct 30) asked whether Claude in Chrome should walk through iCrew to find features the new system has not accounted for (boat usage, damage reports, reservation insights, lineups for larger boats). Claude in Chrome cannot be driven from this cloud session, so Grant ran it himself with a read-only prompt (never press anything that creates or changes data, record no member names, emails or phones, go slowly) and sent back a Claude Doc, **"iCrew feature inventory"**, taken on Oct 30 with a coach and admin account (member-side screens only sampled). Nothing was created, changed, sent or cancelled in iCrew, and no member data is in the notes or here.

The building comparison: until now only the leasing office had been seen (the reservations report). This is a walk through the other floors.

- **What iCrew is, in counts.** 20 teams, 568 members, 309 sessions, 141 reservations, 98 shells (103 shell records), 64 oar sets, 366 assets (docks, kayaks, paddle boards, a canoe, cardio, gym, room and work spaces), 19 documents, 6 registration profiles, 14 incidents, 6 events, 9 locations. It also runs non-rowing programs (spin, yoga, strength) as teams and sessions. **iCrew retires its classic portal on Nov 1, 2026** in favour of a new one, so menus and possibly the export layouts may change. iCrew bills the club about $0.75 per member a month (about 600 members, roughly $500 a month with tax).
- **Its real booking settings** (Club Profile, Reservation Settings): default 90 and maximum 240 minutes, 14 days ahead, **no maximum per member**, no minutes between usages, "no limits on privately owned" on, oars optional, default member skill level Unrated, custom shell permissions off, recurring reservations off, take-an-open-seat off, current-day-only off, per-seat oars off, check-in reminder email off, On-the-Water log on, require distance rowed off, waitlist max 99, a session can block reservations while it runs and club events show "Res. blocked!". Modules on: Documents, Equipment, Events, Dates Away, Related Links, Damage Reports, Chats, Incident Reporting, In-App Messages, Boat Location Tracking. Off: Launches, Erg Tests, Journals, Coach's and Volunteer Hours, Punch Cards, Family Units, Dragon Boats, Forms/Surveys/Elections/Quizzes, To Do List.
- **Boat usage.** An On-the-Water log (tap to Check out and Check in, editable times and a note; Scheduled, On the Water, Back on Land) so the Out and In columns of the Reservations report are probably **real times, not the booked times** (this reopens BL, question CM). Reports: Shell Usage (hours, meters, count, privately owned filters), Oars Usage, Distance Rowed, Rowing and Coxing History. Distance is mostly not captured (meters nearly always 0). Boat Location Tracking is on.
