- **Damage reports** (our biggest gap). Anyone files one (a Damages list, a button on each shell, a tile on the member home, a printable QR code per boat). Fields: equipment, item, date reported, reported by, description, **major damage**, location of damage, how it occurred, assigned to, members involved, repair completed, date repaired, repaired by, hours spent, cost, repair notes, supplies used, always show on reservation page, **priority 1 to 4**. No photo field. Alerts: per-asset subscriptions emailing people when a report is logged or a repair completes (a few admins on nearly all boats); a changelog line says members with an upcoming reservation get a text if their boat is reported damaged (not confirmed on). **Nothing takes a boat out of service automatically** (a shell has an "Unavailable for use" field and a major-damage flag; whether major damage blocks booking is unconfirmed). 8 open at the time. A **separate incident report** (14 on file; its own QR code): status, summary, date and time, description, cause, on the water, location, coached session, team, people involved (injured, entered the water, parent notified, role), non-club people, involved shells (damaged, significant), water and weather conditions, first aid and who gave it, emergency action, squads called, non-club vessels, corrective action, ways to avoid it, follow-up and administrative notes.
- **Reservation insights.** Club Insights (monthly charts: sessions, session attendance, hours per boat, messages, new members, damage reports, zip codes). A Cancelled Reservations Audit Trail is the only way to spot late cancellations; **no no-show report**. Inactive Members, Member Participation, Session Attendance and Attendance Plans reports.
- **Lineups.** A tile-based editor per coach-run session (Shells, Oars and Rowers panels; side preference and skill on each tile; sorting by side, gender, name, age, skill; cox and bow seats; guest tiles), one coach at a time, **no saved templates seen**, private or public with a team setting, no automatic notice to the crew found. A club option auto-releases boats and oars if nobody is seated N hours before a session. Lineups belong to sessions, not to a member's own boat booking.
- **Equipment records.** A shell has seats, weight class, location, unavailable-for-use, rigging (sweep or scull, convertible with a Swap Rigging page), required skill level, make and model, last inspected, privately owned with several owners, rack space, disposed. Oars: type, name, quantity, location, inspection, reservable. Other assets: reservable, location, purchase, serial, inspection, custom fields. Boat privately-owned: many, with owners attached and exempt from limits.
- **Permissions.** Skill levels Unrated, LTR, Novice, Intermediate, Advanced, High Performance; boats carry a required level and weight range; shell permissions by boat size; certifications launch, bow and flip test (with an expiry date) and "Passed flip test" and "Approved to row on own". Documents (waivers, policies, a quiz) have expiry dates and "block until on file": about 540 water-sports waiver signatures, about 130 each for the USRowing waiver and SafeSport policy. A Missing Items report shows who is blocked. Profiles also hold emergency contact, date of birth, height, weight and an allergies, conditions and medications field (in Dynamic Views).
- **Other things a club might not realize.** Sessions and teams (fees, limits, drop-in, auto-archive), regatta tools (sign-up deadline, trailer manifest, USRowing import), registration profiles with a public link and waitlists, messaging (email, text, app, scheduled, session chat), Premium iCrew Timing, PaySimple store and payments (blank here), Dynamic Views and Member Segments, a kiosk Today page, an iPhone app and push notifications, Dates Away, Boathouse Weather, a Scheduling Assistant.
- **Not opened or unclear** (the notes say so): the Add and New forms (by design), several reports (Club Directory, Document Tracking, Email Recipient Issues, Team Membership Changes, Custom Reports), the Fees pages, the new portal, the Manage settings pages, the mobile app and the iCrew Timing screen; whether major damage blocks booking, whether lineups clone with a session, whether making lineups public notifies the crew, whether text damage alerts are on.

**Grant's answers (Oct 30):** damage and incident reports first (not the others offered: waivers and certifications that expire, real out and in times with hours per boat, oars with the boat); **copy iCrew's real booking numbers** (14 days ahead, 90 minutes by default up to 4 hours, no limit per member, no gap between bookings, private boats exempt from limits); lineups are **some of both** (members book and name their own crew, and coaches also seat crews for some sessions). The booking numbers were applied the same day (see "Booking numbers from iCrew"); damage and incident reports and lineups are not built yet: see questions CJ to CP.

## Booking numbers from iCrew (built Oct 30; merged as PR 40)

Grant's answer (Oct 30, from the iCrew crawl): **copy iCrew's real booking settings** as the system's starting numbers. They stay placeholders until staff confirm them (question 5).

The building comparison: the house rules on the lobby wall. They were the manager's guesses; now they are the rules the old building already ran by, so tenants notice no change on move-in day.

- **The numbers** (`src/club/settings/booking-rules.json`): **14 days ahead** (was 7), **90 minutes to start with** (a new rule, `defaultMinutes`; the booking form used to start at a fixed hour) and **up to 4 hours** (was 2), the shortest still 30 minutes in 30-minute steps, and **no limit on how many bookings a member holds at once or makes in a day** (was 2 and 1). Hours (5 am to 8 pm), the midnight opening, the 2-hour cancel cutoff and no gap between bookings are unchanged. The file's note says where the numbers came from.
- **A limit of 0 means no limit** (`maxFuturePerType` and `maxPerDayPerType`; any other rule must still be 1 or more). The booking check only counts a limit when it is above 0; the rules words say so ("There is no limit on how many shell bookings you can hold or make in a day."; with one limit set, the other is named "with no limit on how many you make in a day"). `parseRuleBook` also checks the new default length (between the shortest and longest, a whole number of steps) and still refuses a day limit above the held limit when both are set.
- **Private boats exempt from limits** (iCrew's "no limits on privately owned"): with no limit on anyone, this changes nothing today, so no special case was built. If staff ever set a limit, private boats follow it like every other boat (Grant's Oct 30 answer to the private-boats question 6 stands) unless Grant says otherwise.
- **The engine tests run on fixed test numbers.** `testkit.ts` has `TEST_RULES` (the old placeholders: 7 days, 1 hour to start with, up to 2 hours, 2 held at once, 1 a day), and `ruleBookWith` starts from them, so editing the club's real numbers never breaks an engine test. The real file's own numbers are held by `rulebook.test.ts` and the new `engine/club-numbers.test.ts` (the 14-day window to the minute, 90 minutes and 4 hours, six bookings in a day and twelve in all, a limit of 0 on one rule turning off only that rule, the words). The browser tests run on the real file, so six booking tests and one class test were updated for the 90-minute start and the longer window; the rebook test no longer counts a limit (the limit words are covered in the engine tests).
- **Not built, on purpose:** per-class or per-item defaults for the new rule beyond the existing layering (a class or item can override `defaultMinutes` like any rule); a real exemption for private boats; hours per week or other limit kinds.
- **Proof it can fail:** broken on purpose 19 ways, all caught the first time: **engine, rules file and words (16):** a limit of 0 refused or allowed for every rule, "0 held" and "0 a day" refusing everything, each of the three sets of no-limit words lost, the default length range, step and edge not checked, and the file's window, longest booking, default length and both limits changed; and the day picker one day short. **Browser (3, each with a rebuild):** the form starting at the shortest length, the rules saying there is a limit, and the rules giving the wrong window. A redundant guard I had added to the day-limit check (it could never be true) was deleted instead of tested.

## Damage reports (built Oct 30; merged as PR 40)

Grant's answers (Oct 30): damage and incident reports came first among the missing pieces from the iCrew crawl (question CJ). To the plan he said: **any signed-in member or staff may file** one; a **major** report gives staff a one-click "Take out of service" and **does not take a boat out by itself**; people are told **on screen only** for now (no new emails); the **incident (injury) report is a second round**, staff-only to read. Photos and a QR code on each boat were left out on purpose.

The building comparison: the maintenance request box. A tenant drops in a request (what is wrong, where, how it happened, how serious). The office triages it (how urgent, who is on it), can close the unit until it is fixed, and closes the request with what was done and what it cost. What the tenant wrote is never rewritten, a closed request stays closed, and every step is a line in the request's own log.

- **Pieces.** `DamageReport` (the boat, who filed it and when, what is wrong, where, how it happened, major or minor, priority 1 to 4, status open / being fixed / repaired / dismissed, who is fixing it, and when closed: the repair date, by whom, time, cost and notes, or the reason it was dismissed) and `DamageLogEntry` (one line per step: filed, note, assigned, priority changed, started, taken out of service, returned to service, repaired, dismissed). Both live in `ClubState` (`damageReports`, `damageLog`) and are never removed.
- **Rules are data.** `src/club/settings/damage-rules.json` (placeholders): `filedPerMemberPerDay` 10 (0 means no limit; staff are not limited; counted back exactly 24 hours), `majorPriority` 1, `minorPriority` 3, and the longest text for each box (`descriptionMax` 300, `locationMax` 120, `howMax` 500, `noteMax` 500, `assignedToMax` 80, `repairedByMax` 80, `repairNotesMax` 1000, `dismissReasonMax` 200). `parseDamageRules` refuses a typo, a missing key, a silly number, and a major priority less urgent than the minor one, in words.
- **The engine** is `engine/damage.ts`: `fileDamageReport`, `addDamageNote`, `assignDamageReport`, `setDamagePriority`, `startDamageRepair`, `takeOutOfServiceForReport`, `markDamageRepaired`, `dismissDamageReport`, and the views `openDamageNotices`, `noticeWords`, `bookingDamageNotices`, `myDamageReports`, `reportableItems`, `damageQueue`, `damageDetail` and `officeDamage` (also part of `officeSummary`). Each step is one all-or-nothing change that writes the report's log line in the same step. `takeOutOfService` and `returnToService` in `bookings.ts` were split into `addOutOfService` / `endOutOfService` (which work on the change's own copy) so a report's button runs the same code inside its own change.
- **Who may do what.** File: a member still on the club's books (a private boat only if they are on its list) or staff, never the system. A member can file 10 in 24 hours. Add a note: staff any time; the person who filed it while it is open (any other member is told the report does not exist). Everything else is staff only. **A closed report (repaired or dismissed) cannot change at all**; if the damage is back, someone files a new one.
- **What members read.** The open reports on a boat show on its Book page card and on the My bookings row of anyone with an upcoming outing on it (booker or crew). `noticeWords` quotes "what is wrong" and "where": "Major damage reported on Oct 28, 2026: "..." (Bow). Check with club staff before you use it." or "Minor damage ... You can still use it." Never who filed it and never how it happened (the form says so). The filer's own list (`/club/damage`) shows status, repair date, a dismissal reason and their own notes, never priority, who is fixing it, hours, cost or staff's notes.
- **What staff do.** `/club/admin/damage` (the queue: major first, then most urgent, then oldest, with days open; a second list of closed ones) and `/club/admin/damage/[reportId]` (everything, the log newest first, the other open reports on the boat, and the buttons: who is fixing it, priority, mark as being fixed, take the boat out of service, mark repaired with when / by whom / hours / cost / notes and an "Also return the boat to service" tick, dismiss with a reason, add a note). The tick is left off, with a note, when another major report is still open on the boat. Taking the boat out of service is the Equipment page's step with the reason "Damage reported: ..." (cut to 200 characters), lists the bookings that stay, and is refused if the boat is already out of service for any reason. The Staff office has a damage board (counts, major reports on a boat still in service, the top five).
- **The database enforces** (migration `20261105000000_club_damage_reports.sql`, tested against a real Postgres, each rule broken on purpose): reports and log lines are never removed and the log is never changed; what the filer wrote never changes; the status only moves forward and a closed report is frozen; the closing details are present exactly when the report is closed; a new report has its "filed" line, and a change of status, priority or assignee has a matching log line written at the same moment (checked at the end of the change); a member can only file and add notes to their own report, only while it is open; a member cannot file on a private boat that is not theirs. A rule left out on purpose: the database does not refuse a report's update time going back (two app servers' clocks can differ by a moment). The local test database is plain ASCII (CI's is UTF-8), where a "..." is counted in bytes, so text that is stored uses three dots, not an ellipsis.
- **Sample data.** Four invented reports: a major one being fixed on Merganser (already out of service), a minor open one on Pelican, a repaired one on Loon (with hours and cost) and a dismissed one on Tern.
- **Server actions and the live-site test.** Next.js knows a server action only on the pages that use it, so the six staff buttons are called, in `club-hidden.spec.ts`, on a report's own page (`/club/admin/damage/<id>`); called on the list page they answer 200 with an error instead of "page not found". The first run of that spec failed for exactly this reason (a mistake in the test, not a leak).
- **Not built, on purpose:** photos; a QR code per boat; emails or texts when a report is filed or a boat is repaired (BR); the incident (injury) report (CJ, second round); reopening a closed report; editing what was filed; per-boat subscriptions; assigning a report to a person in the system (who is fixing it is free text); blocking bookings automatically on a major report.
- **Proof it can fail:** the new code was broken on purpose and each run showed whether the tests noticed. **Engine (73):** the rules about what is typed, the daily limit and its edges, who may file / note / triage / close, private boats, priorities, the take-out-of-service step, repair dates (Pacific day, not future, not before filing), time and cost limits, return to service, the other-major count, dismiss, what members and staff read, the queue order, days open, the board. 66 were caught the first time. **Four misses** got stronger tests and were then caught: whole days open rounded down; "Being fixed" wording in the engine's views; the other-open list's order and leaving closed ones out; and a non-staff person being told they cannot take a boat out of service before anything about the report. A list entry that matched twice was fixed and caught, one more was added for the other-open order and caught, and **one check could never change anything** (a report counting itself as another open major, when it is already repaired) and was deleted instead of tested. **Database (48):** 44 caught the first time; two misses (a priority or assignee change with its log line written at another moment) got tests and were then caught; **two cannot change anything real** and stay uncaught on purpose (allowing "open to open" changes nothing; an extra status word is already refused by the "starts open" and "only moves forward" rules). A sample of the "caught" results was read to see that they failed in a test about the thing broken (all did). **Store mapping (12):** every field of a report and of a log line read back exactly as written (assignee, priority, repair time, cost, notes, the dismissal reason, how it happened, who filed it and how), the filer saved, report changes saved, a rewritten log line refused, and the log read back in the order it was written. All 12 were caught the first time. **Browser (7, each with a rebuild, after an unbroken run that passed):** the Book page notice, the My bookings notice, the staff page hiding how it happened, a member opening the staff queue, the report form clearing after filing, the Staff office board's words, and the "bring the boat back" tick being on with another major report open. All 7 were caught the first time; the failing tests of three were read and each was about the thing broken (on both servers).
- **Mistakes of mine to remember.** (1) The first engine break-it run "caught" everything in a second: the test database had stopped (connection refused) and every test failed for that reason. The unbroken baseline had not been run first. It was run, the database restarted and the whole run repeated. Run each command unbroken first, every time, and look at how long it takes. (2) Two browser tests booked the same member into the same time slot when run side by side and one was refused ("nobody in two boats at once"); each now uses its own member. (3) I killed a runner by process number, correctly, but left one file mid-break; I restored it from the runner's saved copy and checked the tree matched the commit before going on.

## Practices and lineups (built Oct 30; merged as PR 42, proof of the tests in PR 43)

Grant's answers (Oct 30, question CL): a **coach is staff, plus members that staff mark as coaches** (a coach can set up practices and seat crews and nothing else in the staff office); lineups are **optional** and only for **coached or organized rowing sessions**, never for ordinary reservations (Grant: "an option for those sessions, not mandatory for all reservations"), so a member booking a boat and naming their own crew is unchanged; a practice **reserves its boats** while it runs; rowers see their seat **on screen only**; and there is **no skill check** (the coach decides).

The building comparison: booking the rooftop for a resident event. The office holds the roof (the boats) for the evening, so no tenant can book it for themselves while the event runs, and the organizer writes down who stands where. A tenant who had already booked the roof is not turned away by the system: the organizer and the office are shown who they are, and talk to them.

- **The coach flag.** `Member.isCoach` (undefined means no). `setCoach` is staff only and writes a `coach_changed` line to the household's file ("Ben Quill was marked as a coach by Test staff."). A button on each adult in the household desk marks or unmarks. A coach is an active member who is marked; one who leaves the club or is unmarked stops being able to run anything (staff still can). `canRunPractices` is staff or an active coach; `canEditPractice` is staff or the coach who runs that practice.
- **Pieces.** A `Practice` (name, note, start and end, who runs it: a coach, or null when staff do, status scheduled or cancelled), the `PracticeBoat` lines (a boat reserved for it) and the `PracticeSeat` lines (a person in a seat of a boat, with the side they row on: port, starboard or none). Boat and seat lines are only added and ended (with who ended it and when, and for a seat why), never removed, so the lineup keeps its own history. A practice is never removed; it is cancelled, once, and only before it starts.
- **Rules are data.** `src/club/settings/practice-rules.json` (placeholders): the longest name (80) and note (500), the longest practice (6 hours), the most boats (20), how far ahead one can be set up (120 days, counted on the Pacific calendar) and the longest reason (200). `parsePracticeRules` refuses a typo in words.
- **Setting one up** (`createPractice`): a coach (who then runs it) or staff (who may name a coach, or run it themselves). Every problem is reported at once, in words: a name, the times (end after start, not in the past, not too long, not too far ahead), a coach who is not a coach, and each boat. Only a club boat, kayak or paddle board can go on a practice: not a private boat (its owners decide who uses it), not a dock, the club room or the gym, not a pool, not a boat that is out of service then, and not a boat another practice has at an overlapping time (back to back is fine).
- **A practice reserves its boats.** `checkBooking` has two new reasons, neither of which staff can set aside because they are facts: `boat_in_practice` ("Albatross is reserved for Morning session from 7:00 to 9:00 am.") and `in_practice` (somebody seated in a lineup cannot book another boat then, as booker or as crew; also true the other way round, a class sign-up is refused as `person_busy` for someone in a lineup). On the Book page the boat's slots show "Practice" (`SlotState` has a new `practice`, with its own legend entry and colour). A cancelled practice, or a boat taken off one, frees the boat at once.
- **Bookings made before the practice are flagged, never cancelled** (Grant's rule). `practiceClashes` lists the upcoming bookings on a practice's boats that overlap it. The practice page shows them to the coach and to staff: the coach sees names only, **staff also see the booker's email and phone** (a coach is a member, so other members' contact details stay with staff). The list screen counts them. The booker sees a notice on their own row of My bookings (`practiceNoticeFor`: "Albatross is also reserved for Morning session from 7:00 to 9:00 am. Please check with club staff before you go."). The booking stays upcoming.
- **The lineup** (`seatMember`, `clearSeat`): a seat is a number from 1 to the boat's seats, with a side. A person is in one seat of a practice. `checkSeat` returns every reason at once, none of which can be set aside: the seat does not exist or is taken, the boat is not on the practice, the person is not an active member, is already in the practice, has a boat booked (as booker or crew), is signed up for or waiting on a class, or is in another practice's lineup, at that time. There is **no skill check**. Moving someone is taking them out and seating them again. Taking someone out keeps the line, with "Taken out by Sam Coach: reason". A boat taken off a practice empties its seats, saying so on each line.
- **When it can change.** Boats and seats can change until the practice ends; it can only be cancelled before it starts; the name, note and times never change (cancel it and set up a new one). A coach changes only the practices they run; another coach sees the practice and a note says only staff or its coach can change it.
- **What people see.** Staff and coaches see every practice (`practiceList`: coming up soonest first, past and cancelled newest first, with boats, seats filled and clash counts) and all of one practice. A member who is seated sees the practice and **only their own boat with the people in it** (`practiceDetail`), on `/club/practices/<id>`, and everything else is "page not found". `/club/my-practices` and a "Practices you are in" block on My bookings show their boat, seat, side, coach and crew. A cancelled practice disappears from a rower's screens.
- **Leaving the club.** Nothing in the leave-the-club step changed. A seated person who leaves keeps their seat line; the lineup shows "(no longer an active member)" and the coach or staff take them out. Their time is not a problem because they cannot book.
- **The database enforces** (migration `20261106000000_club_practices.sql`, tested against a real Postgres): a member is marked or unmarked as a coach only with a matching `coach_changed` line in the household's file (checked at the end of the change, so the line may come after) and only an active member can be newly marked; a practice starts out scheduled, is run by an active coach or by staff, a member who sets one up runs it themselves, and it never changes its name, note, times, coach or maker, is cancelled once with who and when, never comes back and is never removed; a boat line is only a club boat, kayak or paddle board, is on a practice once at a time, is refused when another scheduled practice has the boat at an overlapping time (two practices asking at once get it once, by the same per-boat lock bookings use), is only ended (once) and cannot be ended while people are still seated in it; a **booking on a reserved boat during the practice is refused** (and a practice may still be set up over an existing booking: the app flags it); a seat is in a boat on the practice, within the boat's seats, for an active member of an active household, one person per seat and one seat per person of a practice, seated by staff or by the active coach who runs the practice, and is only ended once. Each rule was broken on purpose by a test. One real mistake was found by these tests: a `CHECK` that compared a member's id to a coach that was not named evaluates to "unknown", which a check treats as passing, so a member could set up a practice with no coach; it now uses `coalesce`. The "no change without a line" rule for the coach flag matches any line saying the same thing about that member (as the editing-details rule does), not only one written in the same change.
- **Server actions** (`practices/actions.ts`): `setUpPractice` (goes to the new practice's page), `addBoatToPractice`, `takeBoatOffPractice`, `seatPerson`, `takeOutOfSeat`, `cancelThePractice`, and `markCoach` in `admin/households/actions.ts`. The practice buttons start with `startSignedInAction()` and the engine decides who may do what (Next.js knows an action only on the pages that use it: `club-hidden.spec.ts` calls `setUpPractice` on `/club/practices/new` and the rest on a practice's own page). `PracticeRefused` carries every reason; the buttons show them together.
- **Sample data.** One invented coach, Kenji Tanaka (Intermediate), and one practice, "Sunday technique session", eleven days from today (a Sunday, chosen to stay clear of the days the older booking tests use), 7 to 9 am, run by him: Albatross (Erik Lindqvist port, Maren Lindqvist starboard, Hollis Whitcombe port, Nora Abernathy starboard), Pelican (Tomas Vandermeer, Alex Brooks) and Kingfisher with its seat empty. Sasha Bellweather had booked Kingfisher for 8 to 9 am that day before the practice was set up, so the sample shows a flagged booking.
- **Not built, on purpose:** **cox seats** and eights (no boat in the system has a cox seat or more than four seats; they come in as new boat types when the real equipment list is loaded, and the seat limit then follows the class); copying last week's lineup or saved templates (iCrew has none either); a coach choosing from a squad; check-in or attendance for practices; emails or texts when someone is seated or a practice is cancelled (on screen only, BR); moving a person from one seat to another in one step; editing a practice's times; repeating practices weekly; a private boat on a practice; recording out and in times or distance.
- **Proof it can fail** (PR 43, Oct 30 to 31): the code was broken on purpose 242 ways and each run showed whether the tests noticed (each test command was run unbroken first and passed). **Rules file (8):** all caught. **Store mapping (14):** every field of a practice, a boat line, a seat line and the coach flag read and saved; all caught. **Buttons (9):** 8 caught; the miss (the note about earlier bookings shown even when none overlaps) got a test. **Engine, and its wiring into the booking check, class sign-up and day view (144):** 130 caught the first time. Of the 14 misses, **10 were real gaps and got tests:** a boat back in service before the practice still refused (the edges of an out-of-service period); a dock booking at the same time keeping someone out of a seat; the past-practices list not held to its 50 lines; the list offering changes on a practice that is over; a practice staying in My practices at the very moment it ends; crew not listed in seat order; and the boat list and coach list a coach picks from, which had no test at all (private boats, docks, pools, the order, a coach who has left). **4 cannot change anything real and stay uncaught on purpose:** a practice's own boat lines counted when its boats are checked (twice: adding a boat already on it is refused earlier, and lines that came off are skipped); the one-place half of a two-part boat check (a pool is never on the water); and the check that a boat is still on the practice when its seats are listed (taking a boat off already empties its seats). **Database (53, and 4 more written for the new tests):** 43 caught the first time. **9 misses were real gaps and got database tests:** a practice inserted already cancelled with its cancellation filled in (the old test's case was refused by another rule, so the "starts out scheduled" rule was never tried); a cancelled practice brought back by clearing its whole cancellation; its time of change rewritten; a practice made by staff naming a member as its maker; a coach who then left the club; the practices table's own wipe guard (hidden behind the boat and seat tables' guards, so it is now tried with those two switched off); any other field of a boat line or seat line changed in the very step that ends it; and the boat lock (the old "race" test seldom overlapped; the new tests hold a second request open behind the first, for a second practice and for a booking). Of the 4 extra, the two new wipe guards were caught. **3 stay uncaught on purpose:** the database check that a reserved boat has one place (a pool is never a shell, kayak or paddle board); the practice trigger's own boat lock (the pools trigger, which fires first because of its name, takes the same lock on every booking; the second lock is kept so the safety does not hang on the order of two trigger names); and the active-household check on a coach (the database already forbids an active adult in an ended household). **Browser (10, each with a rebuild):** 9 caught the first time. One would not compile, twice (the type checker forbids an always-false condition; then my second try named a field that does not exist); the version that compiles, hiding the staff contact details, was caught. After each list the failing tests of a sample were read: each failed in a test about the thing broken, and the biggest failure counts were breakages every set-up passes through (a staff check removed, boats never reserved) and the coach flag, which many tests flip.

## Incident reports (built Oct 31; merged as PR 44)

Grant's answers (Oct 31, to four questions after choosing the incident report from the iCrew crawl, question CJ): **only staff and coaches file one** (a coach is a member staff marked as a coach; ordinary members do not); the form is **short** (about ten boxes) and **staff add the cause, what will change and the follow-up afterwards**; the people involved are **members picked from the list, or a name typed in for anyone else**, each marked hurt or not and whether a parent was told; and after filing, staff can **add notes, say who is following up, record what will change, close it, and optionally link a damage report**. Decided earlier (the damage-report round): staff read every incident, the coach who filed one can see their own, nobody is emailed or texted (on screen only), and nothing about an incident ever shows on a boat's page or on anyone's bookings. Not chosen, so not built: a QR code per boat, photos, a separate "entered the water" box, incidents by anyone signed in.

The building comparison: the incident log in the manager's office. Whoever was on the scene (staff, or a coach) writes down what happened, who was involved and who was hurt. The office adds what caused it and what will change, notes who is following up, links the work order if a boat was damaged, and files it away. What the person on the scene wrote is never rewritten, a filed-away report never changes, and every step is a line in the report's own log.

- **Pieces.** An `Incident` (who filed it and when, when it happened, what happened, where, on the water or not, the water and weather, first aid given and what, emergency services called and which, status open or closed, and staff's own boxes: the cause, what will change, who is following up, the damage report linked, and when and how it was closed), its `IncidentPerson` lines (a member picked from the list, or a typed name; hurt or not; a parent told or not), its `IncidentBoat` lines (the boats or spaces involved) and the `IncidentLogEntry` lines (filed, note, reviewed, closed). All four live in `ClubState` and are never removed; people, boats and log lines are never changed.
- **Rules are data.** `src/club/settings/incident-rules.json` (placeholders): how far back an incident can be dated (90 days), the most people (20) and boats (10) on one report, and the longest text for each box. `parseIncidentRules` refuses a typo, a missing key and a silly number in words, and keeps every limit inside what the database column holds.
- **The engine** is `engine/incidents.ts`: `canFileIncident`, `fileIncident`, `addIncidentNote`, `reviewIncident`, `closeIncident`, `findIncidentPeople`, `incidentBoatChoices`, and the views `myIncidents`, `incidentQueue`, `incidentDetail` and `officeIncidents` (also part of `officeSummary`). Each step is one all-or-nothing change that writes the report's log line in the same step.
- **Who may do what.** File: staff, and an active member staff have marked as a coach; never the system, never an ordinary member, never a coach who was unmarked or whose household ended. A coach may name only boats and spaces they are allowed to see (a private boat they are not on the list of looks like a boat that does not exist). Notes: staff any time (even on a closed report); the coach who filed it while it is open; anyone else is told the report does not exist. Review and close: staff only, and only while it is open. **A closed report cannot change at all** (a note in the log is the one thing staff can still add); if it happens again, someone files a new report.
- **Filing** reports every problem at once, in words, and saves nothing until there are none: what happened is needed; every box has its limit (exactly at it is fine, one over is refused); the yes or no for on the water, first aid and emergency services must be answered; first aid notes need first aid given and emergency notes need an emergency call; the time cannot be in the future or more than 90 days back; each person is a member from the list (an active member, listed once) or a typed name (listed once whatever the capital letters), never both; a boat is named once. Control characters are flattened and line breaks kept only in "what happened". The filed line reads "Reported by X. Nobody was hurt.", "1 person was hurt." or "N people were hurt.", with " Emergency services were called." when they were.
- **Staff's review** (`reviewIncident`) changes only what is different, in one step with one log line ("Reviewed by X. Cause recorded: ... Follow-up by changed to ... Link to the damage report removed."); a save that changes nothing is refused in words. The damage report link is a choice from the 40 newest damage reports (the linked one is always on the list), and the report's page links through to the damage report.
- **What people read.** A coach sees only the reports they filed (`myIncidents`): what they wrote, the boats and people, the status, and their own notes. **Never** the cause, what will change, who is following up, staff's notes or what was done. Staff see the queue (open: anyone hurt first, then emergency services called, then the oldest; closed: most recently closed first, with days open), a report's page with everything and its log (newest first), and an **incident board on the Staff office page** (open, how many have someone hurt, how many called emergency services, and the five most pressing). Nobody else sees any of it, and the damage and booking screens do not mention incidents.
- **Screens.** `/club/incidents` (staff and coaches; anyone else gets "page not found"): the form (day and time, what happened, where, on the water, water and weather, boats, who was involved with a name search of three letters and typed names, first aid, emergency services) and, for a coach, "Your reports" with a note box while a report is open. `/club/admin/incidents` (staff only: open and closed lists) and `/club/admin/incidents/[incidentId]` (staff only: the report, the people, the review form, the close form, a note box and the log). A coach has a "Report an incident" link in the menu; staff reach it from the Staff office tools. The yes or no questions are required boxes, so the browser itself stops a form with one left blank; the engine's words are for what the browser cannot know. The form clears after it works, and taking a name off re-runs the search so the person is offered again.
- **The database enforces** (migration `20261107000000_club_incident_reports.sql`, tested against a real Postgres): reports, people, boats and log lines are never removed and the table-wide wipes are refused; the words the filer wrote never change; a closed report is frozen, a closed report has its closing note and an open one has none; only staff or an active coach file, and a new report starts open with none of staff's boxes filled; first aid and emergency notes need the matching yes; the people and boats can only be added **in the very change that files the report** (`created_xact`), a member named from the list must be an active member, and a member or a typed name is on a report once; a report needs its "filed" line (checked at the end of the change); a change to the cause, what will change, who is following up or the damage link needs a matching "reviewed" line written at that moment, and a status change needs a matching "closed" line; a member's log line can only be "filed" or a note, a note only by the coach who filed it, and not on a closed report; and a log line cannot be dated before the report was filed. Row level security is on and nothing is granted to the public roles. Several of the check constraints sit behind a trigger that fires first (a constraint runs after the triggers), so the tests break exactly one thing at a time.
- **Server actions and the live-site test.** `incidents/actions.ts` (`fileIncidentAction`, `addIncidentNoteAction` and `searchIncidentPeople`, which start with `startSignedInAction()`; the engine, not the page, decides who is staff or a coach; the people search answers only people who may file, so the box cannot be used to browse the membership) and `admin/incidents/actions.ts` (`reviewIncidentAction` and `closeIncidentAction`, which start with `startStaffAction()`). Next.js knows an action only on the pages that use it, so `club-hidden.spec.ts` calls the first three on `/club/incidents` and the two staff buttons on a report's own page; every new page and action is "page not found" on the live site.
- **Sample data.** Three invented reports (`sample/incidents.ts`): an open capsize filed by the sample coach Kenji Tanaka with Tomas Vandermeer hurt, first aid given, reviewed by staff (cause, what will change, a safety officer following up); an open dock cleat incident filed by staff and not yet reviewed; and a closed near-miss from twelve days ago.
- **Found while building, said plainly.** (1) The first run of the engine tests found a real wording bug of mine: the refusal for too many boats read "2 boat or spaces". (2) The first run of the Postgres store found a real migration bug of mine: one shared trigger function used `new.member_id` on the boats table, which has no such column, so the people and boats checks are now two functions. (3) The first browser run found that the search box kept saying "No member found" after a name was taken off the list, until the person retyped; the form now searches again. (4) Three of my browser tests waited for a note inside a form that goes away when the page refreshes (the close form, as in earlier rounds), assumed there was one Eriksen in the sample (there are two), and expected the engine's words where the browser's own required-box check stops the form first; all three were fixed in the tests, not the screens.
- **Not built, on purpose:** photos; a QR code per boat; emails or texts when a report is filed or closed (BR); reopening a closed report; editing what was filed; a separate "entered the water" box (iCrew has one); coached session, team, non-club vessels and squads called (iCrew's other boxes); who may read a report beyond staff and its filer; a way to delete or export a report, and nothing yet enforces the 18 months Grant said to keep them (Oct 31; questions CS and CU); linking a report to a practice.
- **Proof it can fail:** the break-it-on-purpose runs have **not been done yet** for this feature (as for damage reports and lineups, they come as a follow-up pull request if Grant wants them). What was run: the tests on both stores and a real Postgres, and the first-run failures above, each of which was a real defect or a wrong expectation that was fixed.

## The honor snack bar iPad (built Oct 31; merged as PR 45)

Grant's request (Oct 31): a screen that works as **the honor snack bar iPad** for people taking snacks: they enter their name and the number of snacks, **every snack costs $1.50**, the screen walks through buying, **saves it for month-end billing tied to the right member account**, and **goes back to the first screen so the next person can use it**. The request was complete, so no plan questions were asked; the choices that were Claude's are question CT. In the same message Grant said to keep incident reports for 18 months (question CU).

The building comparison: the honor box in the lobby. A tenant takes a snack, writes their name and how many in the notebook, and it goes on their unit's account for the month. The iPad is the notebook: it only ever shows the name search, never anyone's total, and it turns to a fresh page by itself after every entry.

- **The walk-through.** (1) The start screen (`/club/snacks/<code>`, the screen the iPad rests on): "Honor snack bar", "Every snack is $1.50.", and a name box. Nothing is listed until three letters are typed, and never more than five active members, so the shared screen cannot be used to browse the membership (the same search as the visitors screen, `findHosts`). (2) "Hi, Ben" (`/club/snacks/<code>/<memberId>`): the price again, a big minus and plus starting at one snack, the total ("3 snacks = $4.50") and one big button that says exactly what it will do ("Add 3 snacks, $4.50, to my account"); "That is not me" goes back. The count stops at 20 (`itemsPerEntryMax`). (3) The thank-you ("Thanks, Ben! 3 snacks, $4.50, added to your account for October 2026. Enjoy."), which shows for 8 seconds and then sends the iPad back to the start screen; the other screens go back after 30 quiet seconds. Looking at any screen records nothing; only the button does. The thank-you is only shown for a purchase made in the last five minutes, so a bookmarked address cannot replay old ones.
- **Where it goes.** One ordinary **charge** on the member's own household's account (the household is the billing unit): category Snack bar, a sale (never negative), on the member's own tab, dated today (Pacific), description "3 snacks at $1.50", plus the count (`quantity`), the price of one when it was bought (`unitCents`) and the screen's token (`entryToken`). It is billed with everything else: the household's invoice for the month has one **Snack bar** line (QuickBooks "Snack Bar Sales") that adds up the month's snack charges, together with any snack sale staff typed in, described as "Snack bar: N charges in October 2026" (N counts purchases, not snacks; the snacks per purchase are on the Charges screen). No sales tax, as for every line (AY). A purchase cannot be added to a month that was already billed, and a month cannot be closed over the top of it: nothing about closing a month changed.
- **Rules are data.** `src/club/settings/snack-rules.json`: `priceCents` 150 (Grant's price), and as placeholders `itemsPerEntryMax` 20, `itemsPerDayMax` 40 (the most one member can have recorded on the iPad in a day; voided purchases and staff's typed charges do not count), `thankYouSeconds` 8 and `idleSeconds` 30. `parseSnackRules` refuses a typo, a missing key, a silly number and a day's limit smaller than a press's, in words.
- **The engine** is `engine/snacks.ts`: `recordSnacks` (one all-or-nothing change under the store's lock), `snackItemsOnDay`, `snackBarMonth` (what the iPad took in a month, for staff), and the words (`snackCountWords`, `snackPriceWords`, `snackTotalWords`, `snackThanks`). Only the member themselves can add snacks (the iPad acts as whoever was picked, as it does for visitors); club staff use the Charges screen. The member must be an active member of an active household. **One purchase per screen:** each time the member's screen is drawn it carries a random token, the same token is recorded once (the engine checks under the lock and the database has a unique index), and a second press of the same screen, a double tap or the browser's back button gets the first answer and adds nothing; a token reused for a different number or member is refused. The button also turns itself off once pressed.
- **The address.** The snack bar has **its own private address**, `/club/snacks/<code>`, where the code is a keyed fingerprint of the word "snacks" made with the same `CLUB_IPAD_SECRET` as the check-in iPad's (`snackCode` in `engine/ipad-address.ts`), so it is a different address (a leaked check-in address does not open the snack bar and the other way round) and **nothing new needs setting up**. Opening it signs nobody in and sets no cookie; every wrong address is "page not found". Changing `CLUB_IPAD_SECRET` switches both off. Staff see both addresses on `/club/admin/ipad`, with the set-up steps (open it once in Safari on the snack bar iPad, Add to Home Screen, leave it on the start screen). The button is `/api/club/snacks` (`startIpadPost(request, "snacks")`, so the snack code must be genuine and the person is whoever was picked); any refusal sends the screen back with "Those snacks could not be added. Check the number, or ask club staff for help." and nothing from the address is echoed. The screens sit in `src/app/club/snacks/` with their own layout (no menu, no picker, no links out).
- **What staff see.** The Charges screen (`/club/admin/charges`) has a line "Snack bar iPad, October 2026: N snacks in M purchases, $X" and marks each iPad purchase "entered by Ben Quill on the snack bar iPad", with the count in its note; a wrong one is voided there with a reason like any charge (a member cannot void), and a billed one can no longer be voided (adjust it instead).
- **The database enforces** (migration `20261108000000_club_snack_bar.sql`, tested against a real Postgres): the charges table, which used to take only staff entries, now also takes a member's own: but a member's entry must be a snack-bar sale (not an adjustment) on the member's own tab, entered by that member, with a count (1 to 100), a price (1 cent to $1,000) and a token (16 to 64 letters, digits, dashes or underscores), the amount exactly count times price, the token recorded once ever, the member an active member of an active household; staff's entries never carry any of those fields; and once saved, none of them can change. Each rule was broken on purpose by a test. The existing guards still apply (a closed month refuses a charge, charges are never removed). One older test changed: it used a member as "a charge by someone who is not staff", which is now allowed in the snack bar's own form, so it uses the system instead.
- **Not built, on purpose:** more than one price or a list of different snacks; a stock count; receipts or emails; payment (month-end billing only); a "my snacks this month" page for members; a member undoing their own entry; the iPad kiosk lock itself (Guided Access is set on the iPad); a separate `CLUB_SNACK_SECRET`; photos.
- **Proof it can fail.** The new code was broken on purpose and each run showed whether the tests noticed. **Engine (7):** the one-purchase-per-screen token removed, the day's limit not enforced, anyone able to buy for anyone, the amount one cent off, members who left able to buy, voided purchases still counted toward the day, and a billed month not refused: all caught the first time. **Database (3):** the amount rule dropped, the count no longer locked after saving, and the active-member check dropped: all caught the first time. **Browser (3, each with a rebuild):** the thank-you never returning to the start, the snack screen opening with the check-in iPad's address, and the form handler ignoring which iPad it was given: all caught the first time. The failing tests of each were read and each was about the thing broken. Mistakes of mine on the way (all in my own tests, fixed there): one expected sum was added up wrong (450 + 300 + 400 is 1,150, not 1,750); the test helper labelled a member "Ben" where the real iPad uses "Ben Quill"; and two database tests named the wrong rule as the one that fires first (a constraint runs after the triggers, so a missing maker is stopped by the guard, and a missing count by "a member's entry must carry the fields").

**After the merge: a Claude in Chrome test of the preview (Nov 1) and what came of it.** Grant had Claude in Chrome run a test plan on the snack bar's preview. The screens, the three-letter search, the counter, the limits, the timeout, the wrong-address 404 and the check-in address not opening the snack screen all behaved. Three steps "failed": after the button no thank-you appeared (the screen came back to "Hi, Walter" with the count at 1), and the staff Charges page said "nothing has been put on accounts yet". The tester guessed a date mix-up (a purchase dated Nov 2 by the world clock while the screens look at Nov 1). **That is not the cause**, and it was checked three ways: the thank-you looks a purchase up by its number and age only (no date); the engine dates a purchase on the **Pacific** day (new engine tests at 5:12 pm on Nov 1, 11:30 pm the same evening, 9:30 pm on the last day of a month and either side of the clocks falling back, on both stores; broken on purpose with the world-clock date, all 8 fail); and I reproduced it on the preview with curl.
**The real cause is how the preview keeps its records.** With `CLUB_DATABASE_URL` unset the member area keeps the invented club in the memory of one running server (`globalThis.__ccsClubStore` in `store/instance.ts`). On Vercel a button's handler (`/api/club/snacks`) and the pages are separate server instances, each with its own copy, and an instance that sits idle is thrown away. So the handler saved the purchase in its own copy (that is why the 40-a-day limit still held across presses, and why the record numbers 232, 233, 234 climbed), while the thank-you page and the staff page looked in other copies and found nothing. My own purchase on the preview got number 232 again, a fresh copy. Local runs, the browser tests and CI use one server (or a shared Postgres), so they never show it. **Consequence: on a Vercel preview without a database, anything saved by a button or API handler may not show on the next page** (bookings, snacks, RSVPs, check-in, and so on). That is a limit of the test copy, not a bug in the product: with Postgres every instance reads the same records. It does mean a preview cannot honestly demonstrate the stateful flows until it has a shared database (question CV). Nothing was changed in the engine for this.
**What was changed because of the tester's other findings** (the follow-up pull request): the day's limit now has its own words on the iPad ("That is the most snacks one person can add in a day, so these were not added. Please ask club staff."), still without showing anyone's total (`SnackDayLimit` in `engine/snacks.ts`, notice `snacks_day`), and the general refusal no longer says "Check the number" (it now reads "Please start again, or ask club staff for help."); "That is not me" (and the other iPad "back" links, all `ipad-back`) is a real 56-pixel button instead of a small underlined link; the quiet "This screen goes back to the start..." line, the "Type your name" label and the "no member found" lines are bigger and in the dark text colour (`ipad-meta`, `ipad-search-label`, `snack-label`, `ipad-none`). **Not changed, on purpose:** the typed letters appear in the web address (the search is an ordinary GET form; the iPad runs full-screen from the home screen with no address bar, and a partial name is all it holds); and the old search message stays on screen for a moment while the next page loads (that is a browser loading a new page, not stale data; a "Searching..." indicator was left out).

## Previews with a shared database (built Nov 2; in review)

Grant's answer (Nov 2, to question CV): **yes, a free throwaway database for previews only** (not for the live site, not for real members). The building comparison: until now every back office clerk kept a private notebook, so the clerk who took your booking was not the one who answered the next question. This is one shared record book for the model apartment.

- **Which database.** Any plain Postgres works; the store refuses Supabase addresses on purpose. Recommended: **Neon's Free plan** (checked on neon.com/pricing, Nov 2, 2026: $0, no credit card, "permanent (not a trial)", 100 compute-hours a month and 1 GB per project, the compute goes to sleep after 5 minutes of nobody using it and wakes on the next request, so the first request after a pause is slower). Choose the **US East (N. Virginia)** region, which sits next to Vercel's `iad1` servers, and copy the **direct** connection string (Neon's "Connection pooling" switch **off**). The pooled string is not recommended here: the one-time sample load takes a session-level lock, which a transaction-mode pool does not support, and it was not tried.
- **The settings** (Vercel, Settings, Environment Variables, **Preview only**): `CLUB_DATABASE_URL` (the direct string), `CLUB_AUTO_MIGRATE=1`, and the two that are already there (`CCS_ALLOW_SAMPLE_DATA=1`, `CLUB_IPAD_SECRET`). Then **redeploy the preview** (a setting only reaches builds made after it is saved).
- **What happens by itself** (`src/club/store/migrate.ts`, hooked into `createClubPool`, so all four stores wait for it): the first request applies every file in `supabase/migrations` in order, each in its own all-or-nothing step under one lock (two servers starting together apply each file once), and records the file names in `public.ccs_club_migrations`; then the existing start-up loads the invented sample club if the database holds no households. A later pull request that adds a migration is applied by the next preview's first request. A file that fails is named in the log, nothing from it is kept, and the next request tries again from it. The files ship inside every server function (`outputFileTracingIncludes` in `next.config.ts`; checked in the build's trace files).
- **How long the first request takes.** Setting up makes about 4,100 round trips to the database (16 for the rules, about 4,085 to load the sample), so it takes 2 seconds on a local database, roughly 10 seconds in the same region and over a minute across regions. Vercel's default limit with Fluid compute (on for this project, created after April 2025) is 300 seconds. After that it is fast. Anyone else who arrives meanwhile waits behind the lock.
- **Safety.** It is **off unless `CLUB_AUTO_MIGRATE` is exactly `1`**, and then refuses on the live production site (`VERCEL_ENV=production`, even with the sample-data switch set), wherever invented data is not allowed, and for any hosted Supabase address, all before it connects. It is meant for a brand new empty database: it assumes the tracking table is the only record of what is applied, so a database that was set up another way must not be pointed at it.
- **Starting over.** In Neon's SQL editor run `drop schema club cascade; drop table public.ccs_club_migrations;` and redeploy (or just visit a page): it sets itself up again and reloads the sample, dated from that day. The sample's "today" bookings are dated from the first load, so they go stale after a few days; starting over refreshes them.
- **What still lives in memory on a preview.** The test outbox that shows the sign-in "emails" (`/club/outbox`) is still kept per server, so email-link sign-in cannot be demonstrated on a preview; use the "act as" picker. Nothing else is: the club's records, the sign-in sessions, the old-bookings archive and the tour requests all use the database when it is set.
- **Proof.** `migrate.test.ts` (11 tests, on a real Postgres): off unless asked for, refusals (live site, not allowed, hosted Supabase) before any connection, every file applied in order to an empty database with the same tables and functions as the tests' own database, nothing applied the second time, two servers at once apply each file once, a failing file is named and keeps nothing while the earlier files stay (and a later file applies alone), a failure is not remembered, and the club's connection pool waits for the set-up (for a plain query and for a connection with a callback) and does not when it is off. By hand, on a built app: two servers started at the same moment on an empty database both answered in about 2.2 seconds, 18 of 18 files were recorded once, the sample (14 households) loaded once, a snack purchase posted to one server showed its thank-you on the other, and the staff Charges page on the other server showed "3 snacks in 1 purchase, $4.50".
- **Not verified yet:** Vercel and Neon themselves (the database does not exist yet), that Neon's default role may create the `btree_gist` extension the migrations ask for, and how long the first request really takes there.
- **Not built, on purpose:** the outbox in the database; seeding from a faster path (the sample's 4,085 inserts could be batched); a build-time set-up step; any use for the live site or real members (the real club database is a deliberate, separate step, question D and the go-live checklist).

## Small fixes from the screen review (built Nov 2; in review)

Another model test-drove the staff and member screens in a browser (Nov 1) and reported 44 findings. Grant (Nov 2) chose **the small, clearly-real fixes now** and **no running total on the shared snack iPad** (it stays private). The six "blockers" in the report were the preview keeping records per server instance (question CV), not the product, and are re-tested once the shared database is live. Most "missing spaces" in the report ("after30seconds") were the reading tool dropping spaces between tags, not real; the one real case was the Staff office list ("5:00 pm" glued to its dot).

The building comparison: a walk-round with the tenant handbook. Signs that were confusing get reworded, a dead door gets a "this door is closed" sign, and the lobby directory lists the rooms you actually have a key to.

- **Switched-off buttons look switched off** (`.club-button:disabled` in `club.css`: pale background, grey text, a "not allowed" cursor). Before, a button that was busy or refused looked exactly like a live one.
- **A refused booking says what to do.** The heading reads "This booking cannot go ahead as chosen" (it used to say "yet", which sounded like waiting would help), with a line under the reasons: change your choices and check again; skill levels are granted by club staff, so ask them to check your record. The reasons themselves are unchanged (the engine's words, held by many tests).
- **A day outside the window is no longer shown silently as another day.** `pickDay` (`web.ts`, replacing `dayParam`) returns the day to show and a sentence when the address asked for a day that is not on offer: too far ahead shows the last open day ("Friday, Dec 25 is further ahead than you can book yet. Showing Friday, Nov 11, the last day open."), already gone shows today ("... has already passed ..."). A bad or missing day shows the first day with no note. On Book and Classes (`day-note`).
- **"My boats" is in the member menu**, for a member who owns a private boat or was invited to one (never staff, never anyone else). The menu now needs the club's records, so `readClubOnce` (`store/read-once.ts`, `React.cache`) shares one read between the menu and the page under it; Book, My bookings, My household, a household page and My boats use it. A page that does not use it still reads for itself. The Book page's own "You can use private boats" note stays.
- **The damage report page links straight to the people to tell**: once a boat is out of service, "See who has it booked, to tell them" goes to the Equipment page's list for that out-of-service period (it was a bare "Equipment" link to a page that lists nobody until you arrive with the right address).
- **Words.** "Chairs" became "places" everywhere a member or staff member reads it for a class (the class list and page, My classes, the manage-classes form, the Staff office board, the check-in and departure screens and every engine message). Internal names (`chairs` form fields, `chairsText`, `ChairsTooFew`, test ids) are unchanged, and so is the database's own message ("Chairs cannot go below ..."), which a migration owns and which is only seen if two staff race. The end-a-membership page reads "The adult in this household will no longer be able to book" (was "The adult in it can no longer book"), and the green note after ending reads "The adults in this household can no longer book". The incident form says "call 911 first". The visitor rule gives a worked example because staff read "more than 4: every visitor costs $10" two ways: "... every visitor that day costs $10.00, not just the extra ones: 5 visitors cost $50.00 in all." (The reviewer suggested 5 visitors cost $10; that is not Grant's rule, so it was not adopted.) The sample practice is "Morning technique session" (it was "Sunday technique session" but is dated eleven days ahead, so on most days the page showed a Thursday).
- **Spacing.** The Staff office "Full, with people waiting" list reads "5:00 pm &middot; 12 of 12 places".

**Not done, on purpose (for Grant to decide, or not real):**
- The reviewer's "no-show rule" suggestion is invented (question I is still open); the **test-only outbox is not added to the member menu**; the public site's "since 2013" and "since 1910" in one paragraph is a wording choice for Grant (F40).
- **Moving the "Back to ..." links** out of sentences into one consistent place: about 20 pages, each with its own sentence and several held by tests. It is a layout decision for Grant (a real Back link at the top of every staff form page?), so it was left.
- **Design questions** still for Grant: a confirmation step before Take off / Close month / Confirm no-show, a simpler Staff office layout, a sticky boat column and filters on the booking grid, and a branded "page not found" with the club menu (it must still reveal nothing on the live site).
- **The snack iPad shows no running total** (Grant, Nov 2).

- **Checks (the final files, one full run):** lint 0 errors (1 warning, the old unused `RULE_BOOK` in `testkit.ts`), type check clean, **2,781 unit and database tests pass** (1 skipped), in 126 files, against a real Postgres; the production build passes; **623 of 623 browser tests pass** (the in-memory server, the Postgres-backed server and the two live-site servers). The first unit run showed 15 failures in 5 files, every one an expectation that held the old "chair" words; they were updated and the files re-run (197 of 197) before the full run.
- **Proof it can fail:** the new tests were not yet broken on purpose (a follow-up if Grant wants it, as for incident reports). Each new check does assert the thing it is about: the menu link is present for an owner on two pages and absent for a member with no private boat, the day note is exact for too-far, gone, on-offer, empty and nonsense days, the end-page and Staff office wording is matched with a pattern that fails on the old text, and the disabled-button test compares a switched-off button's colour and cursor with a live one.

## Phone apps in the Apple and Android stores: kept in mind, not built (Oct 24, 2026)

Grant (Oct 24): it is **likely** the club will want an interconnected app for Apple and Android, listed in the app stores. "Interconnected" is read as **one system, not a second product**: the app and the website use the same households, logins, bookings, classes, events and bills, so a change made in one shows in the other. Nothing is built for this yet and no step depends on it. It changes how new work is written, not what is built now.

The building comparison: the building's front door is the website; the app is a second door into the same lobby. The lobby (the rules and the records) must stay one room, with no rule written into either door.

Habits to keep so a second door is cheap later:
- **Rules live in `src/club/engine/` and records in `src/club/store/`, never in a page.** A page, a form handler or a server action is a thin door that asks the engine. This is already how every step is built; keep it that way.
- **Every "who is this" question goes through `getCurrentActor()`.** An app will sign in differently (a long-lived key kept on the phone instead of a browser cookie, and a sign-in email link that opens the app), and that must be a change inside that one function.
- **Refusals stay plain sentences made by the engine**, so an app can show exactly the words the website shows.
- **Anything a person can do on a screen is an engine function first.** A native app cannot use a web page's buttons, so each action will later be offered as a plain request-and-answer interface (an API) in front of the same engine functions.
- **No payment inside the app, as today** (dues and charges go through QuickBooks), which keeps the store rules simple.

What an app would add, and what it would need (decide when it is scoped, and read the stores' current rules first, because they change):
- **A plain request-and-answer interface** next to the pages, for the app to talk to, with the same staff-only and member-only checks. Tested like the pages, including "not found" on the live site until switch-on.
- **Notifications on the phone.** This would answer open questions N, T and X (today nobody is told when a waiting list moves them up, an event changes, or their place is about to run out). Needs a sender and a list of each person's phones.
- **Sign-in from the app** (link opens the app; the phone keeps its own key; "sign out everywhere", open question AM, becomes needed).
- **Store requirements.** Both stores want a privacy policy and a description of what is collected. Apple's rules have required that anyone who can create an account can **ask for its deletion from inside the app**; today only staff end a household (AX), so a "request to end my membership" button would be needed. A bare wrapper around the website can be refused as too thin, so the app should do something a phone does better (notifications, tap-to-check-in, quick booking).
- **Money.** Apple and Google charge for developer accounts (Apple yearly, Google once), and the accounts must belong to the club, not to one person (question E). Stop and ask before paying for anything.
- **The check-in decision may be revisited** (AE: the phone button trusts the member and checks no location). An app could scan a dock code or check location if that ever becomes a problem.
- **The front-desk iPad** could become a locked-down app instead of a private web address; the private-address design (AF) works until then.

Ways to build it, for later (no choice made): an **installable version of the website** (cheapest, but not in the stores), a **thin store app around the website** (fast, listed in the stores, but risks being refused as too thin), or a **real phone app** written in TypeScript sharing the club's rules code (the most work, the best fit for the stores). The rules engine is already TypeScript, which makes the third option natural.

## Testing (required; nothing is done until all of this passes)

CI runs on every push and pull request: install, lint, type check, unit tests, build, browser tests.

- **End-to-end core flow:** pick a test member, find a free single, book it, see it in My bookings, cancel it, see the slot free again. Second flow: book a double with a qualified crewmate.
- **Rule edge tests:** exactly at the booking window and one minute past; max length and one step over; per-member limit and one over; unqualified member on a restricted boat; booking during an out-of-service period.
- **Repeated and realistic use:** book, cancel, rebook many times and limits still count right; many members fill a whole day; the window rolls over at midnight; twenty simultaneous attempts on one boat and slot produce exactly one booking.
- **Household tests:** ending a household blocks every member, cancels future bookings, logs every change, and leaves past history intact.
- **Daylight saving:** bookings on Nov 1, 2026 and the days around it.
- **Safety:** the picker and `/club` are absent in a production setting. No dashboard page shows member names. Dashboard tests pass unchanged.
- **Member check-in:** the window (30 minutes before to the end, and one minute either side); only people on the reservation; one outcome per outing with two taps at once (two app servers too); a member cannot undo a staff decision; events, the club room and cancelled reservations never; the iPad lists only people with something open and drops them once done; a wrong iPad address or a forged button finds nothing; the iPad shows no menu; it returns to its start by itself; the phone button on a small screen.
- **Signing in:** a link only for an address that can sign in, with the same answer for everyone else; a link works once, briefly, and looking at it records nothing; partners are asked who they are and the device remembers; a wrong choice or a forged form changes nothing; ten presses of one link sign in once (on both stores); a household ending, an adult leaving or a staff address coming off the list ends their browsers at once; staff appear under their own names; posts from other sites are refused; the session cookie cannot be read by scripts; signing out really ends the key; every page and handler is "not found" on the live site.
- **Editing details:** every detail changes and is recorded with old and new values and who did it; nothing else about the person changes; a phone, name or capital-letter change signs nobody out and an email change signs out every device of that person only (partners on the shared address keep theirs); the old address opens nothing and the new one works, including a link sent to the old address earlier; a refused change (nothing changed, a mistake, not staff, someone who has left, a household that ended) signs nobody out and writes nothing; a failed sign-out changes nothing; a sign-in between the two sign-outs is still ended; the sentence fits the file at full length; simultaneous corrections each leave one line in order; staff only, including a member trying their own record; a narrow screen.
- **Roster import:** the reader on each kind of mistake with its row number (every problem at once, never just the first), the file formats a spreadsheet really writes (quoted cells, any line endings, the invisible marker, spaces and capitals in headers, `1/5/2020` dates); the plan changes nothing however often it is run; the run is staff only, adds exactly what was previewed on both stores, adds nothing when anything is wrong, when the file or the club changed since the preview, or when everything is already here; a second upload skips what is here (matched by QuickBooks ID, also when that household has ended) and never changes, ends or removes anyone; two staff confirming at once add once; 300 households in one go and ten rounds of uploads; an imported adult can sign in; the server action refuses the member area being off, a member, nobody signed in, no file, a big file, an unknown button and an Add that was not checked; on the screen the preview, the problems with their rows, the 50-household list limit, a stale preview cleared by a new file, the template download (and its example rows refused) and a narrow phone; and the page, template and action (called with its real ID) are "page not found" on the live site.
- **Public site:** every page shows its heading, the preview banner and the footer and is noindex; the menu lists the five pages and Sign in and every link goes where it says, over several laps; the Sign in button reaches the member sign-in; Membership shows both plans with their prices; Contact shows working phone and email links; the two placeholder pages say they are placeholders and no page shows anyone's own email address; no member, household or booking information appears; unknown pages are 404; the content file refuses each kind of mistake in words; on a phone nothing scrolls sideways and the menu can be tapped; and on the production-style servers every page answers "page not found" with none of its words or its title.
- **Billing (step 9):** charges are staff only; a sale is never negative; an adjustment needs a reason and may be a credit under any line; nothing is dated in the future, in a month already closed, or before the records loaded; voiding needs a reason, keeps the line and stops once the month is closed; visitors: staff for the desk, a member only for themselves on the iPad, today only, 1 to 12 at a time and 30 a day, a member who left cannot, voided entries are not counted, and the fee exactly at 4, 5 and more; the month: dues for everyone who was a member on the 1st (joined on the 1st, ended on the 1st, ended during the month, type changed on or after the 1st), event tickets (each kind of RSVP, guests, the price at RSVP, cancelled and free events, the month the event ends), visitor fees per member per day, sales added up per household and kind, adjustments as their own lines, credits; the plan changes nothing however often it is run; a closed month cannot be planned or closed again, months go in order, a month cannot be closed before it has finished, and a household with something to pay and no QuickBooks ID stops it; the run is staff only, refuses a month that changed since the preview, and two staff closing at once close it once (on both stores); the files: header, dates, due dates, names, parts at exactly 100 invoices and 999 rows, credits left out and counted, invoices that are all credits, the plain file with its flags, formula protection, and the same bytes on every download; the database rules each broken on purpose; the server actions (staff only, plain words, nothing saved on a mistake); the screens: charges, void, visitors, the iPad (three letters before anything is listed, at most five active members, the count, the fee words, the entry and day limits, forged buttons and wrong addresses, the return to the start), the QuickBooks ID card, the preview with its problems, notices and every line, closing (a stale preview is caught and looked at again), the closed month (files, same bytes, nothing more can be dated in it), October refused, and a phone-sized screen; and every page, download, button and server action (each called with its real ID from the build) is "page not found" on the live site.
- **Old bookings (step 10):** the reader on every kind of mistake with its row number (every problem at once, capped at 200), the formats a spreadsheet or iCrew really writes (BOM, quoted cells, LF endings, `MM-DD-YYYY`, `4:00pm`, 12 o'clock, 12:00am as the end of the day, both clock-change days), later days left out, repeats kept once, an event's shared ID kept per person; names (accents, apostrophes, hyphens, middle names, Jr., nicknames in brackets, seat numbers, joint bookings, a shared last name); the plan changes nothing however often it is run; the run is staff only, adds exactly what was previewed, refuses a file with problems, another file with the same number of rows, and a changed archive, and adds nothing twice (also six at once, overlapping files at once, and across two app servers); 3,000 bookings in one go and 23,000 (3.7 MB) through the browser; a member sees only their own, newest first, 50 a time, with no one else's name, and nothing at all when another member (including a former one) has the same name; staff search, the names list, and the sample archive; the database rules each broken on purpose (including the Pacific day from any connection time zone); and the page, template and server action are "page not found" on the live site (the action called with its real ID).
- **Resignations:** the reader on every kind of mistake with its row number (an empty or over-long ID, a template example row, a name or note over the limit, an ID listed twice, an empty row skipped), the formats a spreadsheet writes (the invisible marker, quoted cells, any line endings, columns in any order and under other names), and a file that is not a CSV, has no QuickBooks ID column, or is over the size or row limit (exactly at it and one over); the read-back changes nothing however often it is run, lists every unknown ID at once, never ends a household whose name is not the one on file (and ignores spacing and capitals when it is), skips households that have already ended and does not count them against the limit, stops a file exactly one over the limit, warns at exactly the percent and not below, and names the next month for dues across a year end; the run is staff only, ends exactly what was previewed (including a boat booked by one resigning household with crew from the other and a waiting list that runs through three households, with a bystander moving up), leaves exactly the records that ending the same households one at a time leaves (on both stores), writes each household's file with its note, signs the adults out, ends nothing from a file with a problem or when the file or the club changed since the check (a different file, different counts, an adult removed, a household ended by hand), ends once when three people confirm at once, ends none of them when one fails part-way, and handles forty households in one go and four at a time ten times over; the server action refuses the member area being off, a member, nobody signed in, no file, a big file, an unknown button and an end that was not checked; on the screen the read-back with its cards and notices, the problems with their rows, a stale read-back cleared by a new file, a household ended by hand after the check refused, skipped households, the cap of 50 and the 50-line limit on problems, three rounds in a row, the template download (and its example rows refused) and a phone-sized screen;
