and the page, template and action (called with its real ID) are "page not found" on the live site.

- All external services are mocked. Tests never touch the live Supabase project or any real service.
- The engine tests run on **both stores** (memory, and Postgres when `CLUB_TEST_DATABASE_URL` is set). Anything the engine does must give the same answer on each. The booking browser tests also run against a Postgres-backed server (Playwright project `postgres`).
- Database rules are tested against a real Postgres (`CLUB_TEST_DATABASE_URL`; CI provides a throwaway one). Without that variable those tests are skipped and say so.

## Open questions

Numbers 2, 5 and 8 keep the numbering from the lost Oct 20 file.

- ~~2. Build path~~ Answered Oct 22: full replacement on Vercel.
- 5. Real booking-rule values (every number in the step 2 table is a placeholder). Waiting on staff.
- ~~8. Juniors~~ Answered Oct 22: out of scope.
- A. Can an ended household come back? Phase 1 says no: a returning household is a new record, so the old history stays closed. Needs Grant's confirmation.
- B. Real qualification names and levels. Phase 1 uses placeholder sculling levels 1 to 3 plus sweep.
- ~~C. A shared email (partners on one address)~~ Answered Oct 23 and built: after the link the page asks "Which of you?" and the device remembers the answer; each partner has their own session.
- D. When the production switch-on happens: the date the `/club` area becomes visible on the production site, and who approves it.
- E. Who gets which Vercel/Supabase/GitHub accounts under the CCS-owned email. Also (Oct 25): who holds collegeclubseattle.com's settings (DNS). Grant: someone else holds it, not him; the email service needs a few records added there.
- ~~G. Postgres-backed store and racing requests~~ Answered Oct 22: built. Changes take turns under one database-wide lock, so limits and "two boats" are checked against fresh data. If the club ever gets busy enough for that to be a bottleneck (hundreds of simultaneous bookings), the lock can be narrowed to per-member and per-item locks; not needed now.
- ~~H. Unmarked past outings~~ Answered Oct 23: a boat or space booking nobody checked in for becomes a no-show automatically when it ends; staff can correct it afterwards (step 5).
- I. What does a late cancellation lead to (a warning after N, a fee, nothing)? Phase 1 only records it.
- J. When a member leaves, other people's boats lose that crew member but keep the booking. Confirm that is what staff want, or whether the owner should be told.
- K. Real class rule values. Every number in `class-rules.json` is a placeholder (open question 5 covers boats).
- ~~L. Staff overriding class rules~~ Answered Oct 22: no. Staff cannot sign a member into a class past the rules; they raise the chairs instead.
- ~~M. Unmarked past classes~~ Answered Oct 23: same as H, a class chair nobody checked in for becomes a no-show when the class ends.
- ~~N. Telling people when the waiting list moves them up~~ Answered Oct 22: fine for now. Step 3 shows it in My classes only; email or text comes in a later step.
- O. A late cancellation of a class is only recorded (same as question I for boats).
- P. Real event rule values. Every number in `event-rules.json` is a placeholder.
- Q. A forwarded email link lets whoever has it answer for that one adult for that one event, and nothing else. There is no way to switch off one person's links; rotating `CLUB_LINK_SECRET` switches off everyone's. Is that acceptable for a pilot? And who holds the real secret (open question E)?
- ~~R. "Ticketed"~~ Answered Oct 23 (A): a price per spot is recorded on each RSVP and the household is billed later with its dues (QuickBooks CSV, step 9). Nothing is collected at RSVP.
- ~~S. Who gets freed spots~~ Answered Oct 23: the first party in line **that fits** goes ahead; a bigger party keeps its place in line until it fits. No spots sit empty.
- T. Nobody is told when an event changes or is cancelled, or when the waiting list moves them up. Step 4 shows it on the event page only. Email or text comes later.
- ~~U. A late cancellation of an event is only recorded~~ Answered Oct 24 (step 9): a late-cancelled RSVP is billed, the whole party at the price recorded at RSVP. Late cancellations of boats and classes are still only recorded (I, O).
- ~~V. When the one-hour hold starts~~ Answered Oct 23: from the member's last button press (join, change party, Keep my place), not from opening the page. Still open, small: staff-added waitlist entries (none exist yet) should also run out? Decide when step 5 lets staff add people.
- ~~W. One-hour hold for classes~~ Answered Oct 23: no. Class waiting lists never run out; revisit once events have been in use.
- X. With no emails, nobody is told their place is about to run out; they see the time on the event page and in the joined note. Email or text comes later (same as T).
- ~~Y. Events and automatic no-shows~~ Answered Oct 23: staff mark events. Unmarked events show "Awaiting attendance record" until they do.
- Z. Check-in opens 30 minutes before the start and an unchecked booking becomes a no-show the moment it ends. Both are placeholders in `attendance-rules.json`. Grant's reply to the grace-period question was about phone check-in and exempt reservations instead, so there is no grace period (`noShowAfterEndMinutes` is 0); staff can correct a late tap. Confirm, or set a grace period.
- ~~AA. Correction limit~~ Answered Oct 23: staff can correct an outcome any time, with no reason required (every correction is on the record).
- ~~AB. Crew~~ Answered Oct 23: one outcome per outing (a check-in by anyone on it, or by staff for any of them, marks it attended).
- ~~AC. Member check-in~~ Answered Oct 23 and built: the iPad lists only people with something to check in for right now, with a box to type the first letters of a name, at a private address; the phone has an "I'm here" button that trusts the member and records "from their phone". Two people with the same name are told apart by household; people sharing an email are picked by name, so the shared email does not matter.
- ~~AE. Phone taps trusted from anywhere~~ Answered Oct 23: keep it simple. Staff see "from their phone" and can correct it. Add a location or dock-code check later only if it becomes a problem.
- ~~AF. The iPad's private address~~ Answered Oct 23: the private address alone is fine for the pilot (no staff PIN, no Guided Access). Anyone who has the address can use the iPad screens; rotating `CLUB_IPAD_SECRET` switches it off. Still open, small: who holds the real secret (question E).
- ~~AG. Undoing a mistaken tap~~ Answered Oct 23: staff only. A member cannot undo a tap; staff correct it and the correction is on the record.
- ~~AH. Crew taps~~ Answered Oct 23: one tap checks the whole outing in (one outcome per outing, AB). The record says who tapped.
- AI. The real email sender for sign-in links. **Service chosen Oct 25: Resend** (Grant picked it over Postmark, Brevo and Amazon SES). Resend's own pricing page: free for 3,000 emails a month but only 100 a day; Pro is $20 a month for 50,000 with no daily cap, which is wanted for the month around cutover when everyone signs in at once. Nothing is bought and no account exists yet. Still open: who creates the Resend account (under the CCS-owned shared email, question E), the address it sends from (for example a no-reply address on the club's domain), and the DNS records the domain needs (Grant: someone else holds the domain, so a request to forward is in the numbers sheet's checklist). **Built Oct 27** (see "The real email sender"): the sender reads `RESEND_API_KEY`, `CLUB_MAIL_FROM` and `CLUB_MAIL_REPLY_TO` from private settings, is never chosen where invented data is allowed, and is tested against a pretend Resend; replies go to the public membership address, and `npm run mail:test -- address` proves it before any member signs in. Still open: the account, the real From address and the DNS records (above), and turning off link tracking. `CLUB_SITE_URL` must be set when real email goes out.
- ~~AJ. Login rule values~~ Answered Oct 23: keep 15-minute links, 30-day sessions and 5 links an hour for now. They are still placeholders in `login-rules.json` and easy to change.
- ~~AK. A member whose email on file is wrong cannot sign in~~ Answered Oct 23 and built: staff edit a person's name, email and phone; a change of email signs the person out of every device; the household's file records old and new values and who changed it. Changing the email does **not** ask the old address to confirm (staff are the front desk and the old address may be the mistake).
- ~~AO. Members changing their own details~~ Answered Oct 23: staff only. A member who needs a change asks staff. Revisit if staff are asked too often (a member changing only their own phone would be the first thing to allow).
- AP. The household's file, which every adult in the household can read, now holds old and new emails and phone numbers. Fine for a pilot (the same household page already shows each adult's email and phone), but confirm before real members.
- ~~AQ. The About and Rowing and fitness text~~ Answered Oct 23: use placeholder information for now. Both pages stay as placeholders built only from sentences on the club's public pages, each with a visible preview note. When the real text is ready, staff (or Grant) paste it from the members-only Club information, Programs and Rowing pages and remove the notes.
- ~~AR. Directors on the Contact page~~ Answered Oct 23: **no** emails for the directors on the public site; an info email or a web form is preferred. Their names and roles stay; the one public email is the membership address (membership@collegeclubseattle.com, already public on the current site). Still open, small: if the club has a general info address, swap it in (one line in `site.json`); the web form comes with the real email sender (AI).
- ~~AS. Prices on the Membership page~~ Answered Oct 23: the two plans, prices and initiation fees copied from the current public page are current. The system's own membership types are Resident (33 and over), Intermediate (under 33) and Non-Resident; the public page says Intermediate is for ages 21 to 32 and to ask about non-resident rates.

Who prepares the real roster file~~ Answered Oct 24: staff assemble it in a spreadsheet from the QuickBooks customer list and wherever the adults' details are kept, starting from the blank template (the import expects its columns, see Step 7); the preview catches mistakes before anything is saved. Nothing to build.
- ~~AV. Re-uploads~~ Answered Oct 24: a second upload only adds. A household already here is never changed, so a corrected email or a changed membership type goes through the household desk. Grant's follow-up, now question AX: if the import only adds, the removal process when memberships resign needs thinking through.
- ~~AX. Resignations~~ Answered Oct 27 and built: a file staff upload (upload, read-back, confirm), households found by QuickBooks ID, ending right away when staff confirm, all or nothing, through the same code as the household desk (see "Resignations"). Grant: 6 to 20 households resign in a typical month. Where the list comes from and who prepares it is BO; the two typo-guard numbers and who presses the button are BN.
- ~~AW. History starts at the import~~ Answered Oct 24: fine for now. Households in the file are added as active from their join date; the household's file starts at the import (a line saying it was added from a roster file, with the join date), not at the real join date.
- ~~AL. Who edits the staff list~~ Answered Oct 23: whoever owns the site's settings (question E), in Vercel's private settings; a change needs a redeploy. No staff screen for it.
- AM. Should a member be able to sign out all their devices, and should staff be able to end someone's sessions? Not built.
- AN. When the member area is switched on for real members (question D), the picker and the test outbox stay off automatically; check that the real sender, `CLUB_SITE_URL` and the staff list are in place first.
- AY. **Sales tax.** Grant (Oct 25): **charge no taxes at all**, snacks and drinks included, because his monthly statements show no tax. Built: the `taxable` list in `billing-rules.json` is empty, so no line carries tax; the mechanism stays (naming a kind in the list marks its lines taxable again) and is tested with a taxed fixture. Two things for the bookkeeper (they are in the numbers-to-confirm sheet): (1) a statement with no tax line does not prove none is owed, since tax may be inside the price, rung up at the register or paid by the club, and bar and prepared food are generally taxable in Washington (Seattle's combined rate was 10.55% in Q3 2026 per the Department of Revenue and can change each quarter); this is a question for the bookkeeper or the state, not advice from me; (2) QuickBooks Online's invoice import is **unavailable when automated sales tax is turned on** in the account. If it is on, the plain charges file is the fallback and the invoices are entered another way.
- AZ. **Real billing numbers and names.** Every number and name in `billing-rules.json` is a placeholder: the Non-Resident dues amount (invented), the free visitors per day and the fee, and the QuickBooks product or service names (QuickBooks matches them exactly, including any category prefix) and customer names (each household's name must be the same in QuickBooks), and the 15-day due date. Waiting on staff and the bookkeeper (same as question 5).
- BA. **Visitor names.** Only a count is kept. If the club ever wants to know who visitors were (safety, repeat guests), the iPad would have to ask for names. Not built.
- BB. **Bar and snack-bar detail.** Each household gets one bar line and one snack-bar line a month; the individual charges and their notes stay on the Charges screen and are in neither file. Fine for now?
- BC. **Late-cancelled tickets.** A late-cancelled RSVP is billed even if its spots were then given to the waiting list (Grant's rule: everyone who held spots when it was too late to cancel). Confirm, or bill only when the spots stayed empty.
- BD. **Members seeing their bill.** Members cannot see charges or invoices in this system; the QuickBooks invoice is what they see. A "my charges" page could come later.
- BE. **Who closes a month, and when.** Any staff member can close a month once it has finished. Suggestion: one named person, on the 1st. Not enforced.
- BF. **The app: first version and timing.** What should the first Apple and Android app do (members booking boats and classes, RSVPs, tap-to-check-in, notifications; staff tools later?), and should it wait for the cutover (D) or come before? Which of the three ways to build it (see "Phone apps")? Not decided.
- BG. **App store accounts and cost.** Who owns the Apple Developer and Google Play accounts (same question as E: the CCS-owned email, not one person), and who approves the yearly and one-time fees. Nothing is bought until Grant says so.
- BH. **Account deletion and the privacy policy for the stores.** Apple expects people to be able to start deleting their account from inside the app; today only staff end a household. What should "I want to leave the club" do in an app (a request to staff, tied to AX)? Who writes and owns the privacy policy page?
- BI. **The cutover tools (the other half of step 10).** Not scoped. What should they be: a checklist screen, a "switch on" step with its checks, a dry run, a way to bring over reservations that are still in the future, a freeze on new iCrew bookings? Ask Grant before building (the dashboard's home address and the domain are separate decisions, AT and E).
- BJ. **Reservations that are still in the future when the club switches over.** The archive only keeps days that have passed. Boat bookings move all at once, so the future ones in iCrew need a plan (bring them over as real bookings, or ask members to rebook). Not built.
- BK. **Earlier years of iCrew history.** Grant said 2026 only for now. A bigger archive is the same upload in pieces (4 MB at a time); nothing to build unless the files turn out too big for one request.
- ~~BL. What iCrew's Out and In columns mean~~ **Reopened Oct 30 by the iCrew crawl (CM).** Answered Oct 27: keep treating them as the booked times (Grant, after being told they differ from the booked times on most rows of the real report). Old bookings stay "reserved" and are never shown as "attended". Revisit if the club ever wants to show who actually used a boat.
- BM. **Name matching.** iCrew has names, no emails. A member is matched when their first and last word equal the old name's, and only if no other member (past or present) has the same key. A nickname in brackets is ignored, but "Bob" for "Robert" will not match and shows on staff's list. Confirm that is acceptable, or decide how staff should match the leftovers (not built: staff can see them, not link them).
- BN. **Resignation guard numbers, and who presses the button.** `maxHouseholdsPerFile` (50) and `warnPercentOfActive` (25) in `resignation-rules.json` are placeholders. Any staff member can end memberships from a file, as with closing a month (BE). Suggestion: one named person, at month end and before the 1st so no further dues are billed. Not enforced.
- BO. **Where the resignation list comes from in practice.** Grant chose a file staff upload; who builds it and from what (QuickBooks customers marked inactive? a list from the board?) is for staff to settle. A "who's missing" report (compare a fresh QuickBooks list to the club and show who is not on it, ending nothing) was offered and not chosen; revisit if building the file turns out to be hard.
- BP. **Failed sign-in emails are invisible.** If Resend refuses (domain not verified, quota, a wrong key), the member sees the same "a link is on its way" page as always, and the only trace is a line in the server log. That is deliberate for strangers (it must look the same for every address), but staff have no alert. Do staff want one (for example a count of failed sends on the Staff office page), or is the test command before switch-on enough? Not built.
- BQ. **Resend's link and open tracking.** Must be off for the club's domain, or a sign-in link is rewritten through Resend's servers. Nothing in the code can check this; it is a go-live item (in the numbers sheet's checklist once the account exists).
- BR. **Which other emails next.** The sender can now carry more than the sign-in link: a waiting-list move-up or event change (N, T, X), a note to a person whose email address staff changed (AK), a note to a household whose membership ended. Which, if any, first, and what each should say? Not built.
- ~~BS. Where the new homepage lives~~ Answered Oct 29 and built: the home of `/site`, hidden on the live site; moved to `/` at cutover with the dashboard's new address (AT, D).
- ~~BT. The iCrew card on the member sign-in screen~~ Answered Oct 29: left out.
- ~~BU. The "since" claim in the hero~~ Answered Oct 29: "A Seattle institution since 1910".
- ~~BV. Where "Request a tour" goes~~ Answered Oct 29 and built: a real form, requests saved and shown on a staff Tour requests screen, and emailed to the membership address once real email is on.
- BW. **The logo files, the hero photo and the real "How to join" steps 1 and 2.** The two logo PNGs did not arrive as files; the hero photo is a labelled placeholder until Grant supplies one; steps 1 and 2 are the designer's wording until the club confirms its process. Waiting on Grant.
- BX. **What the Request a tour form promises, and how long requests are kept.** The form says nothing about privacy, and tour requests are never removed (the database refuses). Does the club want a line under the form ("we only use this to arrange your visit"), a retention period, and a way to delete or export a request? Who follows up, and how fast? Not built.
- BY. **The notice email for a new tour request** goes to the membership address (or `CLUB_TOUR_NOTIFY_TO`) only once the real email sender is switched on (AI); until then staff must look at the Tour requests screen. Confirm that is acceptable for the pilot, or whether a different address should be told.
- BZ. **"The Weekly Watercooler"** is named in the sign-in band by the designer. This system has no such thing yet (the old site has a weekly email archive). Confirm the name and where members should find it, or the line changes.
- CA. **The menu between 768 and 899 pixels** keeps the Menu button (six links and the Member login pill do not fit on one line before 900). Fine, or should the inline menu start at 768 with smaller type?
- CB. **The real equipment list.** The starter sheet went to Grant on Oct 30. Waiting on staff to fill in seats, skill level, private or club-owned and in service, to confirm the pool sizes (60 dock places, 49 club places, 8 gym places), and to say which of the 11 shells and 3 kayaks not booked since before August are retired.
- ~~CC. Private boats~~ Answered Oct 30 and built the same night (see "Private boats"): **owners plus the people they name** can book a privately owned boat (63 of the 124 individual items in the report look private).
- ~~CD. Pools~~ Answered Oct 30 and built the same day (see "Pools"): one pool per kind for dock, club room and gym spaces, counted rather than numbered.
- ~~CE. Cardio machines~~ Answered Oct 30: left out (8 machines, 1,073 bookings in 2026). Revisit if staff want them reserved.
- CF. **Editing or retiring an item.** The equipment upload only adds, and nothing else can change or retire an item that is already here, so a wrong seat count or skill level from a first upload needs a data fix. Is an "edit or retire an item" tool wanted before switch-on? Not built.
- CG. **Private boats: things assumed, for Grant to confirm.** (1) Taking someone off a boat **cancels their upcoming bookings on it** (the button says how many first), and takes them out of other people's outings on it. (2) **Staff cannot book someone onto a private boat for a lesson**: it is the owner's say, not a club rule, so the "book past the rules" override does not cover it; a coach would be invited first. (3) An owner can invite **any active member, with no limit on how many**. (4) Only staff change the owners; an owner cannot take another owner off, and an invited person cannot leave the list themselves. (5) A boat whose owner left the club is locked, but **bookings invited people already made stay** (staff are shown the count). (6) Nobody is told by email when they are invited or taken off (no sender for that yet, BR).
- CH. **Private boats in the real equipment sheet.** The Owner emails column (or full names) must name members who exist in the system, so the real roster import (step 7) has to come before the real equipment upload, or the owners are added on the Private boats screen afterwards. About 60 boats look private in the report; who owns each is for the owners and staff to say. Waiting on CB.
- CI. **Pools: things assumed, for Grant to confirm.** Grant did not pick answers to these four (he sent the iCrew idea instead), so the recommended ones were built: (1) a class running in a pool's room **takes the whole room**; (2) members see **only how many places are left**, never a place number; (3) **one place per person at a time** in a pool (staff cannot set it aside); (4) the starting sizes are **dock 60, club room 49, gym 8** (placeholders from the highest numbers seen; to be confirmed). Also assumed: a pool's places are fixed when it is made (no editing until the edit-or-retire tool, CF); dock, gym and club room share the boat placeholders (a pool might need a longer day pass; question 5).
- ~~CJ. Damage and incident reports~~ (Grant, Oct 30: first among the missing pieces.) **Damage reports built Oct 30** (see "Damage reports"; merged as PR 40). **The incident (injury, capsize, near-miss) report built Oct 31** (see "Incident reports"). The assumptions are question CS.
- ~~CK. Booking numbers from iCrew~~ Answered Oct 30 and built the same day (see "Booking numbers from iCrew"): 14 days ahead, 90 minutes by default and up to 4 hours, no limit per member, no gap. They are still placeholders until staff confirm them (question 5). Private boats follow the rules like any boat; with no limit, iCrew's exemption changes nothing (Grant to say if he wants it built for when a limit is set).
- ~~CL. Lineups for coach-run sessions~~ Answered Oct 30 and built the same day (see "Practices and lineups"): a coach is staff plus members staff mark; optional; a practice reserves its boats; an earlier booking is flagged and not cancelled; on screen only; no skill check. The assumptions are question CR.
- CM. **Out and In times.** iCrew has an On-the-Water log (tap to check out and in, editable times), so the Out and In columns of the Reservations report are probably real times, not the booked times: **this reopens BL** (answered Oct 27: treated as booked times). Undecided: whether to record real out and in times and hours per boat, and whether to keep the old Out and In as actual times when old bookings are imported.
- CN. **Waivers and certifications that expire** (not chosen first). iCrew holds about 540 water-sports waivers and flip-test, launch and bow certifications with expiry dates, and can block a member until a waiver is on file. We have skill levels with no expiry and no waivers. Who keeps waivers after switch-over is open.
- CO. **Later, if wanted:** oars chosen with the boat (64 sets), weight range per boat, sweep and scull rigging, inspection dates, Club Insights style charts, an inactive members list, regatta trailer lists.
- CP. **The real fleet list and iCrew's Nov 1 change.** iCrew's admin lists (Shells 98, Oars 64, Assets 366, with seats, weights, owners) could fill most of the equipment sheet (CB) if Grant downloads them: read layouts and counts only, never print a name. **iCrew retires its classic portal on Nov 1, 2026**: menus and export layouts may change, so any exports wanted should be taken this week (the old-bookings reader, `engine/icrew.ts`, may need a new look if the Reservations report layout changes).
- CQ. **Damage reports: things assumed, for Grant to confirm.** (1) Everyone who books a boat reads "what is wrong" and "where" for its open reports (never who filed it or how it happened); the form tells the filer so. (2) A member can file 10 reports in 24 hours (a placeholder); staff are not limited. (3) A report starts at priority 1 when marked major and 3 when minor; staff can change it. (4) "Who is fixing it" is free text, not a person in the system. (5) Hours and cost are for staff only; the filer sees status and repair date. (6) A closed report can never be reopened or edited; a new report is filed if the damage is back. (7) A major report never blocks bookings by itself, even for the people who already booked the boat (staff press the button and see who to contact). (8) The filer can add notes while the report is open; no one else can except staff. (9) No photos and no QR code per boat yet; no emails when a report is filed or repaired (BR). (10) The incident (injury) report is the second round (CJ).
- CR. **Practices and lineups: things assumed, for Grant to confirm.** (1) A coach sees every practice and can change only the ones they run; staff change any. A practice staff run can be given a coach when it is set up and not afterwards. (2) A coach sees the **names** of members whose earlier bookings overlap a practice, never their email or phone; staff see both. (3) A booking made before the practice stays, even on the practice's own boat, and the booker is only shown a notice; but the coach **cannot seat that same person** while they have a boat booked then (they would be in two places), so staff or the member cancel the booking first. (4) A private boat cannot go on a practice. (5) The lineup can change until the practice ends; a practice can be cancelled only before it starts; its name, note and times never change (cancel and set up again). (6) **No cox seat and no eights yet**: seats are numbered 1 to the boat's seats, each with port, starboard or no side. (7) A person is in one seat of a practice; moving someone is taking them out and seating them again. (8) A seated member who leaves the club keeps a line the coach has to clear; it says "(no longer an active member)". (9) Nobody is emailed or texted; rowers see their seat on My practices and My bookings. (10) The practice numbers (80-character name, six hours, 20 boats, 120 days ahead) are placeholders in `practice-rules.json`. (11) The coach flag's database rule accepts any earlier line saying the same thing about that member, like the editing-details rule. (12) Practices are not checked in or marked attended (no no-show, no out and in times: CM).
- CS. **Incident reports: things assumed, for Grant to confirm.** (1) A coach sees only the reports they filed, with their own notes, and can add notes while a report is open; staff see every report. (2) A coach never sees the cause, what will change, who is following up, staff's notes or what was done. (3) "Entered the water" is not a separate box (iCrew has one): say it in what happened. (4) An incident can be dated up to 90 days back, a report can name 20 people and 10 boats, and every box has a limit: all placeholders in `incident-rules.json`. (5) Any boat or space can be named (a coach only ones they may see). (6) Nobody is emailed or texted when a report is filed, reviewed or closed (BR); staff see new ones on the Staff office board. (7) An incident never appears on a boat's page, a booking or a practice, and cannot be linked to a practice. (8) A closed report can never be reopened or edited; if it happens again, a new one is filed, but staff can still add a note to the log of a closed one. (9) **Keep incident reports for 18 months** (Grant, Oct 31). Nothing deletes them yet: the database refuses removal and there is no export or delete tool, so today they are kept longer than that. What happens at 18 months, and who may press the button, is question CU.
- CT. **The honor snack bar iPad: things assumed, for Grant to confirm.** Grant's request was complete, so no plan questions were asked and these were built on the recommended answers. (1) A snack purchase is **one charge on the member's own household's account**, on the member's own tab, dated today; the household's invoice shows it inside the month's one **Snack bar** line (QuickBooks "Snack Bar Sales"), counted as "N charges" (the count of purchases, not snacks); the iPad keeps the count and the price of each purchase and staff see them on the Charges screen. (2) **Anyone at the iPad can pick any member's name** (an honor system, the same as signing visitors in); a wrong or mischievous one is voided by staff with a reason; the screen shows no totals, only the thank-you for what was just added. (3) **A member cannot undo a purchase**; staff void it on the Charges screen (as for every charge). (4) The snack bar has **its own private address**, different from the check-in iPad's and made from the same secret, so nothing new needs setting up; changing `CLUB_IPAD_SECRET` switches both off. (5) The limits are placeholders in `snack-rules.json`: **20 snacks a press, 40 a member a day**, the thank-you shows 8 seconds, the screen returns to the start after 30 quiet seconds. (6) Only the one flat price: no list of different snacks, no stock count, no receipts or emails, no "my snacks this month" page for members. (7) No sales tax, as for every line (AY). (8) Keeping the iPad on that one screen (Guided Access or a kiosk setting on the iPad) is for whoever sets the iPad up; the screen itself shows no menu and no links out.
- CU. **What happens to an incident report at 18 months.** Grant (Oct 31): keep them for 18 months. Not built, and not decided: whether they are then deleted (by a staff tool, after an export), exported and then deleted, or just left in place; who may press the button; and whether a report that someone is following up, or that is linked to a damage report, is kept longer. The database refuses to remove a report today, so a deletion tool needs its own plan and Grant's say-so (stop and ask before deleting data). The same question is open for tour requests (BX).
- ~~CV. A shared database for previews~~ Answered Nov 2 (Grant: yes, a free throwaway database, previews only) and built the same day (see "Previews with a shared database"). A Vercel preview without `CLUB_DATABASE_URL` keeps its records in each server instance's memory (see "After the merge" under the snack bar). Still open: Grant creating the free database and putting its address into Vercel's Preview settings (this session's Vercel access cannot read or create settings, 403, but can redeploy), and whether the account should sit under the CCS-owned email (question E).
- ~~AD. Which kinds of booking need no check-in?~~ Answered Oct 23: only the club room and work-from-club, so `checkInNotNeededFor` holds Room Space and Work Space. Boats, kayaks, paddle boards, Dock Space and Gym Space still need a check-in. Easy to change later (one line). There is no Work Space item or class in the invented club yet.
- ~~F. Next.js security advisory~~ Answered Oct 22: Grant merged the upgrade to 16.4.2 (PR 11) before this system was merged. The random "version is affected" build failure is gone.

## Session log

### Oct 22, 2026
- Read the repo (README, AGENTS.md, `src/lib`, `db/`, tests, CI). Found CLAUDE.md missing from every branch; Grant asked for a new one from the kickoff prompt (this file).
- Plan agreed with Grant (answers listed under Decisions above).
- **Built (step 1):**
  - Dashboard pages moved into a `(dashboard)` route group, URLs unchanged; the 52 existing browser tests pass untouched.
  - Member area at `/club`: test-member picker (`/club/act-as`), household page with adults, qualifications and history, and a staff list of all households. Read only.
  - `src/club/`: household rules engine (join, add and remove an adult, change type, grant a qualification, end a membership all-or-nothing), booking-eligibility check, in-memory store, 14 invented households, the single `getCurrentActor()` function, and the on/off switch.
  - Database migration `supabase/migrations/20261022000000_club_households.sql` (schema `club`), with an append-only history table, one-primary-per-household, staff-only qualification grants, and `club.end_household()`. Tested against a real Postgres, and the tests were shown to fail when the migration is deliberately broken.
  - CI: Node 24, a throwaway Postgres service, database tests that cannot be skipped in CI, and two extra production-style servers that prove `/club` and the picker do not exist on the live site.
- **Decided / assumed (Grant to confirm in review):**
  - Removing an adult from a household also blocks them and cancels their future bookings, the same way ending a household does. Not in the original spec.
  - Ended households are final; a returning household is a new record (open question A).
  - Step 1 has no bookings yet, so "cancel future bookings" goes through a `BookingLedger` hook that does nothing until step 2 plugs the real calendar in. Tests prove the hook is called and that a failure rolls everything back.
  - The in-memory store runs the screens; the Postgres store is built in step 2, where the double-booking rule needs it.
- **Next:** Grant reviews step 1, then step 2 (bookable items, rules, booking engine, day view, booking form, My bookings).
- **Blocked / needs Grant:** turn on GitHub branch protection; set `CCS_ALLOW_SAMPLE_DATA=1` in Vercel **Preview** only if previews should show `/club`; real qualification names (B). `supabase/config.toml` has not been tried with the Supabase CLI (no Docker in the cloud session).

### Oct 22, 2026 (later): step 1 merged, step 2 built
- **Done:** Grant merged his two pull requests (Next.js 16.4.2 security upgrade, roster note) and approved merging step 1 once CI passed; step 1 merged as PR 12. Step 2 then built on a fresh branch from `main`.
- **Built (step 2):** rules as data with a checker; the booking engine (all of the behaviors above); invented boats using the shell names from the dashboard sample plus kayaks, paddle boards and dock slips; day view, booking form, My bookings, staff equipment page; second migration with the overlap constraint, tested against a real Postgres including 20 simultaneous requests and a 100-request storm.
- **Found and fixed on the way:** (1) twenty colliding database inserts sometimes took seconds (deadlock detection) instead of failing cleanly; fixed with a per-item lock in the database. (2) A test run after merging showed the browser tests assumed a fresh server; they now look in the right list and pass three runs in a row.
- **Decided / assumed (Grant to confirm in review):** staff overrides cover the club rules but never facts; crew leaving the club lose their seat but the booking stays; a staff cancellation is never recorded as the member's late cancellation; unmarked past outings show "Awaiting attendance record"; a person can hold a dock space while on the water (only boats, kayaks and paddle boards put someone in one place).
- **Next:** Grant reviews step 2. Then the 10-step order continues with step 3 (fitness classes) only if Grant says so; the Postgres store (open question G) is the main gap before any pilot.
- **Blocked / needs Grant:** real values for the placeholder rules (open question 5); real qualification names (B); branch protection on `main`.

### Oct 22, 2026 (evening): the Postgres store
- **Asked by Grant:** "Postgres Store then Step 3".
- **Built:** `PgClubStore` and its wiring (`getClubStore` picks it when `CLUB_DATABASE_URL` is set); a third migration (who-did-it IDs, creation order, crew seat order, empty out-of-service periods allowed); sample loading into an empty database; a second Playwright project that runs the member-area specs on a Postgres-backed server.
- **Found by running both stores:** the in-memory store allowed returning a boat to service in the same instant it was taken out; the database insisted the period end strictly later. Settled in the database's favour with a note (an empty period means it never mattered).

### Oct 24, 2026: step 9, the QuickBooks files
- **Asked by Grant:** "Move forward with next step" (step 9). Answers to the plan: it is not just event tickets (bar drinks, snack-bar items and, over 4 guests, $10 each, must all be accounted for); everyone who held spots pays for event tickets; **QuickBooks Online invoices**; **lock the month, fix it next month**; the extra fee is for **visitors to the clubhouse, not events**; staff **enter bar and snack-bar charges on a screen**; **one invoice per household with a line per kind**; monthly dues are billed too, a **full month if active on the 1st**; visitors **sign in on the front-desk iPad**; the fee is **$10 for every visitor once over 4 in a day**; **QuickBooks adds the tax**. He approved replacing the old "one charge line" decision.
- **Research that shaped it (Intuit's own help pages, no account touched):** QuickBooks Online's invoice import takes at most 100 invoices and 1,000 rows per file, **does not accept negative amounts**, cannot carry sales tax, is **unavailable if automated sales tax is on**, and needs customer and product names to match exactly. So the invoice file is split into parts, credits are left out (and listed in the plain file), the names are rules data, and sales tax is open question AY.
- **Built:** billing types, rules file and strict parser; the migration; both stores (the Postgres loader reaches back to the first unbilled month); the engine (charges, visitors, the month plan and run, the two files, list views, host search for the iPad); six staff and iPad screens with server actions; the QuickBooks ID card on the household desk; downloads; sample charges and visitors; the iPad's "I'm bringing visitors" flow.
- **Found on the way:** (1) the migration's total-check mixed up which table fired it (a run versus a line), and a missing "who entered it" slipped through a CHECK because `NULL` counts as "not failed"; both found by the new database tests and fixed (`coalesce`). (2) The iPad name search treated punctuation-only input as "nothing typed", which the name matcher answers with everyone: the three-letter minimum now counts letters and digits only. (3) The Postgres store only loads the lines of the latest 24 months, so an old month's file could have been short without saying so; the screens and downloads now check (`linesComplete`) and refuse. (4) My first browser tests found real mistakes in my expectations (dates read "Oct 28, 2026", the day box stops a future date before the server is asked, a form that refuses leaves its last day behind), and one real race: a form clears itself after the page refreshes, so a test that typed the next entry too early had its words wiped. (5) The visitor wording read "2 visitors more would still be free"; now "2 more visitors".
- **Proof it can fail:** 261 deliberate breakages, 251 caught at the end (details in the Step 9 section); every first miss was either given a new test or shown to be something no real data can reach. Two that matter: the Postgres store could have saved a taxable line as untaxed and nothing noticed, and could have accepted a change to a closed month; both are tested now.
- **Decided / assumed (Grant to confirm):** bar and snack-bar charges add up to one line each per household per month (the detail stays on the Charges screen, BB); event tickets are billed in the month the event ends; a late cancel is billed even if the spots were refilled (BC); dues for a household that ended during the month are the full month (it was a member on the 1st); no charges or invoices are shown to members (BD); any staff member can close a month (BE); visitor names are not kept (BA); the iPad's 30-second return, five-name limit and three-letter minimum.
- **Checks at the end:** see the pull request.

(4) The first run of the browser breakage checks passed everything for the wrong reason: I had used a Playwright flag that does not exist (`--bail`), the run stopped at once, and a pipe hid the error. I found it by running the command by hand, fixed it (`--max-failures=1`), ran the unbroken command first to see it pass, and reran. (5) I once stopped a runner with `pkill -f` on a pattern that was in my own command, which killed my shell; leftovers are now stopped by process number (same lesson as step 9).

Fixed in the stylesheet (`overflow-wrap: anywhere` on the member area's three heading levels, `src/app/club/club.css`), and the test now always uses a long, wide surname (`wmwmwm` plus the tag), so it checks this every run; with that surname it fails 2 of 2 on the old stylesheet and passes 16 of 16 on the new one. (3) A Playwright text check does not accept the unit-test matchers, and `next build` type-checks the tests too, so the build caught it; I used a regular expression. Now it waits for the card to say "Followed up by Test staff...", and compares the count only with the "New" cards in the same page load. Run six times on both servers with four workers (96 of 96), and broken on purpose (the count including followed-up requests) it fails.

- **Found while building lineups:** a database check treated "unknown" as passing, so a member could set up a practice with no coach (fixed with `coalesce`); the Postgres store silently ignored a rename of a practice (it now refuses one); my invented sample practice landed on a slot an older booking test uses (moved to a Sunday no older test touches).
- **The proof (PR 43):** see the proof bullet in "Practices and lineups". 242 breakages; 212 of the first 238 were caught the first time; of the 26 that were not (25 misses and one that would not compile), 21 got a test (or, for the one that would not compile, a version that compiles) and 5 cannot change anything real; 4 more breakages written for the new tests, 2 caught and 2 harmless. 7 stay uncaught on purpose, each with its reason. The common pattern in the misses: my test did refuse the bad thing, but a different rule refused it first (a cancelled practice with no cancellation filled in, a coach who "left" who had never been one, a wipe guard hidden behind two others), so the rule I thought was tested never was.
- **Mistakes of mine to remember:** (1) one breakage (M22) used `false and a or b or c`, which by precedence switched off only the first condition; a breakage must be checked to do what it says (it still showed a real gap, in the change-time rule). (2) The old "race" test for two practices asking for one boat seldom overlapped, so removing the lock went unnoticed; a lock is tested by holding the first request open and checking the second waits. (3) Two browser breakages would not compile; the second try named a field that does not exist (`name` for `bookerName`); I read the type checker's own message before trying a third time. (4) I expected the new booking-lock test to catch removing the practice trigger's own lock; it cannot, because the pools trigger fires first and takes the same lock, and I read all the booking triggers before calling that harmless instead of guessing. (5) The stop hook asked me to commit about every minute while a breakage was in the tree; I committed only my real test files by name, checked the diff each time, and never committed the runner's temporary change.
- **Checks at the end (the final code, `f67c9e6`; the only later change is this file):** lint 0 errors (1 warning, the old unused `RULE_BOOK` in `testkit.ts`), type check clean, **2,559 unit and database tests pass** (1 skipped), against a real Postgres, in 116 files; the production build passes; **573 of 573 browser tests pass** (the in-memory server, the Postgres-backed server and the two live-site servers), in one full run.
